# Review - §832, R-US17's rule (c) preregistered (2026-10-16)

The workflow `rus17c-block-review` (run `wf_581db62c-6be`, 93 agents): seven lenses - facts and references, fidelity, computability, internal consistency, tunability and inference, the game and the bars, form and precedent - read the block in place in `docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md` under the panel's blindness rules. Every finding was put to refute-first skeptics (three for a high, one otherwise; uncertain counts as refuted), and a finding stands when most of its skeptics uphold it. Every report is verbatim below, with what was done. Before the review the session found one defect itself: the block's own text quoted the literal END marker, so the digest convention (the first END marker) cut the block at 686 lines; the line was reworded.

## The first pass - confirmed (28)

### facts-F1 (high; upheld 3 of 3)

**Quote.** In play the president is the one of the vote's day (PresidentialElection.PresidentAt). A president of no party has no PP, so no draw is made and (a) never fires

**Defect.** PresidentialElection.PresidentAt does not return the US president of record. If no game contest is decided it falls back to PresidencyOfRecord.TryAt, and PresidencyOfRecord.Of returns Poland's list only (an empty array for every other country). For the USA it therefore returns false on every date before the game seats a president. The US president of record lives in WorldClock.Governments (GovernmentOfRecord with ExecutiveKind.Presidency, read by WorldClock.TryPresidentOfRecord / TryGovernmentAt). The block also treats 'no president found' the same as 'a president of no party'. So in US-21's run from 12 Mar 2024 to 20 Jan 2025, an implementer following the text gets no PP, makes no draw and fires (a) on no bill: no veto ever, which mis-decides play.

**Evidence.** Assets/Scripts/Elections/PresidentialElection.cs:151-158 (`if (sitting == null) { return PresidencyOfRecord.TryAt(id, date, out president); }`); Assets/Scripts/Elections/PresidencyOfRecord.cs:49 (`Of(CountryId id) => id == CountryId.Poland ? Poland : Array.Empty<President>()`); Assets/Scripts/Elections/WorldClock.cs:177-186 and :406 (Biden as GovernmentOfRecord, ExecutiveKind.Presidency, party DEM).

**Fix proposed.** Replace with: "In play the president is the one of the vote's day: the game's seated president where PresidentialElection.PresidentAt finds a decided contest, otherwise the president of record from WorldClock (TryGovernmentAt with ExecutiveKind.Presidency; PP = its party, REP or DEM). No president found on a US vote day is a DEFECT, not 'a president of no party'." Alternatively, require US-21 to extend PresidencyOfRecord.Of to the USA, and say so in the block.

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: I checked the claim against the code and the plan, and it holds. 1. **The quote is accurate.** USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:781-783 reads: "In play the president is the one of the vote's day (PresidentialElection.PresidentAt). A president of no party has no PP, so no draw is made and (a) never fires". 2. **PresidentAt finds no US president today.** PresidentialElection.cs:151-157 falls back to `PresidencyOfRecord.TryAt(id, date, out president)` when no decided contest has taken office. PresidencyOfRecord.cs:49 is `Of(CountryId id) => id == CountryId.Poland ? Poland : Array.Empty<President>()`. TryAt (PresidencyOfRecord.cs:130-135) therefore returns false with `president = default`, so BackingParty is null, on every US date before a game contest decides. 3. **The US president of record lives in WorldClock.** WorldClock.cs:406-407 holds Biden (DEM) and Trump (REP) as `GovernmentOfRecord` with `ExecutiveKind.Presidency`. They are read by `TryGovernmentAt` (WorldClock.cs:429) and `TryPresidentOfRecord` (WorldClock.cs:177). 4. **The plan does not close the gap.**    - US-6 (plan line 233) says: "The presidents' dates stay in `WorldClock.Governments` — no second copy." That rules out filling `PresidencyOfRecord.Of` for the USA.    - US-7 (line 246) seats the game's winner as "a government of the presidency kind" through US-2's transition.    - US-8 (lines 250-258) stores the contest beside Poland's but never makes PresidentAt's fallback read WorldClock.    - So during US-21's run from 12 Mar 2024 to 20 Jan 2025 (plan line 444), PresidentAt returns false throughout Biden's term.    - US-21's own text (line 441) requires "the president of the day — the record's or the game's". 5. **Nothing in the block handles it.**    - Line 994 ("goes to the president of the day (US-21)") adds no lookup.    - The carve-out at lines 738-740 lists only the ChamberVerdicts projection, the plate, the gate's name and US-21's 2024 run. It does not cover who the president is, so the block decides this itself.    - The block never says what happens when no president is found. Read literally, the default struct has no party, which triggers the block's own "no PP, no draw, (a) never fires". The result is no veto under Biden, which is unfaithful to H1.    - US-21's diagnostic (lines 1346-1353) asserts nothing about the vote-day president, so the instrument would not catch it. **Verdict: the defect is real.** It is at least a false reference that two honest implementers would resolve differently: one extends PresidentAt, the other lets the US get no veto. Read literally, it mis-decides play. The proposed fix is sound: fall back to WorldClock's presidency of record with PP from its party, and make a missing president on a US vote day a DEFECT.
- UPHELD: I could not refute it; the defect is real. I checked the block's line, the code it points to and the plan around it. 1. The quote is exact. USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:781-783 reads: "In play the president is the one of the vote's day (PresidentialElection.PresidentAt). A president of no party has no PP, so no draw is made and (a) never fires". 2. PresidentAt does not find the US president of record. When no game contest is decided it falls back to the record's list (PresidentialElection.cs:158: `if (sitting == null) { return PresidencyOfRecord.TryAt(id, date, out president); }`). That list holds Poland only (PresidencyOfRecord.cs:49: `Of(CountryId id) => id == CountryId.Poland ? Poland : Array.Empty<President>()`). TryAt therefore returns false for the USA on every date (PresidencyOfRecord.cs:130-135). 3. The plan keeps it that way. The US presidents of record live in WorldClock.cs:401-407 (Biden and Trump as GovernmentOfRecord, ExecutiveKind.Presidency, cabinets "DEM" and "REP"). They are read by WorldClock.TryPresidentOfRecord and TryGovernmentAt (WorldClock.cs:177-186 and :429), which is what the record row uses (GameController.ParliamentRows.cs:462-463). 4. Nothing else in the block covers it. A search of lines 730-1505 finds no WorldClock or Governments source for play's president. The only other mention is "A bill both chambers send goes to the president of the day (US-21)" (plan:994). The block's no-draw list covers only "a president with no PP" (plan:860). Nothing covers no president being found at all. 5. The consequence is real. US-21's own text intends "the president of the day — the record's or the game's" (plan:441). Its Done-when needs a run from 12 Mar 2024 to 20 Jan 2025 with Biden in office (plan:443). Followed literally, the block's pointer finds no president there, so no PP, no draw and no veto. On severity: medium (a false reference) is a fair alternative to high. US-21's prose at plan:441 states the intent, and an implementer would likely notice PresidentAt returning false for the USA. That softens the impact but does not make the reference correct, so the defect stands. The proposed fix is consistent with plan:233: use the game's president where PresidentAt finds a decided contest, otherwise WorldClock's presidency of record, and treat no president on a US vote day as a DEFECT. The alternative fix, extending PresidencyOfRecord.Of to the USA, contradicts US-6's "no second copy" and should not be used.
- UPHELD: The defect is real, and nothing else in the block handles it. The block (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:781-783) takes the president in play from PresidentialElection.PresidentAt and gives no other source. That method (PresidentialElection.cs:151-158) returns the game's seated president only when a decided contest has TakesOffice <= date. Otherwise it falls back to PresidencyOfRecord.TryAt, which loops over PresidencyOfRecord.Of(id) (PresidencyOfRecord.cs:130-135). Of returns Poland's list and an empty array for every other country (PresidencyOfRecord.cs:49). So for the USA it returns false. The US president of record lives only in WorldClock.Governments: Biden DEM 2021-01-20 to 2025-01-20 and Trump REP from 2025-01-20 (WorldClock.cs:406-407), read through TryGovernmentAt and TryPresidentOfRecord (WorldClock.cs:177-186, 429). The plan rules out a second copy: US-6 (plan line 233) says "The presidents' dates stay in `WorldClock.Governments` — no second copy." No plan text says US-8 or US-21 extends PresidentAt for the USA. The only PresidentAt mention in the plan is the block's own line 782. US-21 does not need US-8 (plan line 443: Needs US-10, US-19, US-20). Even with US-8 built, the game's contest takes office on 20 Jan 2025, so across US-21's 12 Mar 2024 to 20 Jan 2025 run PresidentAt still falls through to the empty record and returns false. The block covers only "a president of no party" (lines 782-783, 860) and "a president whose party holds no House seat" (line 1000). It never covers "no president found". The existing precedent, PresidentialVeto.Decide (PresidentialVeto.cs:167), returns null, meaning no veto, when PresidentAt finds no one. An implementer who follows the block and that precedent therefore gets no PP, no draw and no veto for the whole run. Line 994 defers to US-21's "president of the day — the record's or the game's" (plan line 441), which states the intent but not the mechanism, so the cited reference is false for the USA. The code facts quoted in the claim are accurate. The proposed fix is sound: use the game's seated president where PresidentAt finds a decided contest, otherwise WorldClock.TryGovernmentAt with ExecutiveKind.Presidency, mapping the row's party (DEM/REP) to the PP (D/R); and make "no president on a US vote day" a DEFECT. Severity: high holds if the text is applied literally, because play would be mis-decided. It is at least medium as a false reference, since US-21's own wording points the other way and a zero-veto run would show in its printout.

**What was done.** Done: in play the president is the game's seated one where PresidentialElection.PresidentAt finds a decided contest, otherwise the president of record from WorldClock.TryGovernmentAt (ExecutiveKind Presidency); no president found on a US vote day is a DEFECT, a president found with no party has no PP.

### game-F1 (high; upheld 3 of 3)

**Quote.** - THE RUNTIME: UsVeto.cs, pure, lands with the instrument and stays unwired until US-21.

**Defect.** The first-run commit cannot pass the cheap bar it requires ("the cheap bar with UsVetoDrawCheck added"). UsVeto.cs lands under Assets/Scripts, and before US-21 only Assets/Editor code names it. UnwiredSubsystemCheck is in the cheap Suite. Its UNREACHABLE pass counts every Assets/Scripts file whose public types no non-harness game file names, against UnreachableCeiling = 2, and the measured count is already 2 (ElectoralCollege.cs and Rosatellum.cs). So UsVeto.cs makes it 3 and the ratchet fails. The project's rule forbids raising a ratchet to make a run pass. (The UNWIRED pass is spared only by accident: the method name 'Applies' also appears in SimulationManager as PresidentialVeto.Applies, and the check matches on names.)

**Evidence.** Assets/Editor/UnwiredSubsystemCheck.cs: 'private const int UnreachableCeiling = 2;', and a file is UNREACHABLE when 'NOT ONE of the public types it declares is named anywhere else in game code', with Assets/Editor excluded. COMPLETED.md s788/s792: '2 of 287 UNREACHABLE ... the same two', and 'UNREACHABLE 2 of 289'. Generated/UsVetoDefections.cs would be reached through UsVeto.cs, but UsVeto.cs itself would be reached by nothing, the same pattern as UsPresidentialReturns being reached only through the unreachable ElectoralCollege.cs.

**Fix proposed.** Have the block state how the bar stays green. Either (i) the first-run commit records UsVeto.cs as a named UNREACHABLE GAP, with the ceiling moved 2 -> 3 by Elias's ruling and returned to 2 at US-21 (asserted there); or (ii) the arithmetic lands instrument-side (Assets/Editor) and US-21 moves it into Assets/Scripts as a byte-identical move, asserted. Add 'UnwiredSubsystemCheck unchanged at 2 UNREACHABLE (or as ruled)' to THE FIRST-RUN COMMIT's bars.

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: The defect is real, and nothing in the block or the rest of the plan deals with it. (1) The quote is exact. USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1339 reads "- THE RUNTIME: UsVeto.cs, pure, lands with the instrument and stays unwired until US-21." Lines 1139-1140 put the file at Assets/Scripts/Elections/UsVeto.cs, called by Assets/Editor/UsVetoDrawCheck.cs. Line 1293 says "Nothing calls UsVeto in play before US-21." Lines 1492-1494 require the first-run commit to pass "the cheap bar with UsVetoDrawCheck added". (2) UnwiredSubsystemCheck is in the cheap suite: CheckSuite.cs:252. (3) How it counts. UnwiredSubsystemCheck.cs:74 sets "private const int UnreachableCeiling = 2;". Lines 283-309 mark a file under Assets/Scripts as UNREACHABLE when none of its public types is named in any other non-harness file. IsHarness (377-382) counts everything outside Assets/Scripts as harness, so Assets/Editor is excluded. Lines 343-353 fail the run when the count is above the ceiling, and the error text says "LOWER the ceiling, never raise it." Plan line 143 also allows only "lowered to what the bar measures". (4) The count today is 2 under a ceiling of 2. COMPLETED.md:26484 names the two files, ElectoralCollege.cs and Rosatellum.cs. COMPLETED.md:37481/37539/37646 (s786-s792) give "2 of 287 UNREACHABLE" and "UNREACHABLE 2 of 289". (5) No game file names UsVeto. A grep of Assets/Scripts finds no "UsVeto" anywhere. The catalog will not name it either: every Generated catalog, PolishVetoRates included (the pattern the block cites at 1338), uses named tuples and names no runtime type. UsVeto.cs names UsVetoDefections, so the catalog is reached, but nothing reaches UsVeto.cs. The plain implementation therefore gives 3 UNREACHABLE against a ceiling of 2, and the required cheap bar fails. (6) The project's own precedent shows the project avoids raising this count. At s786-s792 the new US catalogs were made partial parts of UsPresidentialReturns, reached through the already-counted ElectoralCollege.cs, so the count stayed at 2. (7) The block never mentions the ratchet. It contains no "UnwiredSubsystemCheck", "UNREACHABLE", "ceiling" or "ratchet" between lines 730 and 1501, and plan line 143 covers only lowering. Two honest implementers would split: one raises the ceiling without a ruling (forbidden), one hides the file through an accidental name match, one stops. The claim's side note checks out. "Applies" is named in SimulationManager.cs and other game files, so the UNWIRED pass is spared by a name match. The word "cannot" is slightly too strong, because an accidental match on a nested type's name could rescue the count. That does not refute the defect: the block requires a bar that its own plain implementation fails, and it gives no ruled way through.

With the tuple pattern the block itself cites, the required bar fails and the only remedy is forbidden, so high is defensible. Either fix the reviewer proposes would close it. The precedent favours (ii): keep the arithmetic in Assets/Editor and move it into Assets/Scripts byte-identical at US-21. In both cases, add "UnwiredSubsystemCheck at 2 UNREACHABLE" to THE FIRST-RUN COMMIT's bars.
- UPHELD: I tried to refute this and could not. The defect is real. 1. The block puts a new file under Assets/Scripts that nothing will reach. USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1339 says "THE RUNTIME: UsVeto.cs, pure, lands with the instrument and stays unwired until US-21", and :1293-1294 says "Nothing calls UsVeto in play before US-21." Nothing on the record would make UsVeto.cs reachable. The generated catalogs use no runtime types: PolishVetoRates.cs uses tuples, and UsPresidentialReturns.cs contains no mention of ElectoralCollege, which is why ElectoralCollege stays unreachable. The new catalog keeps that pattern, so it names no UsVeto type. UsVeto.cs would be reached by nothing and the count would become 3. 5. The block does not handle this anywhere. The reviewer's side remark about the UNWIRED pass also checks out. :1289 requires UsVeto.Applies, and PresidentialVeto.cs:45 already declares Applies. Because the check matches on names, UsVeto.cs would count as called, by accident. "Cannot pass" overstates it slightly. The bar could go green by accident (a public type name that happens to occur in another game file) or by a contrived reference from the catalog to a struct in UsVeto.cs. The second would be the kind of masking the check exists to prevent (see its §790 note on partial types). Either way, two honest implementers would resolve this differently. Unless the block says how the bar stays green, the first-run commit would need an amendment after the digest is frozen. The fix should be along the lines proposed: put the arithmetic in Assets/Editor until US-21, or write a ruled, time-boxed ceiling into the block, and add the UNREACHABLE count to the first-run bars.

**What was done.** Done (the fix's option ii, the project's own precedent for unconsumed code): UsVeto.cs and the catalog land in Assets/Editor until US-21's gate first calls them, then move into Assets/Scripts byte for byte, asserted by blob id; the first-run commit's bars require UnwiredSubsystemCheck's UNREACHABLE count unchanged.

### computability-C1 (medium; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** That one n is used everywhere: N_MIN, the union, the draw index, CP and the label.

**Defect.** For a pooled cell the block uses two different n's but says there is one. N_MIN tests the cell's own |L| ("A cell with n < 12 is POOLED"). The draw index, CP and the label use the union ("a pooled cell counts its union"; "UNFITTED n=<n> ... (pooled n=<n'>)"). That leaves the digest's "cell.<XX>.n=<n>" ambiguous between own and union. The n of a derived figure ("the number of observations in the cells the figure reads ... a pooled cell counts its union") can also double-count. If HP draws from HP u SP while SP keeps its own list, SP's observations count twice. If both cells draw from one union, it is unclear whether the union counts once or twice.

**Evidence.** THE FIT, THIN CELLS: "A cell with n < 12 is POOLED ... It draws from the union"; "The partner keeps its own list unless it too is below 12". THE PRINT: "n: the number of observations in the cells the figure reads (full cells; a pooled cell counts its union)". Decisive digest: "cell.<XX>.n=<n>".

**Fix proposed.** Define n_own = |own observations|, which alone decides pooling, and n_drawn = |drawn list|, which is used for the draw index, CP and the label. Print and digest both ("cell.<XX>.n_own=", "cell.<XX>.n_drawn="). Define a derived figure's n as the number of DISTINCT observations (roll-call identity plus role) in the union of the drawn lists the figure reads.

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: I could not refute this. The defect is real, but its weight is in the derived-figure n, not the digest. Every quote is accurate: - The "one n" sentence is at USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:916-918. - The pooling rule is at :929-933: "A cell with n < 12 is POOLED ... It draws from the union", and "The partner keeps its own list unless it too is below 12". - The print rule is at :1055-1056: "a pooled cell counts its union". - The UNFITTED label is at :1089. - The digest line "cell.<XX>.n=<n>" is at :1435. 1. **The sentence contradicts the block.** :916 defines L as "its observations", the cell's own list. Yet :861 indexes "the cell's drawn list L", which for a pooled cell is the union (:930, and the catalog's "already merged for a pooled cell" at :1332). :1089 then prints two n's itself: "UNFITTED n=<n> ... (pooled n=<n'>)". Read as one value, the claim that one n serves N_MIN, the draw index, CP and the label is false for a pooled cell. N_MIN must use the cell's own count, because pooling has not happened yet when it is tested. The draw index, CP and the label use the union. The draw itself is safe in practice, because the runtime indexes the catalog array (:1335). Whether q_any/q10 of a pooled cell are taken over its own list or the union is settled only by reading :1055 together with the "one n" sentence. 2. **The digest line is ambiguous, but only in its bytes.** :1435 does not say whether n is the cell's own count or its drawn count. :1436 does say the obs lines are the "drawn list". Either choice carries the same information, because a cell's own count can be recovered from the H|/S| prefix of the identities in the drawn list. The decisive digest is only compared with itself across runs (:1134, :1459-1465). So this part changes the digest's bytes between two implementations but never a decision. 3. **The derived-figure n is a substantive ambiguity, and the block resolves it nowhere.** P(sent_j) and every total read all four cells. Take HP pooled into HP∪SP while SP keeps its own list (:931). Read literally, "a pooled cell counts its union" (:1055) gives HP a count of |HP∪SP| and SP a count of |SP|. Summed, SP's observations count twice. If both cells pool, the union U is counted either once or twice. Nothing in the block says the observations are counted distinct: :1083 only appends "(XX pooled with YY)". Two honest implementers would print different n's. The summing reading inflates the n that H2 requires beside every fitted figure ("the fit is thin ... prints its n"), which overstates the evidence. The impact is limited to the print and the digest bytes. n never enters THE DECISION (:1219-1237), which reads exact rationals. That puts it at medium, the "two honest implementers" case. The proposed fix resolves all three points: n_own decides pooling; n_drawn is used for the draw index, CP and the label; both are printed and digested; and a derived figure's n counts DISTINCT (identity, role) observations.

**What was done.** Done with consistency-IC-2 and IC-3: L_own and n_own decide pooling; the drawn list L and n serve the draw, CP, q_any, q10, the mean share and the label; a derived figure's n counts distinct observations; the digest carries n_own and n.

### computability-C5 (medium; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** R9: the role taken from the vote-day president in place of the decision president.

**Defect.** R9 cannot be computed from what the block allows. THE RECORD says "The rule reads nothing else of the US record", and the CSV carries only the decision president (president, president_party). No source is named for the president on a roll call's date. Roll calls dated 2017-01-03 to 2017-01-19, which the block explicitly admits, fall under a D president, so every role flips for them. R9 also does not say what the role change touches: the fit's observations only, or V, premise (B)'s PP stance and rule (a) as well. R9's outcome goes into the generated verdict line, so this text must be settled before the digest is taken.

**Evidence.** THE TERMS: "The rule reads nothing else of the US record and never the member XML"; "Asserted: every roll call of a counted measure is dated 2017-01-03 or later". veto_record.md reading 2: terms change at noon on 20 January. CSV header: only president and president_party.

**Fix proposed.** Name the source, for example: "the president on the roll call's date by the 20th Amendment's noon of 20 January 2017/2021/2025, read from records_by_date.md (or PresidencyOfRecord); a roll call before 2017-01-20 has PP D". State the scope, for example: "R9 changes only which cell, P or O, an observation enters; V, (B) and (a) keep the decision president".

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: I tried to refute this and could not. The defect is real. 1. Nothing gives a source for the vote-day president on the record.    - USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:745-749 limits the rule to the CSV: "The rule reads nothing else of the US record and never the member XML". The CSV header (brief facts.md:116) carries only `president` and `president_party`, which is the decision president.    - The block's only vote-day source is at :781-782: "In play the president is the one of the vote's day (PresidentialElection.PresidentAt)". That is play only, and it cannot serve the record. PresidentialElection.cs:151-158 falls back to PresidencyOfRecord.TryAt. PresidencyOfRecord.cs `Of(id) => id == CountryId.Poland ? Poland : Array.Empty` holds no US president, so TryAt returns false for the USA on every date.    - WorldClock.cs:401-407 is not read by PresidentAt, and its US executives begin at Biden on 2021-01-20 (veto_record.md:58, "its §2 table begins in 2021").    - No source in the project holds Obama, or Trump's first term, by date. Grepping the plan, COMPLETED.md, us_veto_backtest.pl, US_VETO_BACKTEST.md and veto_record.md finds no other definition of R9. 2. The block admits the case where R9 matters. :777-778 asserts only "every roll call of a counted measure is dated 2017-01-03 or later". Roll calls on 3-19 January 2017, 2021 or 2025 can therefore sit under a president other than the decision president. veto_record.md:58-61 says the window is by decision date: "terms change at noon on 20 January"; "A decision dated 20 January is the incoming president's". 3. Two honest implementers would compute R9 differently.    - One takes PresidentAt, the source the block names for the vote-day president. It gets no president, so under :782-783 there is no PP and no draw, and R9 collapses.    - The other hard-codes the 20th Amendment boundaries, which also needs a party for Obama that no project source holds.    - Scope is unstated as well. The readings "each run the whole rule with one change" (:1355), and role feeds four places: the cells (FITTED ROLL CALLS, "the PP of its OWNER", :905-906), V (:825-826), premise (B)'s PP stance (:884) and rule (a) (:995-999). Taken literally, R9 would make (a) read the wrong party's House nays for a measure the next president decided. 4. It matters before the digest. R9's outcome is named in the generated verdict line where it differs (:1245-1246, :1256-1258), and the block is frozen by its digest. The quoted evidence is accurate. Medium severity fits: an ambiguity two implementers would resolve differently, possibly uncomputable as written. Fix: name the source, for example the 20th Amendment's noon of 20 January 2017, 2021 and 2025 as constants, citing veto_record.md reading 2, with Obama's party declared as D. Then state the scope, for example: "R9 changes only which cell, P or O, an observation enters. V, (B) and (a) keep the decision president."

**What was done.** Done: THE PRESIDENT ON A DATE is defined in THE TERMS (the 20th Amendment's noon of 20 January); R9 changes only which cell an observation enters - V, (B) and (a) keep the decision president.

### computability-C6 (medium; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** P(NONE_EXP) = the same over V's MAJORITY-procedure measures.

**Defect.** The block defines P(NONE_EXP) in two incompatible ways. In TOTALS it runs over the MAJORITY-procedure measures, which include reconciliation (SENATE PROCEDURE: "MAJORITY (reconciliation, US-18's 2 U.S.C. 641(e) path)"). THE DECISION calls the same figure "expedited-only". A reconciliation measure in V scores deterministically at K = 0, so its P(sent) of 0 or 1 can move this product to 0. Two implementers would print different figures.

**Evidence.** TOTALS: "P(NONE_EXP) = the same over V's MAJORITY-procedure measures." THE DECISION, PRINTED ONLY: "the pooled and expedited-only P(NONE)".

**Fix proposed.** Pick one and use it in both places. Either "P(NONE_EXP) = the product over V's EXPEDITED-kind measures" or rename the figure P(NONE_MAJ) over MAJORITY procedure.

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: The contradiction is real, but it matters less than the reviewer says. The reviewer quoted the text correctly. Evidence (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md): - Line 1162 defines "P(NONE_EXP) = the same over V's MAJORITY-procedure measures." - The block defines MAJORITY procedure as two sub-procedures. Lines 822-824 give "MAJORITY (expedited) if its kind is EXPEDITED; else MAJORITY (reconciliation, US-18's 2 U.S.C. 641(e) path)". Line 956 repeats this: "SENATE, MAJORITY procedure (expedited or reconciliation)". - Line 1243 calls the same figure "the pooled and expedited-only P(NONE)". By the block's own labels, "expedited-only" means only the MAJORITY (expedited) subset. Line 1162 adds the reconciliation path to that. - Nothing else in the block settles it. A grep finds NONE_EXP and "expedited-only" only at lines 1162 and 1243, and the brief does not mention the figure. - A reconciliation measure can be in V. V's definition (lines 825-826) does not exclude the RECORD BUDGET CLASS, line 974 scores such a measure "at K = 0 in each chamber, with no draw", and the record holds every public law (veto_record.md:4). - So two implementers reading lines 1162 and 1243 would build this figure over different sets. Why the severity is overstated: - The figure cannot change the decision. It is "PRINTED ONLY, never part of the outcome" (lines 1240-1243), and only p_none_xxii enters the decisive digest (line 1445). - On the pinned record the value is probably the same either way. The plan states that the other party never held both chambers in the 115th-119th Congresses (line 723). Under (B) at K = 0, with the president's party voting NAY, nothing in V can then pass both chambers. A reconciliation measure in V therefore has P(sent) = 0 and multiplies the product by 1. The reviewer's "Two implementers would print different figures" holds in general, or after a re-pin, but probably not on this extract. - The m in the figure's label is a separate gap. Line 1058 gives m only for the pooled P(NONE) (|V|) and for P(NONE_XXII) (|V_XXII|). P(NONE_EXP) has no m, so its label's m would differ between the two readings whenever V holds a reconciliation measure. Fix: use one definition in both places. Either use the expedited kinds at line 1162 ("over V's measures whose SENATE PROCEDURE is MAJORITY (expedited)") and keep line 1243, or rename the figure P(NONE_MAJ) and say "MAJORITY-procedure" at line 1243. In either case, add the matching m (that subset's size) at line 1058. I would rate it low to medium rather than medium.

**What was done.** Done: P(NONE_EXP) is over V_EXP (EXPEDITED kinds) in both places.

### consistency-IC-1 (medium; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** UNCONTESTED (both blocs on one side, or the PP not AGAINST): no draw, and the test at

**Defect.** THE MODEL item 2 says the PP standing AGAINST always draws on the veto path ("The PP standing AGAINST (NAY) draws from HP or SP"; the only exclusions listed are the OP standing against, budget, S = 0 and the rest). The UNCONTESTED bullet says that when both blocs stand on one side there is no draw, "off V, under (A) and in play". So when both parties stand NAY in play or under R1/(A), item 2 says the PP draws and UNCONTESTED says it does not. Separately, "On V under (B) the PP always draws, both-NAY included" contradicts item 2's "no draw ... a RECORD BUDGET CLASS measure" and "a party with S = 0", and the very next bullet also contradicts it.

**Evidence.** MODEL item 2 vs WHEN THE DRAW MATTERS (UNCONTESTED; "On V under (B) the PP always draws, both-NAY included"; "RECORD BUDGET CLASS on V: the test at K = 0 in each chamber, with no draw"). When both stand NAY the outcome cannot change (Kp <= floor((S-1)/2) never reaches a majority). But whether a draw is made decides the draw counter, the play key that US-21's diagnostic counts ("one play key per chamber per bill"), and P(the draw matters) / MARGIN (cap is "0 otherwise" for a party that does not draw).

**Fix proposed.** Pick one rule and state it in both places. For example, change item 2 to "The PP standing AGAINST draws from HP or SP, except where the OP also stands AGAINST outside (B) on V (UNCONTESTED)", and change the (B) sentence to "On V under (B) the PP draws whenever it draws under item 2 (both-NAY included; never on a RECORD BUDGET CLASS measure or with S = 0)".

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: The contradiction is real, but it changes nothing the rule computes. I'd treat it as low severity, not medium. 1. **The core conflict holds.** In USA_STAGE_PLAN.md, THE MODEL item 2 (lines 851-860) says "The PP standing AGAINST (NAY) draws from HP or SP". Its exclusion list removes only "an OP standing AGAINST" and never the PP's own draw. Item 1 (line 850) says "Nothing is drawn or recorded outside item 2". UNCONTESTED (lines 971-973) says "(both blocs on one side ...): no draw ... This applies off V, under (A) and in play. On V under (B) the PP always draws, both-NAY included." That contrast shows "no draw" really means no draw is made, not just that none enters the division. So when both parties stand AGAINST in play or under R1/(A) (R1, line 1358), item 2 has the PP draw and UNCONTESTED says it does not. No sentence in the block says which wins. Item 6 (lines 876-878) only says crossers enter where they change the outcome, so it doesn't settle whether a draw is made. 2. **The claimed consequences are mostly wrong.** K is capped at floor((S-1)/2) (line 864). With both parties NAY, Y = Kp < S_PP - Kp <= N. So no House majority, Senate majority or three-fifths cloture is reachable, and the outcome is the same whichever reading is used:    - P(pass) = 0/1 either way.    - P(the draw matters) = 0 either way.    - MARGIN prints "unreachable" either way. With the OP not drawing, D only runs over [0, cap_PP], and no D in that range reverses the outcome, so cap_PP's value doesn't matter.    - The decisive digest is unchanged, since it reads only (B), where the block is explicit.    - Every draw-counter assertion (lines 1282, 1290, 1349) covers budget, non-US or projection paths only, never an ordinary US bill.    - "One play key per chamber per bill" (line 1350) is a uniqueness check that either reading passes. 3. **The second point is weak.** The next bullet already removes the budget class explicitly ("RECORD BUDGET CLASS on V: ... with no draw", line 974), and item 3 sets K = 0 when S = 0. On (B), "always" is loose wording, not a rule that would decide anything wrong. So two honest implementers could still differ on whether UsVeto makes the draw. The block states opposite things in its WHERE-A-DRAW-IS-MADE item and its margin section, so it is a real defect, and fixing it before the digest is taken costs nothing. The reviewer's proposed fix works: carve both-AGAINST outside (B) out of item 2, and replace "always" with "whenever item 2 draws".

**What was done.** Done: item 2's no-draw list gains "under (A) or in play, a PP standing AGAINST where the OP also stands AGAINST (UNCONTESTED)"; UNCONTESTED says the PP draws on V under (B) wherever item 2 draws, never on a RECORD BUDGET CLASS measure or with S = 0.

### consistency-IC-2 (medium; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** A CELL'S LIST L: its observations. n of a cell = |L|

**Defect.** L and n are each defined twice with different meanings. THE FIT makes L the cell's own observations and n its own count, which the pooling test uses ("A cell with n < 12 is POOLED"). THE MODEL item 3 makes L "the cell's drawn list" with n = |L|, which for a pooled cell is the union. THE FIT then claims "That one n is used everywhere: N_MIN, the union, the draw index, CP and the label", which cannot hold for a pooled cell. Downstream this leaves open whether the decisive digest's cell.<XX>.n is the own or the union count, even though its obs.<i> lines run over the "drawn list" (so a pooled cell's line count would not equal n). It also leaves open whether q_any/q10 and item 2's printed observations use the own list or the union.

**Evidence.** THE FIT 'A CELL'S LIST L' and 'THIN CELLS'; THE MODEL item 3 'an index j into the cell's drawn list L (THE FIT), n = |L|'; THE PRINT 'a pooled cell counts its union'; TOO THIN 'UNFITTED n=<n> ... (pooled n=<n'>)'; digest 'cell.<XX>.n=<n>' and 'cell.<XX>.obs.<i>=... (i 0-based, drawn list, fit order)'.

**Fix proposed.** Define two names. OWN LIST L_own with n_own (used for N_MIN and pooling), and DRAWN LIST L with n = |L| (the union for a pooled cell; used for the draw index, CP, q_any/q10, the mean share and the label). Delete "That one n is used everywhere". In the digest write cell.<XX>.n_own=<n_own> and cell.<XX>.n=<|drawn list|>, with obs lines over the drawn list.

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: The defect is real. The quote is accurate (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:916-918): "A CELL'S LIST L: its observations. n of a cell = |L| ... That one n is used everywhere: N_MIN, the union, the draw index, CP and the label." For a pooled cell the block uses n for two different numbers: (1) The cell's own count. The pooling test needs it: "A cell with n < 12 is POOLED" (930). Reading it as the union would be circular. TOO THIN uses n the same way, "UNFITTED n=<n> ... (pooled n=<n'>)" (1089), with n for the own count and n' for the union. (2) The size of the drawn list. THE MODEL item 3 says "an index j into the cell's drawn list L (THE FIT), n = |L|" (861). THIN CELLS says "It draws from the union" (930). THE PRINT says "a pooled cell counts its union" (1055-1056). So "that one n" cannot be a single number for a pooled cell. Item 3's pointer "(THE FIT)" also resolves literally to "L: its observations", the cell's own list. Some uses are settled elsewhere, so they are not the live problem: - The draw list is settled by the catalog: "already merged for a pooled cell" (1331-1332). - The label and CP n are settled by THE PRINT (1055-1056). Nothing settles the decisive digest. It writes "cell.<XX>.n=<n>" (1435), then "cell.<XX>.obs.<i>=... (i 0-based, drawn list, fit order)" (1436-1437). One honest implementer writes n as THE FIT's "n of a cell" (the own count). Another writes the number of obs lines (the union). The same open choice applies to an UNFITTED cell, which has no drawn list to list. It also applies to PRINTED item 2's "n; every observation" (1170): own list or union? A grep of the plan finds no other text that defines "own n", "own list" or the digest's n. That ambiguity sits inside a frozen, decisive artifact, so medium is the right severity. The proposed fix works: name n_own (used for N_MIN and pooling) and n = |drawn list| (used for the draw, CP and the label), delete "That one n is used everywhere", and give the digest both values. One addition: say what the digest writes for an UNFITTED cell (n_own, n' and no obs lines).

**What was done.** Done: two names, L_own/n_own (pooling) and L/n (the drawn list); "That one n is used everywhere" deleted; the digest writes cell.<XX>.n_own and cell.<XX>.n.

### consistency-IC-3 (medium; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** - n: the number of observations in the cells the figure reads (full cells; a pooled cell

**Defect.** A derived figure such as P(sent_j), E_XXII or precision reads all four cells, but the label has a single n=<n>. The rule does not say whether n is the sum of the four cells' counts. If HP is pooled into the union with SP while SP keeps its own list, summing "the cells the figure reads" counts SP's observations twice. Two implementers will print different n values on every derived figure, the verdict line included.

**Evidence.** THE PRINT 'n:' bullet vs THE LABEL '"<value> FITTED n=<n> m=<m> ..." for every derived figure' and THIN CELLS 'The partner keeps its own list unless it too is below 12'.

**Fix proposed.** State it exactly. For example: "n of a derived figure = the number of DISTINCT observations (by roll-call identity and role) in the union of the drawn lists the figure reads". Or print one n per cell: "n=HP:<a>,HO:<b>,SP:<c>,SO:<d>".

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: The defect is real, though the reviewer overstates its reach. 1. The quote is accurate. USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1055-1056 reads "n: the number of observations in the cells the figure reads (full cells; a pooled cell counts its union)". The label at :1082 carries a single "n=<n>" for every derived figure, and :1083 appends " (<XX> pooled with <YY>)" for each pooled cell the figure reads. 2. Derived figures do read several cells. P_H(j) reads HP plus HO when the OP stands YEA (:855). P(sent_j) = P_H * P_S (:1122) reads up to all four cells, and the totals, precision, E_XXII and the verdict line's "FITTED n=.. m=.." (:1251) do too. 3. The one-sided pooling case exists. THIN CELLS (:929-932) says "A cell with n < 12 is POOLED with ... the other chamber ... It draws from the union" and "The partner keeps its own list unless it too is below 12". So with HP pooled and SP fitted, SP's observations sit in two drawn lists: HP's union and SP's own. The catalog stores each cell's list "already merged for a pooled cell" (:1332), and the digest lists cell.<XX>.obs per cell (:1436).    - An implementer who follows "a pooled cell counts its union" and sums each cell's count over the cells read gets n_HP + 2*n_SP + n_HO + n_SO.    - An implementer who counts distinct observations gets n_HP + n_SP + n_HO + n_SO.    - When both cells are below 12 and both draw from the union, the sum doubles the whole union. 4. Nothing else in the block settles it. :916-918 ("n of a cell = |L| ... used everywhere: N_MIN, the union, the draw index, CP and the label") defines only a cell's own n, not a figure that reads several cells. A search of the block for "distinct", "n=<n" and "counts its union" turns up no other definition. The brief has none either. 5. The overstatement: without pooling, the sum and the distinct count are the same number. So the two readings print different n only when a cell is pooled, not "on every derived figure" as claimed. Pooling is a live possibility on a fit this thin (13 vetoable measures, H2), and when it happens the label H2 requires (n printed beside every fitted figure) comes out differently under two honest implementations, the verdict line included. That is medium as the task defines it. The decisive digest is not affected: it holds only cell.<XX>.n (:1435), not figure n. The fix should say that n of a figure is the number of distinct observations, keyed by roll-call identity and cell role, across the union of the lists the figure reads. Printing one n per cell would also resolve it.

**What was done.** Done: a derived figure's n is the number of DISTINCT observations (by roll-call identity and role) in the union of the full drawn lists it reads.

### consistency-IC-4 (medium; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** p_sent.<congress>|<measure>=<p/q>

**Defect.** The decisive digest always carries per-measure p_sent/p_named rationals and the five totals. Under S3, though, THE DECISION and THE PRINT say every figure that reads an UNFITTED cell "prints UNFITTED, with no value" ("When S3 fires, S1 and S2 are not evaluated, and their figures print UNFITTED."). So the digest has no defined content for those lines on an S3 run. It is also unclear what an UNFITTED cell's obs.<i> lines hold (its own list? the too-small union? nothing?). The S3 run has to be registered ASKED "with the outcome and the digest", so its digest must be computable.

**Evidence.** THE RULING RECORD's digest line list (p_sent, p_named, e_sent ... p_none_xxii, obs lines over the 'drawn list') vs THE PRINT 'TOO THIN' ('every figure that reads it prints "UNFITTED", with no value') and THE DECISION S3.

**Fix proposed.** Add: "Where outcome=S3, every p_sent.*, p_named.* and e_*/p_none_xxii line is written with the value UNFITTED, and an UNFITTED cell's obs lines list its own observations (fit order)". Or say the instrument still computes them from the too-small union, for the digest only.

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: The defect is real. I checked whether the block settles it anywhere else, and it does not. The digest's lines need rationals on every run. USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1443-1446 lists "p_sent.<congress>|<measure>=<p/q>" for every measure of V, then p_named, then "e_sent=<p/q> e_sent_xxii=<p/q> e_named=<p/q> e_hits=<p/q> p_none_xxii=<p/q>". Line 1448 allows only "p/q" in lowest terms. There is no UNFITTED token for these lines. One exists only for the cells: "cell.<XX>.status=FITTED|UNFITTED" at :1433. The block also says those same figures have no value under S3: - :1091 "every figure that reads it prints "UNFITTED", with no value" - :1225 "A decision cannot be computed on an unfitted cell (H4) ... When S3 fires, S1 and S2 are not evaluated, and their figures print UNFITTED" - H4 in facts.md:63 says "an unfitted figure prints UNFITTED and never counts in a decision" Under (B), the PP draws in both chambers on every measure of V (:973, :855). So an UNFITTED HP or SP is read by every p_sent and p_named and by all five totals. The conflict is concrete, not an edge case. The block still needs an S3 digest: - :1456 registers S3 as "ASKED (sNNN), with the outcome and the digest". - :1459-1460 passes each state only while the digest is equal. - :1487 requires planted UNFITTED and S3 runs in the mutation suite. So the instrument must produce an S3 digest, and the block never says what those lines hold. One honest implementer computes them from the union, since :930 says a thin cell "draws from the union". Another writes UNFITTED, following "with no value". Those give different digests, which makes this a medium ambiguity. It is worse when the union is empty. The empty-list rule at :868 covers an empty list "only in a LOO list, a bootstrap replicate or an INFLUENCE removal". It does not cover an empty full-fit union. There j = floor(u*0) clamped to n - 1 = -1 is undefined, so no p/q can be computed at all. The project has met this question before. R-US18's record at :1745 had to state it outright: "the decisive digest carries an unfitted (b')'s figures and whether it is fitted". R-US17's block has no such sentence. The quoted text is accurate (:1443). The proposed fix works: either write UNFITTED as the value on S3, or say the digest's figures are computed from the union, including the empty-list case.

**What was done.** Done with form-F2: every digest key is written in every run (UNFITTED values; the empty sum and product over an empty V_XXII; an UNFITTED cell's lines over its union).

### consistency-IC-5 (medium; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** The ONE exception is THE US-10 IDENTITIES (THE FIRST RUN, item

**Defect.** COUNTED ONLY says every count, sum, denominator, cell, rate and DIGEST reads counted measures only, with ONE exception. Other sections of the block read all rows: the decisive digest carries unpredictable.<i> lines (and TRUMP'S section says they "enter the decisive digest by name only"); THE FIRST RUN item 1 prints "measures and vetoes, counted and in all"; the UNPREDICTABLE assertion has to scan all rows; and THE OVERRIDE / TRUMP'S section prints the two sustained override votes "on their own line". An implementer who holds to COUNTED ONLY would drop the digest's unpredictable lines and the 'in all' counts.

**Evidence.** THE TERMS 'COUNTED ONLY'; THE RULING RECORD digest 'unpredictable.<i>=<congress>|<measure>'; THE FIRST RUN item 1; TRUMP'S 2025 VETOES 'their sustained votes print on their own line only' and 'enter the decisive digest by name only'.

**Fix proposed.** Reword the clause to list every exception: "Exceptions: THE US-10 IDENTITIES; the UNPREDICTABLE set's own derivation, its line and its digest entries (by name); item 1's 'in all' record counts; the two sustained override votes' line. None of these enters any figure, denominator or decision."

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: The defect is real. I could not refute it. The quote is accurate: USA_STAGE_PLAN.md lines 769-772 say "every count, sum, denominator, cell, rate and digest of this rule reads counted measures only. The ONE exception is THE US-10 IDENTITIES (THE FIRST RUN, item 10)". Nothing ever qualifies that sentence, yet at least five other provisions of the same block read rows that are not counted: 1. **The decisive digest's own lines.** Line 1438 adds `unpredictable.<i>=<congress>|<measure>  (CSV order)` to the decisive digest. Those are exactly the two rows that are not counted. Line 1320 (TRUMP'S section) says "they enter the decisive digest by name only". 2. **The source line.** Line 1432 adds `source=<SourceDigest>` to the decisive digest. Line 748 defines SourceDigest as "SHA-256 over its raw bytes", which covers every row, the two uncounted ones included. The reviewer missed this one. It is a second digest exception. 3. **Item 1's counts.** Line 1167 prints "measures and vetoes, counted and in all". The "in all" figures are counts over every row. 4. **Trump's per-president row.** Line 1316 prints "0 counted vetoes; 2 declared unpredictable", which counts rows that are not counted. 5. **The override line.** Line 1315 prints the two uncounted measures' sustained votes "on their own line only". The UNPREDICTABLE assertion at lines 763-764 also has to scan every row. That one is arguably part of defining the counted set rather than an exception, but the clause says nothing about it. **Is it handled elsewhere?** Only in part. TRUMP'S section adds the digest exception for itself, at line 1319: "read by THE US-10 IDENTITIES only ... and they enter the decisive digest by name only". COUNTED ONLY still says there is ONE exception, so the block contradicts itself. **Severity.** The specific provisions are explicit: the digest's line list is exhaustive ("Nothing else enters"), and item 1 says "in all" outright. So most implementers would follow them, and the practical risk is nearer low-to-medium than a sure misreading. Still, a frozen preregistered rule whose general clause is false invites a later dispute that costs an amendment, for example a first-run reviewer flagging item 1's "in all" or the unpredictable digest lines as breaking COUNTED ONLY. **Fix.** The reviewer's proposed list is right but should add SourceDigest. Suggested wording: "Exceptions: THE US-10 IDENTITIES; SourceDigest (raw bytes); the UNPREDICTABLE set's derivation, its line, its per-president count and its digest entries (by name); item 1's 'in all' record counts; the two sustained override votes' line. None enters any figure, denominator or decision."

**What was done.** Done: COUNTED ONLY lists every exception - the US-10 identities, the UNPREDICTABLE set's derivation, line, override line and digest entries, and item 1's counts "in all".

### consistency-IC-6 (medium; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** instrument's exact P(sent) and P(named) as the same rationals (on the full cells).

**Defect.** US-21's diagnostic is told to reproduce the instrument's full-cell P(sent) and P(named) for every counted veto in V. The instrument never produces per-measure full-cell figures. Per measure it computes only the LOO figures ("Per measure j of V, under (B), exact and LOO"). Full cells appear only in R5, which prints "its outcome and headline figures", not per-measure rationals, and in the catalog, which holds lists rather than probabilities. The figures this assertion compares against do not exist.

**Evidence.** HOW THE FIGURES REACH THE GAME, last bullet; THE FIRST RUN 'Per measure j of V, under (B), exact and LOO'; item 12 'DECLARED READINGS, each with its outcome and headline figures'; LEAVE ONE OUT 'In-sample is R5'.

**Fix proposed.** Either have the instrument print (and the catalog carry) each counted veto's full-cell P(sent)/P(named) as rationals ("R5's per-measure figures for the counted vetoes of V"), or have US-21 compare against the instrument's LOO rationals by running the runtime enumeration on the LOO lists.

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: The defect is real, and the quoted text is accurate. USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1351-1352 says US-21's diagnostic must reproduce "the instrument's exact P(sent) and P(named) as the same rationals (on the full cells)". The instrument never produces or keeps per-measure full-cell figures: (1) Line 1151 defines its per-measure figures only as "Per measure j of V, under (B), exact and LOO". (2) Line 1220 says the outcome "reads only the default's exact LOO figures". The decisive digest's p_sent/p_named lines (1443-1444) carry those LOO figures. (3) Full cells are used only by play and the catalog, and by R5 (line 937, "Play and the catalog use the full cells. In-sample is R5"). R5 prints only "outcome and headline figures" (lines 1212-1213: E_XXII, P(NONE_XXII), E[vetoable sent], E[hits], precision, hit rate; also lines 1355-1356), with no per-measure rationals. (4) The catalog (lines 1329-1336) holds digests, the outcome, M and the cells' lists, with no probabilities. "LOO stays instrument-side." (5) The bootstrap per-measure figures (line 1070) and the realizations (line 1125) are also LOO. So the comparison target exists in no printed or stored form. The instrument's only per-measure exact figures are LOO, and the parenthetical "(on the full cells)" contradicts that. R5's whole-rule run does compute in-sample per-measure values in memory. An implementer could expose them, since PresidentialVetoDiagnostic is an Editor class (Assets/Editor/PresidentialVetoDiagnostic.cs:57) and could call into the check. But the block never names R5 or any such output. Two honest implementers would pick different targets: R5's unexposed internals, the printed LOO figures (but the runtime has no LOO lists), or a circular recomputation with UsVeto.PassProbability. That is a false or ambiguous reference: real, at medium severity. The proposed fix works. Either have the instrument print, and the catalog or document carry, R5's per-measure full-cell P(sent)/P(named) for the counted vetoes of V, and point line 1352 at them by name. Or retarget the assertion at the LOO rationals of line 1151.

**What was done.** Done (the fix's first option): beside the LOO figures, the instrument prints each measure of V's full-cell P(sent) and P(named) as rationals, for US-21's diagnostic only, never deciding.

### consistency-IC-7 (medium; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** SETTLED, NOT READINGS: present stands against (US-10, F3)

**Defect.** "Present stands against" is listed as settled and NOT a reading, yet R10 is a declared reading of exactly that choice ("present outside the stance and the observation"). One implementer will run R10 and name it in the verdict line when its outcome differs; another, following SETTLED, will drop it. R10 also changes the stance and so possibly V, which the SETTLED list says is not open.

**Evidence.** DECLARED READINGS 'R10: present outside the stance and the observation (stance YEA iff yea > nay; d over yea + nay)' vs 'SETTLED, NOT READINGS: present stands against (US-10, F3)'.

**Fix proposed.** Either delete R10, or change the SETTLED item to "present stands against in the default (US-10, F3); R10 prints the alternative and never stops the rule". If R10 is kept, say whether V and F3's comparison are recomputed under it.

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: The defect is real. The two quoted passages are accurate and they contradict each other. G:\UNITY\Projects\PoliSim\docs\specs\USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1394 reads "SETTLED, NOT READINGS: present stands against (US-10, F3); the independents (US-10); ...". The thing it calls settled is the BLOC STANCE term at :786-788 ("YEA iff yea > nay + present ... present stands against, F3"). R10 at :1376-1377 ("present outside the stance and the observation (stance YEA iff yea > nay; d over yea + nay)") is a declared reading of that same term. Present is the only SETTLED item with a declared reading against it. Nothing else in R1-R18 reads the independents, the Vice President, the override base (R15 reads the override's sides, not its base), the UNPREDICTABLE set or the scope (R2 changes the fit, not where draws are made). Nothing else in the block resolves the conflict. THE FIRST RUN item 12 (:1212) and the PRINTED ONLY clause at :1245-1246 run and name every declared reading, R10 included, while :1394 says present-stands-against is not a reading. QUESTIONS FOR ELIAS (:1410-1412) gives readings only to contestable choices, so the block says present is both contestable and settled. The brief (facts.md:75, "which members count (yea/nay; present; ...)") gave this choice to the panel. So it is a panel default, and R10 is the legitimate side. The SETTLED entry is the error. Two corrections to the claim: - Its secondary point is already handled. The DECLARED READINGS header at :1355-1356 says "each runs the whole rule with one change", so V is recomputed under R10. - The F3 SET (:789-790) is US-10's fixed opposed_f3 definition, and readings print only headline figures (:1212-1213). So the "say whether V and F3's comparison are recomputed" part of the fix is not needed. Severity is at the low end of medium at most. Readings never stop the rule and never enter the outcome or the decisive digest (:1245-1246, :1449). The conflict changes only whether R10 appears in the verdict line's readings clause and the document. Fix: delete "present stands against (US-10, F3);" from the SETTLED line at :1394. Alternatively, reword it to "present stands against in the default (US-10, F3; R10 prints the alternative)".

**What was done.** Done: SETTLED says present stands against "in the default"; R10 prints the alternative, with V and F3's comparison recomputed, and never stops the rule.

### facts-F2 (medium; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** The cheap bar regenerates the catalog in memory and fails on any byte of difference
    (Generated.PolishVetoRates' pattern).

**Defect.** False precedent. Generated.PolishVetoRates is written by a Perl tool (Tools/veto_b1_backtest.pl) and is never regenerated in memory or compared byte for byte. PresidentialVetoDiagnostic recomputes its rows (AtRisk, Vetoed, rate) from the CSVs and compares the two source digests. GeneratedCatalogCheck says outright that it compares digests rather than re-parsing. An implementer copying 'PolishVetoRates' pattern' builds a value and digest check, not the byte check the sentence demands.

**Evidence.** Assets/Scripts/Elections/Generated/PolishVetoRates.cs:1-8 ("GENERATED by Tools/veto_b1_backtest.pl ... PresidentialVetoDiagnostic re-reads both CSVs and recomputes every figure"); Assets/Editor/PresidentialVetoDiagnostic.cs:232-249 (row-by-row and digest comparison); Assets/Editor/GeneratedCatalogCheck.cs:24-32.

**Fix proposed.** Drop the parenthesis, or replace it with: "(the byte-for-byte form is new here: PolishVetoRates is held only by its rows and source digests in PresidentialVetoDiagnostic)".

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: The defect is real: the parenthesis cites a precedent that does not work the way the sentence says. The quote is exact, at USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1337-1338: "The cheap bar regenerates the catalog in memory and fails on any byte of difference (Generated.PolishVetoRates' pattern)." 1. PolishVetoRates is written by Perl, not regenerated in memory. Assets/Scripts/Elections/Generated/PolishVetoRates.cs:1 reads "GENERATED by Tools/veto_b1_backtest.pl" (also Tools/veto_b1_backtest.pl:26 and :177). Line 8 reads "PresidentialVetoDiagnostic re-reads both CSVs and recomputes every figure". COMPLETED.md:37247 and docs/generated/README.md:18 say the same: the Perl run writes the table, and PresidentialVetoDiagnostic "recomputes" it. 2. The check on it compares values and digests, not bytes. Assets/Editor/PresidentialVetoDiagnostic.cs:232-244 recounts AtRisk and Vetoed per president from the CSVs and compares them to OfRecord row by row, with the rate within 1e-12. Lines 245-249 compare the two CSV SHA-256s with SourceDigest and VetoRecordDigest. Nothing in Assets/ or Tools/ regenerates PolishVetoRates.cs in memory or compares its bytes; a grep for PolishVetoRates finds only these readers. 3. The other existing generated-catalog guard also avoids re-deriving. Assets/Editor/GeneratedCatalogCheck.cs:24-32 states it compares a digest "and not a re-parse". 4. Nothing else in the block defines the "pattern". Searching lines 725-1510 for "pattern", "byte" and "cheap bar" finds the phrase only at 1338. So the parenthesis is a false reference that contradicts the byte-for-byte requirement in the same sentence. That fits the brief's "medium = ... or a false reference". The fix holds: drop the parenthesis, or say the byte form is new here. A byte-for-byte precedent does exist elsewhere: PresidentialVoteBacktest.Run "fails while the block differs from what it measures" (docs/generated/README.md:17). That one could be cited instead.

**What was done.** Done with game-F6: the byte-for-byte catalog check is stated as new here; Generated.PolishVetoRates is named as the precedent for the catalog's shape only, held by its rows and source digests in PresidentialVetoDiagnostic.

### facts-F3 (medium; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** - B1'S YARDSTICK: 45 named of 166, recomputed from
  ElectionsData/poland/third_readings_term10.csv

**Defect.** The numbers are swapped in meaning. B1 names 166 acts, and 45 of those were vetoed. '45 named of 166' says B1 named 45. Elsewhere the block correctly writes 'B1 = 45 of 166', and S1's test 166 * E[hits] < 45 * E[named] reads it correctly, so only this sentence misstates what the documents say.

**Evidence.** Tools/us_veto_backtest.pl:80-83 ($b1Named == 166 && $b1Vetoed == 45); docs/generated/VETO_B1_BACKTEST.md:41 ("precision 45 of the 166 acts B1 names (27.1 %)").

**Fix proposed.** Write: "B1'S YARDSTICK: 166 named, 45 of them vetoed (45 of 166), recomputed from ...".

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: The defect is real. USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:756 reads "- B1'S YARDSTICK: 45 named of 166, recomputed from ElectionsData/poland/third_readings_term10.csv exactly as us_veto_backtest.pl does. Any other result is a DEFECT." The sources say B1 names 166 acts and 45 of them were vetoed. Tools/us_veto_backtest.pl:75-83 adds 1 to $b1Named for every act B1 names and adds 1 to $b1Vetoed for the vetoed ones, then dies unless $b1Named == 166 && $b1Vetoed == 45. docs/generated/VETO_B1_BACKTEST.md:41 says "precision 45 of the 166 acts B1 names (27.1 %)", and the brief (facts.md:62, 126) gives "45 of 166, 27.1 %". The block also defines NAMED as a term of its own at line 836 ("NAMED: sent, and rule (a) fires"), so in its own vocabulary "45 named of 166" says 45 were named, which is backwards. The block's other three mentions get it right: line 1206 ("B1 = 45 of 166"), line 1234 (S1 fires iff "166 * E[hits] < 45 * E[named]", which only works if 45/166 means hits over named) and lines 1253-1254 ("B1's 27.1 % (45 of 166)"). Those lines and the "exactly as us_veto_backtest.pl does" clause make the intended meaning recoverable, so this sits nearer low than medium. It is still a false statement inside a clause whose result is checked ("Any other result is a DEFECT"): an implementer who takes it literally would assert 45 named and flag a DEFECT on every correct run. The proposed fix is correct: "B1'S YARDSTICK: 166 named, 45 of them vetoed (45 of 166, 27.1 %), recomputed from ...".

**What was done.** Done: "B1'S YARDSTICK: 45 vetoed of 166 named (precision 45/166)".

### facts-F4 (medium; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** - DATA: the roll calls of counted measures, which are the only roll calls on disk. No
  cloture vote, failed measure or override party split is saved.

**Defect.** Two false statements about the record. (1) The final-agreement roll calls are not the only roll calls on disk. veto_record.md records 761 roll calls fetched and 748 read (13 finals moved off earlier roll calls), and every override roll call is saved out of tree as member-level XML. (2) The override party split is saved. us_veto_prep.pl parses each override roll call's XML by party and only collapses it to yea and nay totals when writing the extract. THE OVERRIDE section rests on the same premise ('no override crossing can be fitted'). That is true of the extract only, not of what is on disk, and a stop's options could be chosen on the false version.

**Evidence.** ElectionsData/usa/veto_record.md:25 ("761 fetched, 748 read"), :88 ("the review's fixes moved 13 finals off earlier roll calls"); Tools/us_veto_prep.pl:414 (`my $r = $roll{$o->{rel}}; ... for my $p (values %{$r->{party}}) { $y += $p->[0]; $n += $p->[1]; }`).

**Fix proposed.** Write: "DATA: the final-agreement roll calls of counted measures, the only roll calls the extract carries. The extract holds no cloture vote or failed measure. Override roll calls are saved out of tree with member votes, but the extract keeps only their totals, so carrying their party split needs a prep change (re-pinned)." In THE OVERRIDE, change "the extract holds override totals without a party split" so that it names the extract, not the source.

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: The defect is real. Block line 896-897 reads: "DATA: the roll calls of counted measures, which are the only roll calls on disk. No cloture vote, failed measure or override party split is saved." Both claims are false about what is on disk. (1) Override roll calls are fetched to disk as member-level XML. Tools/us_veto_prep.pl:239 adds each override's roll call to %need, the same list the 'rolls' mode prints for fetching (:246-248). Lines 254-256 then read each file from $OUT/$rel. Those files include the override roll calls of H.R. 131 and H.R. 504 (veto_record.md:93, "both sustained in the House on 8 January 2026"). Those two measures are UNPREDICTABLE, so they are not counted measures, yet their roll calls are on disk. veto_record.md:25 and :88 also record "761 fetched, 748 read": 13 superseded final roll calls are on disk too. Even under the block's own term A ROLL CALL (finals only, :773), "the only roll calls on disk" is false. (2) The override party split is on disk, and prep computes it. Lines 262-276 (House) and 285-292 (Senate) build $r{party} by R, D and I for every roll call it reads, overrides included. Line 414 collapses it to yea and nay totals only when writing the CSV. veto_record.md:16 confirms the extract holds "each override roll call with its yeas, nays and result" and no split. This matters beyond wording. S3's and S2's options (:1265-1268) offer only "source more roll calls (the Senate's cloture and failed votes, BILLED)". The false DATA sentence hides a cheaper option: override roll calls with member votes are already on disk, and only a prep change (re-pinned) would carry their split. One part of the claim is overstated. THE OVERRIDE (:1020) already says "the extract holds override totals without a party split", so it names the extract and needs no fix. That leaves the DATA line as a false reference, which is medium severity. The proposed DATA rewrite is correct: "the final-agreement roll calls of counted measures, the only roll calls the extract carries; override roll calls are saved out of tree with member votes, but the extract keeps only their totals."

**What was done.** Done: DATA names the final-agreement roll calls the extract carries; the override roll calls are saved out of tree with member votes and the extract keeps their totals, so their split would be a prep change (re-pinned, by amendment); THE OVERRIDE's reason names the extract.

### form-F2 (medium; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.**     e_sent=<p/q>  e_sent_xxii=<p/q>  e_named=<p/q>  e_hits=<p/q>  p_none_xxii=<p/q>

**Defect.** The decisive digest is specified only for a run in which every figure is a rational. The block itself produces runs where that is not true. When S3 fires, 'S1 and S2 are not evaluated, and their figures print UNFITTED', so p_sent, p_named and the e_* lines have no p/q value. When V_XXII is empty, its figures print 'UNFITTED m=0', yet as an empty sum and an empty product they would be 0/1 and 1/1. For an UNFITTED cell, 'cell.<XX>.obs.<i>' is defined over the 'drawn list', and an unfitted cell has none. One implementer would leave these lines out, a second would write UNFITTED, a third would compute them anyway. Each produces a different digest for the same outcome, so a later reimplementation would show 'a moved digest' with no change of fact.

**Evidence.** The digest key list is given with 'Rationals are "p/q" in lowest terms ... Nothing else enters'. The S3 text, S2's case 'no Rule XXII measure on the veto path', and the TOO THIN print rules say these figures print without a value. The R-US18 precedent fixes this: 'each computed whatever an earlier condition of its step gives'.

**Fix proposed.** Add under THE DECISIVE DIGEST: "Every key is written in every run. A figure that reads an UNFITTED cell is written key=UNFITTED. Over an empty V_XXII, e_sent_xxii=0/1 and p_none_xxii=1/1. An UNFITTED cell writes the obs lines of its own observations in fit order (pooled=the partner it would pool with)." Alternatively, follow R-US18's form and state that every figure is computed whatever S3 gives, even where it prints UNFITTED.

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: I confirm the defect. The quote matches USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1445 exactly, and nothing elsewhere in the block fills the gap. (1) The digest has to cover S3 runs. Its outcome key lists S3 (line 1447). An S3 first run is registered "ASKED (sNNN), with the outcome and the digest" (1456), and the mutation suite plants an S3 path (1487). Yet 1225 says "When S3 fires, S1 and S2 are not evaluated, and their figures print UNFITTED". TOO THIN (1091) adds "every figure that reads it prints 'UNFITTED', with no value". So in such a run e_named, e_hits, e_sent_xxii, p_none_xxii, and any p_sent/p_named that reads the unfitted cell, have no value. The digest still requires "<p/q>" (1443-1445) and says "Rationals are 'p/q' in lowest terms ... Nothing else enters" (1448-1449). The block never says the figures are computed anyway. Its only reason for not computing them is "A decision cannot be computed on an unfitted cell (H4)" (1224). Some implementers would leave the lines out, some would write UNFITTED, and some would compute on the n<12 union. Each choice gives a different digest. (2) Empty V_XXII is a real case. S2's case "no Rule XXII measure on the veto path" prints "UNFITTED m=0" (1229-1230, also 1092). The digest still wants e_sent_xxii=<p/q> and p_none_xxii=<p/q>. The empty sum and product would give 0/1 and 1/1, so the print and the digest disagree. (3) The obs lines are undefined for an UNFITTED cell. They read "(i 0-based, drawn list, fit order)" (1436-1437). An UNFITTED cell draws nothing (933), so it has no drawn list. cell.<XX>.n is also open between the two n's in the UNFITTED print, "n=<n> ... (pooled n=<n'>)" (1089). The fix the claim cites is real and absent here. R-US18's digest line has "each computed whatever an earlier condition of its step gives" (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1693), and the R-US17 block has no equivalent. Medium severity is right: none of these choices moves the outcome, but each moves the frozen digest. The proposed fix is to state that every key is written in every run, with a fixed form for UNFITTED values and for empty V_XXII.

**What was done.** Done: every key in every run; key=UNFITTED for a figure reading an UNFITTED cell; e_sent_xxii=0/1 and p_none_xxii=1/1 over an empty V_XXII; an UNFITTED cell's n and obs lines over its union, pooled naming its partner.

### form-F5 (medium; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** - S1, FAR MORE (R-US17's first STOP as G5 reads it, PROVISIONAL; also R-US17's test "(a)

**Defect.** False reference. G5 is the plan's register of open readings, items 1-24 (plan, lines ~2016-2040). None of those items reads R-US17's 'far more'. The reading that 'far more' means a pooled precision below B1's was made by US-10 before its first run and recorded in §802 and in US-10's Built line, labelled PROVISIONAL under G5's principle. A reader who follows 'as G5 reads it' to G5 finds nothing about it.

**Evidence.** COMPLETED.md §802: '**"Far more"** is a pooled precision below B1's (PROVISIONAL, G5).' US-10's Built line in the plan: '"far more" read, before the first run, as a pooled precision below B1's on Poland (PROVISIONAL, G5)'. G5's own list (plan, lines ~2016-2040) has no such item.

**Fix proposed.** Replace it with: "S1, FAR MORE (R-US17's first STOP as US-10 read it before its first run, s802, PROVISIONAL under G5; also R-US17's test ..."

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: The defect is real. The block's text is quoted accurately at USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1232: "S1, FAR MORE (R-US17's first STOP as G5 reads it, PROVISIONAL; ...". The phrase says the reading comes from G5, but G5 does not contain it. G5 (plan:2016) is a principle ("the open readings - PROVISIONAL on the recommendation where reversible, stopped where not") plus a fixed register of 24 items (plan:2017-2040). Its records are named as president_returns.md reading 3, coalition_declarations_2023.md §12 and COMPLETED.md §778-§780, §783, §784. None of the 24 items concerns R-US17 or "far more". COMPLETED.md:37737 also confirms that G5's register is exactly "the 24 open readings" of 2026-10-06. The "far more" reading was made by US-10. Its two records are COMPLETED.md:37953 in §802 ('"Far more" is a pooled precision below B1's (PROVISIONAL, G5)') and US-10's Built line at plan:287 ('"far more" read, before the first run, as a pooled precision below B1's on Poland (PROVISIONAL, G5)'). There "G5" marks the reading as provisional under G5's principle. It does not mean G5 states the reading. Nothing else in the block (lines 730-1505) gives the reading's source. The block's other G5 mention, plan:783 ("Poland's G5 items 14-15"), treats G5 as a numbered register, so a reader would look for an item there and find none. The decision formula itself (166*E[hits] < 45*E[named], plan:1234) is fully stated, so the outcome is not affected. Still, this is a false provenance in a block that will be frozen, which the rubric rates medium. The proposed fix (cite US-10's reading before its first run, s802, PROVISIONAL under G5) matches both records.

**What was done.** Done: S1 is "R-US17's first STOP as US-10 read it before its first run, s802, PROVISIONAL under G5".

### game-F6 (medium; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.**   - The cheap bar regenerates the catalog in memory and fails on any byte of difference
    (Generated.PolishVetoRates' pattern).

**Defect.** The reference is false. Generated.PolishVetoRates is written by a Perl tool (Tools/veto_b1_backtest.pl). It is guarded by PresidentialVetoDiagnostic, which re-reads the two CSVs, recomputes the figures row by row and compares the two source digests. Nothing regenerates the file in memory or compares bytes, and the generator is not the check. An implementer following 'the pattern' would build a figure-and-digest comparison, not the byte comparison the sentence requires.

**Evidence.** Assets/Scripts/Elections/Generated/PolishVetoRates.cs header: 'GENERATED by Tools/veto_b1_backtest.pl ... PresidentialVetoDiagnostic re-reads both CSVs and recomputes every figure'. PresidentialVetoDiagnostic.cs:233-248 compares the OfRecord rows and the SourceDigest/VetoRecordDigest strings.

**Fix proposed.** Drop the parenthesis, or replace it with '(UsHouseCountCheck's card-block pattern: regenerated in memory, compared byte for byte)'. Keep 'Generated.PolishVetoRates' only as the precedent for the catalog's shape: a static class with a SourceDigest const and record arrays.

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: The defect is real. The quote is exact: USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1337-1338 reads "The cheap bar regenerates the catalog in memory and fails on any byte of difference (Generated.PolishVetoRates' pattern)." It sits as a sub-bullet under THE CATALOG, which UsVetoDrawCheck writes (1327-1328), so the parenthesis belongs to the cheap-bar sentence. The cited precedent does not do what that sentence describes. (1) Assets/Scripts/Elections/Generated/PolishVetoRates.cs:1 says "GENERATED by Tools/veto_b1_backtest.pl", and line 8 says "PresidentialVetoDiagnostic re-reads both CSVs and recomputes every figure". So a Perl tool writes it, not a C# check, and no C# check regenerates it. (2) Its guard, PresidentialVetoDiagnostic.cs:220-249 (registered in CheckSuite.cs:219), recounts at-risk and vetoed statutes from the CSVs. It compares them row by row against OfRecord (233-244) and checks the two CSV SHA-256s against SourceDigest and VetoRecordDigest (245-249). It never renders the file and never compares bytes. (3) A search for PolishVetoRates across *.cs/*.pl/*.sh/*.ps1 finds only veto_b1_backtest.pl, PresidentialVeto.cs and PresidentialVetoDiagnostic.cs. GeneratedCatalogCheck.cs:29-32 even argues against re-parsing and uses a digest compare, and it covers SwedishValkretsReturns2022, not this file. Nothing else in the block defines "PolishVetoRates' pattern" differently, so the plan's only other mention (1296) does not rescue the citation. The task's rubric classes a false reference as medium. The sentence's own words (in memory, any byte) are explicit enough that a careful implementer would probably follow them. Still, the parenthesis points to a figure-and-digest precedent, which is the opposite of the rule. The fix: drop the parenthesis, or keep PolishVetoRates only as the precedent for the catalog's shape (a static class with a SourceDigest const and record arrays) and name a check that really regenerates and byte-compares. Before citing UsHouseCountCheck as that check, confirm it regenerates its card block in memory and compares bytes. Its WriteReadings/STALE logic at UsHouseCountCheck.cs:723 suggests it does, but I did not fully verify it.

**What was done.** Done: as facts-F2.

### inference-b1-mislabel (medium; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** - B1'S YARDSTICK: 45 named of 166, recomputed from

**Defect.** The yardstick is misdescribed. B1 named 166 bills, and 45 of them were vetoed (precision 45/166). "45 named of 166" inverts this. The block also requires an exact match ("Any other result is a DEFECT"), so an implementer who asserts named = 45 fails a correct run, or checks the wrong field.

**Evidence.** us_veto_backtest.pl lines 74-84: `$b1Named++; $b1Vetoed++ if $o eq 'vetoed'` and `die ... unless $b1Named == 166 && $b1Vetoed == 45`. S1 (line 1234) correctly uses 166 * E[hits] < 45 * E[named].

**Fix proposed.** Change it to "B1'S YARDSTICK: 45 vetoed of 166 named (precision 45/166), recomputed from ...".

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: The defect is real. USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:756 reads "B1'S YARDSTICK: 45 named of 166". That wording says B1 named 45 bills, but it is the other way round. Tools/us_veto_backtest.pl:75-83 counts $b1Named++ on every act B1 names and $b1Vetoed++ only on the vetoed ones, then dies unless $b1Named == 166 && $b1Vetoed == 45. docs/generated/VETO_B1_BACKTEST.md:41 agrees: "precision 45 of the 166 acts B1 names (27.1 %)". So B1 names 166 acts and 45 of them were vetoed. Line 756 is the only place in the block that gets this wrong. The other references are all correct: - :1206 "B1 = 45 of 166" - :1234 "fires iff 166 * E[hits] < 45 * E[named]" - :1253-1254 "B1's 27.1 % (45 of 166)" Line 756 also says "recomputed ... exactly as us_veto_backtest.pl does", and that script makes the right assertion. A careful implementer would probably work out the right answer from these, so this is more likely low (clarity) than medium. The flaw itself is still real: the line that defines the term, and attaches "Any other result is a DEFECT" to it, has a false label. Fix: change line 756 to read "B1'S YARDSTICK: 45 vetoed of the 166 acts B1 names (precision 45/166, 27.1 %), recomputed from".

**What was done.** Done with facts-F3.

### computability-C11 (low; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** The ONE exception is THE US-10 IDENTITIES

**Defect.** The claim is false as written.
