The block itself reads non-counted rows elsewhere: item 1 prints "measures and vetoes, counted and in all", the decisive digest carries "unpredictable.<i>" lines, and the sustained override votes of H.R. 131 and H.R. 504 "print on their own line". An implementer enforcing COUNTED ONLY literally would drop those prints.

**Evidence.** THE FIRST RUN item 1. THE DECISIVE DIGEST's unpredictable lines. TRUMP'S 2025 VETOES: "they enter the decisive digest by name only"; "their sustained votes print on their own line only".

**Fix proposed.** List every exception: the US-10 identities; item 1's "in all" counts; the UNPREDICTABLE line and its override line; the digest's unpredictable.<i> lines.

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: The defect is real, and the quote is exact. USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:769-771 says: "every count, sum, denominator, cell, rate and digest of this rule reads counted measures only. The ONE exception is THE US-10 IDENTITIES (THE FIRST RUN, item 10)". Other parts of the same block contradict that "ONE exception" in exactly the categories the clause names. Counts outside item 10 that read all rows: - :1167, item 1: "measures and vetoes, counted and in all". - :1168: "|V| beside F3's 34". The F3 SET at :789-790 calls 34 "its count over all rows". - :1316-1317, the per-president row: "0 counted vetoes; 2 declared unpredictable", a count of rows that are not counted. The decisive digest also reads rows that are not counted: - :1438: "unpredictable.<i>=<congress>|<measure>". - :1432: "source=<SourceDigest>". SourceDigest is the "SHA-256 over its raw bytes" (:748), meaning the whole CSV, the two UNPREDICTABLE rows included. Nothing in the block reconciles these with the clause. TRUMP'S 2025 VETOES (:1319-1320) only says the two vetoes "are read by THE US-10 IDENTITIES only as US-10 read them... and they enter the decisive digest by name only". That repeats the conflict and does not resolve it. The general clause itself still claims a single exception. One part of the evidence is weak. The sustained-vote line (:1315, "print on their own line only") is a printed line, not a count or a digest, so COUNTED ONLY does not govern it. That does not rescue the clause, because item 1's "in all" counts and the digest's unpredictable and source lines fall squarely under "count" and "digest". The practical risk is small, because the specific clauses are explicit and an implementer would most likely follow them over the general one. Even so, the block states something false about itself, so low severity (clarity) is right. Concrete fix: limit COUNTED ONLY to the rule's fitted and scored figures, and list the exceptions where the clause stands: - THE US-10 IDENTITIES (item 10); - item 1's "in all" counts and F3's 34; - the UNPREDICTABLE line, the per-president "2 declared unpredictable" and the sustained-vote line; - the decisive digest's source= and unpredictable.<i> lines.

**What was done.** Done with consistency-IC-5: COUNTED ONLY lists every exception, none of which enters a figure, denominator or decision.

### computability-C12 (low; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** A term with no counted veto prints "no counted veto (H2)"

**Defect.** Trump's second term (0 counted vetoes) is given three print forms, and none of them says which totals still print. One is "no counted veto (H2)"; another is "0 counted vetoes; 2 declared unpredictable" (TRUMP'S 2025 VETOES); TOO THIN gives a third, "UNDEFINED (denominator 0)", for the hit rate. Its E[vetoable sent], E[named] and E[false positives] are well defined and could print or not.

**Evidence.** TOTALS, TRUMP'S 2025 VETOES ("not in the per-president rows (Trump's second term: \"0 counted vetoes; 2 declared unpredictable\")") and TOO THIN.

**Fix proposed.** State it once, for example: "a term with V_COUNT = 0 prints its sums over V as usual, its hit rate as 'no counted veto (H2)' in place of UNDEFINED, and its record line '0 counted vetoes; 2 declared unpredictable'".

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: The defect is real, and low severity fits it. I quoted the block's text correctly, allowing for line wraps. I tried to refute it and could not. (1) USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1154-1155 reads, under "Totals, pooled and per president.": "A term with no counted veto prints "no counted veto (H2)"." It does not say "with no figure", and it does not name which totals it replaces. The block's other replacement rules do say what is dropped. TOO THIN at 1089-1094 has "with no figure and no interval", "with no value" and "with no interval". So two honest implementers could differ: one prints only that line for Trump's second term, the other prints E[vetoable sent], E_XXII, E[named], E[false positives], precision and P(NONE) as usual and swaps in the line for the hit rate only. (2) Lines 1316-1317 give a second string for "the per-president rows (Trump's second term: "0 counted vetoes; 2 declared unpredictable")". The block never says whether these rows are item 1's RECORD per president (1167, "measures and vetoes, counted and in all") or item 4's per-president totals (1154/1177). If they are the totals rows, they clash with the string in (1). (3) TOO THIN at 1093 makes any ratio with an exact denominator of 0 print "UNDEFINED (denominator 0)". That covers Trump's second-term hit rate, E[hits]/V_COUNT with V_COUNT = 0, and nothing says which rule wins. The terms really are split: us_veto_backtest.pl:88 lists 'Donald J. Trump (second term)' as its own president value, so "per president" means per term. The term has 0 counted vetoes: the block asserts only H.R. 131 and H.R. 504 are UNPREDICTABLE (763-765), and the plan's text at 723 says those were his only two vetoes. Nothing in the block settles this. The impact is limited to the print: per-president figures are PRINTED ONLY (1243), and the decisive digest leaves them out (1430-1449). So the outcome cannot change, but the generated document can. That makes it a clarity defect, which is the low severity claimed. The proposed fix of one sentence that states which form goes where is the right remedy.

**What was done.** Done with consistency-IC-10: A TERM WITH NO COUNTED VETO is stated once, in THE PRINT.

### computability-C16 (low; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** record it is asserted for every roll call read.

**Defect.** The quorum assertion on the record does not say which W and which "present" it uses. It could be MEMBERS over all labels or over R and D only, and VOTING (yea+nay+present) over all labels or over R and D only. The Senate's caucus entries are not addressed either. A failure is a DEFECT, so the test must be exact.

**Evidence.** WHEN THE DRAW MATTERS, QUORUM: "floor(W/2) + 1 is always present. On the record it is asserted ...". A LABEL'S SPLIT defines MEMBERS and VOTING per label.

**Fix proposed.** "Asserted: VOTING summed over R, D and I >= floor(MEMBERS summed over R, D and I / 2) + 1, on every roll call of a counted measure."

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: The defect is real, and low severity is right. The quoted text is accurate: USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:980-981 reads "QUORUM: in the model every bloc seat votes, so floor(W/2) + 1 is always present. On the record it is asserted for every roll call read." (the quote runs across the line break). Nothing else in the block defines the test. A case-insensitive grep finds the quorum only at line 980 inside the block (1016 merely quotes CRS), and THE IDENTITIES (item 10, lines 1192-1207) leave it out. **W is undefined.** In this section W has several meanings: - W_H, the SEATS' House sworn count, at line 949; - W_S, the SEATS' sworn count with the caucus, at line 957; - three Rule XXII W's at line 965: "(B) the SEATS' sworn; (A) the roll call's members, all labels; play, the sworn count on the day". (A) under the House majority test has no W at all, and counts "R and D blocs ... independents outside" (lines 952-953). So "W" for a record roll call could be the Congress-level SEATS sworn count, which comes from one row through the overwrite quirk at lines 798-806. Or it could be that roll call's own members, over all labels or over R and D only. **"Present" is undefined.** A LABEL'S SPLIT (lines 784-785) defines MEMBERS and VOTING per label, but the quorum line uses neither term. It does not say whether I counts, and the Senate independent "is in no split" (lines 792-795). **The caucus point is real too.** us_veto_backtest.pl:185-187 shows that Senate independents sit in the I split columns. senate_I_caucus only reassigns them to R or D. So a W built "with the caucus entries in their blocs" (line 958) on top of the I columns would count them twice, and one built from R and D plus the caucus differs from R+D+I whenever an entry is neither R nor D (line 793). Two honest implementers could therefore write different assertions. On real passage roll calls every reading should pass, so it cannot mis-decide, which keeps it at low (clarity). The proposed fix is sound. VOTING over R, D and I against floor(MEMBERS over R, D and I / 2) + 1 uses the roll call's own listed members, which matches the sworn count used at backtest.pl:186. It does not double count the caucus, and its scope ("every roll call of a counted measure") follows COUNTED ONLY (lines 769-772).

**What was done.** Done: the quorum assertion is VOTING summed over R, D and I >= floor(MEMBERS summed over R, D and I / 2) + 1, on every roll call of a counted measure.

### computability-C7 (low; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** B1'S YARDSTICK: 45 named of 166

**Defect.** The numbers are swapped in the wording. B1 named 166 and 45 of those were vetoed. The script checks $b1Named == 166 and $b1Vetoed == 45, and the verdict line itself reads "B1's 27.1 % (45 of 166)".

**Evidence.** us_veto_backtest.pl line 83: "unless $b1Named == 166 && $b1Vetoed == 45".

**Fix proposed.** "B1'S YARDSTICK: 45 vetoed of 166 named".

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: The defect is real. The quote is accurate: USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:756 reads "B1'S YARDSTICK: 45 named of 166". That attaches "named" to the 45, but 166 is the number B1 names and 45 is how many of those were vetoed. Every source agrees on this. In Tools/us_veto_backtest.pl:75-83, $b1Named++ counts every act B1 names and $b1Vetoed++ counts only those whose outcome is 'vetoed'. The script then dies unless `$b1Named == 166 && $b1Vetoed == 45`, and its message prints "$b1Vetoed of $b1Named" ("45 of 166"). docs/generated/VETO_B1_BACKTEST.md:41 says "precision 45 of the 166 acts B1 names (27.1 %)", and US_VETO_BACKTEST.md:103 says "45 of 166 (27.1 %)". The block's own S1 test at line 1234, "fires iff 166 * E[hits] < 45 * E[named]", treats 45/166 as hits over named, so 166 is the named count. Taken literally, line 756 would have an implementer assert named == 45, and the line itself says "Any other result is a DEFECT" (757-758). That check would always fail against the script's figures. Line 1234 and line 1206 ("B1 = 45 of 166") show what was meant, so a careful implementer would probably get it right, and low (clarity) is the right severity. It is still a false statement inside a block that will be frozen. Fix: "B1'S YARDSTICK: 45 vetoed of 166 named".

**What was done.** Done with facts-F3: "45 vetoed of 166 named (precision 45/166)".

### computability-C9 (low; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** the INFLUENCE figures, and every in-game chance

**Defect.** Every FITTED figure must carry n and a BOOT interval. INFLUENCE produces a categorical outcome per removal and a count k of roll calls, so there is no number to bootstrap. Bootstrapping every removal within 2000 replicates is also not specified (pick keys, holding rules). In the same way, a RECORD BUDGET CLASS measure in V reads no cell (it is tested at K = 0), yet "every per-measure probability" is FITTED. Its label would be "FITTED n=0" with a degenerate interval.

**Evidence.** THE PRINT: "A FITTED FIGURE is any number computed from the cells' observations ... every per-measure probability ... the INFLUENCE figures". INFLUENCE (item 11) lists roll calls, their new outcomes and k. WHEN THE DRAW MATTERS: "RECORD BUDGET CLASS on V: the test at K = 0 in each chamber, with no draw."

**Fix proposed.** Move INFLUENCE's k ("k of <distinct fitted roll calls>") and the per-measure figures of RECORD BUDGET CLASS measures to NOT FITTED, printed as "x of y" or as "0/1 at K = 0, no draw".

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: The defect is real, and the quote is accurate: USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1046-1047 reads "precision, hit rate and P(NONE), the INFLUENCE figures, and every in-game chance". That passage makes every FITTED figure carry the label "on EVERY surface" (1047-1049). Line 1064 sends every fitted figure other than a cell proportion to BOOT. 1. INFLUENCE has no number the rule can bootstrap. Item 11 (1208-1211) only recomputes the OUTCOME for each removal. It lists the roll calls that flip the outcome and sets "k = their number". The outcome is categorical (INSTALL/S3/S1/S2/S1+S2, and "Its figures are not part of it", 1237-1238), so k is the only number.    - THE PRINT's m list (1057-1059) has no m for k.    - The replicate procedure (1066-1074) covers only PER-MEASURE figures and TOTALS. It never says whether INFLUENCE is re-run inside each of the 2000 replicates, or over which roll calls.    - So k's required "FITTED n=.. m=.. 95% [..] BOOT" label cannot be computed. 2. The block contradicts itself on k. The verdict line (1256-1257) prints "<FRAGILE: <k> roll calls each flip the outcome | ...>" with no label. That breaks the "EVERY surface" rule at 1047-1049. Two honest implementers would split: one prints k plainly, the other tries to invent a bootstrap for it. Nothing elsewhere settles this: "printed only" at 1208 and 1244 does not exempt a figure from FITTED, since omega is also printed only and is fitted. 3. The second half of the claim also holds. A RECORD BUDGET CLASS measure can be in V: V (825-827) does not exclude the class, and 853-854 and 974 handle "RECORD BUDGET CLASS on V". Such a measure is tested "at K = 0 in each chamber, with no draw" (974), so its per-measure probabilities read no cell. By 1055 its n is 0, yet "every per-measure probability" (1046) is FITTED. Its label would be "FITTED n=0 m=1" with a degenerate [v, v] BOOT interval. This also conflicts with the definition's own lead clause, "computed from the cells' observations" (1045). Low severity is right. The proposed fix works: list INFLUENCE's k and the per-measure figures of budget-class measures under NOT FITTED, printed as "x of y" or "at K = 0, no draw".

**What was done.** Done: INFLUENCE's k and a RECORD BUDGET CLASS measure's per-measure figures are NOT FITTED; a FITTED per-measure probability is one that reads a cell.

### consistency-IC-10 (low; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** Totals, pooled and per president. A term with no counted veto prints "no counted veto

**Defect.** Three different forms are prescribed for the same case, Trump's second term with no counted veto: 'no counted veto (H2)' (THE FIRST RUN), '0 counted vetoes; 2 declared unpredictable' (TRUMP'S 2025 VETOES), and, for its hit rate, a ratio with exact denominator V_COUNT = 0, which THE PRINT prints as 'UNDEFINED (denominator 0)'. The per-president m is also never stated.

**Evidence.** THE FIRST RUN totals; TRUMP'S 2025 VETOES 'Trump's second term: "0 counted vetoes; 2 declared unpredictable"'; THE PRINT 'a ratio whose exact denominator is 0 prints "UNDEFINED (denominator 0)"'.

**Fix proposed.** Say which string goes where. For example: the record row prints '0 counted vetoes; 2 declared unpredictable'; the term's hit rate and the other V_COUNT-based figures print 'no counted veto (H2)' in place of 'UNDEFINED (denominator 0)'; per-president m = that term's |V| (or V_COUNT).

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: I confirm the defect is real, at low severity (clarity). The quotes are accurate. USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1154-1155 says "Totals, pooled and per president. A term with no counted veto prints "no counted veto (H2)"". :1093 says "a ratio whose exact denominator is 0 prints "UNDEFINED (denominator 0)"". :1316-1317 says "not in the per-president rows (Trump's second term: "0 counted vetoes; 2 declared unpredictable")". The claim slightly overstates one point. Two of the strings can be read as belonging to different places: the "0 counted vetoes" string fits item 1, "THE RECORD per president: measures and vetoes, counted and in all" (:1167), and "no counted veto (H2)" fits the totals. Even so, the block leaves three gaps that two honest implementers would fill differently: (1) It never says what "no counted veto (H2)" replaces. Trump's second term is not empty: the brief (facts.md:109) gives 120 measures for it, 118 of them counted once the two UNPREDICTABLE vetoes are removed. So that term can still have members of V and a non-zero E[named] or E[false positives]. In that case its precision is a real 0.0 %, not 0/0. One implementer will replace the term's whole totals row with the string and hide those figures. Another will print every total, with HIT RATE = E[hits]/V_COUNT shown as "UNDEFINED (denominator 0)" under the general rule at :1093. The string's scope is not stated. (2) "Per-president rows" (:1316) is never defined, so a reader cannot tell whether it means item 1's record rows or the per-president totals. It also fits awkwardly with item 1's "in all" column, which does include the two vetoes. (3) The per-president m is never stated. :1057-1058 gives m as "|V| for the sums over V, precision and the pooled P(NONE) ... V_COUNT for the hit rate". Only the pooled P(NONE) gets an m, yet :1154-1161 also prints P(NONE) per president. Read literally, the rule would print the pooled |V| as m on per-president sums, which is false. None of this enters the decisive digest (:1430-1449 lists no per-president figure, and :1243 makes the per-president figures print-only), so the severity is low. The proposed fix holds: - the record row prints "0 counted vetoes; 2 declared unpredictable"; - the block states exactly which totals "no counted veto (H2)" replaces, and whether E[named] and precision still print; - per-president m = that term's count within V (and within V_XXII), with the term's V_COUNT for the hit rate.

**What was done.** Done: THE PRINT's A TERM WITH NO COUNTED VETO says which string goes where (record row; sums over V as usual; V_COUNT-denominator figures "no counted veto (H2)"); a per-president figure's m is the same set within that term.

### consistency-IC-9 (low; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** P(NONE_EXP) = the same over V's MAJORITY-procedure measures.

**Defect.** P(NONE_EXP) is defined over the MAJORITY-procedure measures, which include MAJORITY (reconciliation). THE DECISION's PRINTED ONLY list calls the same figure "expedited-only P(NONE)". THE PRINT's m list also gives no m for it (only |V| for pooled P(NONE) and |V_XXII| for P(NONE_XXII)), so its label cannot be formed.

**Evidence.** SENATE PROCEDURE 'MAJORITY (reconciliation, US-18's 2 U.S.C. 641(e) path)'; THE DECISION 'the pooled and expedited-only P(NONE)'; THE PRINT 'm, for a derived figure: ...'.

**Fix proposed.** Define V_EXP = the measures of V whose kind is EXPEDITED (or rename to P(NONE_MAJ) if reconciliation is meant), and add "|V_EXP| for P(NONE_EXP)" to THE PRINT's m list.

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: I could not refute it. The defect is real, the quotes are accurate, and low is the right severity. 1. **The two definitions disagree.** USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1161-1162 defines "P(NONE_EXP) = the same over V's MAJORITY-procedure measures." The block itself says what MAJORITY procedure covers. At :822-824 SENATE PROCEDURE has two MAJORITY values: "MAJORITY (expedited)" and "MAJORITY (reconciliation, US-18's 2 U.S.C. 641(e) path)". At :956 the heading reads "SENATE, MAJORITY procedure (expedited or reconciliation)". So as written, the figure covers expedited plus reconciliation measures. THE DECISION at :1243 calls the same figure "the pooled and expedited-only P(NONE)", and the name NONE_EXP also says expedited. Two honest implementers would choose different sets. 2. **The difference can matter.** Nothing keeps a reconciliation measure out of V. V at :825-826 takes every counted measure with a PP-NAY roll call and excludes no budget class. The block expects RECORD BUDGET CLASS measures on V (":974 RECORD BUDGET CLASS on V: the test at K = 0"). Such a measure would have P(sent) of exactly 0 or 1. Counting it or not would therefore change the printed figure. 3. **No m is given.** THE PRINT's m list (:1057-1058) gives |V| for "the pooled P(NONE)", |V_XXII| for P(NONE_XXII) and V_COUNT for the hit rate. It gives nothing for P(NONE_EXP). Yet :1046-1047 makes every P(NONE) a FITTED figure, and :1082 requires every derived figure to print "m=<m>". The label for P(NONE_EXP) therefore cannot be formed as specified. The "m=0" UNFITTED rule at :1092 has no defined count to test either. 4. **Nothing elsewhere resolves it.** Neither the block nor the brief settles it: the brief mentions expedited procedures only at facts.md:79 and never P(NONE_EXP). The figure is printed only. It is not in the decisive digest (:1445) or the readings' headline figures (:1212-1213), so it cannot change a decision. Fix: either define V_EXP as the measures of V whose kind is EXPEDITED, or rename the figure to P(NONE_MAJ) and change "expedited-only" at :1243. Then add its m (|V_EXP| or |V_MAJ|) to the list at :1057-1058.

**What was done.** Done with computability-C6: V_EXP is defined (the measures of V whose kind is EXPEDITED), |V_EXP| is P(NONE_EXP)'s m, and THE DECISION's "expedited-only P(NONE)" is renamed P(NONE_EXP).

### form-F7 (low; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.**   made and (a) never fires (the panel's default, by analogy to Poland's G5 items 14-15).

**Defect.** G5 is not Poland's. It is the plan's register of Elias's 2026-10-06 rulings, whose items 14-15 happen to be Poland's §778 readings. Calling it 'Poland's G5' sends a reader looking for a Polish register that does not exist.

**Evidence.** Plan line ~2016: '**G5, the open readings ...**'. Items 14-15 (lines ~2030-2031): '*§778:* a backing party with no member voting puts nothing at risk' and '*§778:* a president no party backs puts nothing at risk'.

**Fix proposed.** Write it as "(DECLARED default, by analogy to G5 items 14-15, Poland's s778 readings)".

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: The defect is real. USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:783 reads "(the panel's default, by analogy to Poland's G5 items 14-15)". G5 is not Poland's. It is item 5 of "Elias's rulings of 2026-10-06, G1-G9 (installed §797)" (plan:2015-2016), and the plan keeps it as its own register ("G9: no page; this register stays in the plan", plan:2044). That register mixes US items (item 1 is US-3 reading 3, item 11 is US-2's shadow) with Polish ones (plan:2017, 2027). Items 14-15 are §778 readings that apply to Poland's veto (plan:2030-2031). Poland's own file agrees: coalition_declarations_2023.md:384 calls it "Elias's ruling G5 (COMPLETED.md §797; the plan's register of 2026-10-06)". The block contradicts itself here: everywhere else it uses G5 without qualification as the plan's register ("as G5 reads it", plan:1232; "with G1, G5, E4 and H4", plan:1410). One correction to the reviewer's evidence: a Polish "G5" does exist, but it means something else. ElectionsData/poland/records_by_date.md:408 has "**G5: the 2023 Senate's composition by committee**" (a data GAP, also at :125), and it has no items 14-15. So "Poland's G5" doesn't just point at nothing, it points a reader at the wrong G5, which makes the misattribution worse. You could read the possessive as covering only "items 14-15", but that reading is strained and clashes with the block's own usage. This stays a low-severity clarity defect. The proposed fix is right: "(the panel's DECLARED default, by analogy to G5 items 14-15, Poland's §778 readings)".

**What was done.** Done: "(DECLARED default, by analogy to G5 items 14-15, Poland's s778 readings)".

### inference-nmin-wilson (low; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** - N_MIN = 12 observations: a floor preregistered with no data read. A data-free note: at n
  = 12 the 95% Wilson interval of a share at p = 1/2 is no wider than 0.5 (0.492; 0.509 at
  11).

**Defect.** The floor's stated reason uses the Wilson interval, but the rule never prints Wilson: cell proportions print Clopper-Pearson. At x = 6 of 12 the CP interval is about [0.211, 0.789], width 0.578, which fails the "no wider than 0.5" criterion the note uses. So the note does not support the floor for the interval actually printed. (The Wilson figures themselves are correct: 0.4925 and 0.5088.)

**Evidence.** Line 1061: "CELL PROPORTIONS (q_any, q10): Clopper-Pearson exact, CP".

**Fix proposed.** Either restate the note for CP (for example, "at n = 12 the 95% CP interval at x = 6 is [0.211, 0.789]"), or say plainly that Wilson is only a yardstick for the floor and not the printed interval.

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: The defect is real, and low severity is right for it. The block justifies the floor with Wilson at USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:840-842: "at n = 12 the 95% Wilson interval of a share at p = 1/2 is no wider than 0.5 (0.492; 0.509 at 11)". But the only interval the rule prints for a cell proportion is Clopper-Pearson. Line 1061 says "CELL PROPORTIONS (q_any, q10): Clopper-Pearson exact, CP", line 1080 gives the label "... 95% [<lo>, <hi>] CP", and line 1171 prints q_any and q10 "(CP)". Nothing else in the block uses Wilson, and nothing explains why Wilson stands in for the printed interval. A grep of the repo's .md files finds "Wilson" only at line 841. The Wilson figures are correct. I checked them by hand: about 0.4925 at n = 12 and 0.5088 at n = 11. The CP interval at x = 6 of 12 is the textbook [0.2109, 0.7891]. I checked by hand that P(X >= 6 | 12, 0.211) is about 0.0250. Its width is 0.578, so under the interval actually printed the note's "no wider than 0.5" test fails at the floor. The defect changes no computation, because N_MIN = 12 and the n < 12 tests at lines 929-933 work either way. It is a clarity defect: a reader would take the note to mean the printed interval is at most 0.5 wide at the floor, and it is not. The proposed fix is cheap and correct: restate the note for CP at x = 6 of 12 ([0.211, 0.789]), or say plainly that Wilson is only a yardstick for the floor and not the interval printed.

**What was done.** Done: the Wilson note is stated as a yardstick for the floor only; the printed interval is Clopper-Pearson, wider at the same n.

## The first pass - refuted (46), with the skeptics' reasons

Two of these (fidelity-F1, inference-S2-knife-edge) were refuted as a declared default rather than a defect. The session put the choice they name - S2's reading of "never" - to Elias beside the block, the default unchanged.

### fidelity-F1 (high; upheld 0 of 3)

**Quote.** case "never sends": E_XXII[vetoable sent] = 0 exactly (that is, P(NONE_XXII) = 1).

**Defect.** R-US17's second STOP is carried over from a deterministic bloc model into a probabilistic one by reading "never" as an exact probability of zero. Under an empirical draw, this STOP can fire only if no index pair in any LOO list sends any measure of V_XXII. So S2 is effectively disabled whenever the SP cell (or the pooled cell) holds one observation whose share carries the PP over a cloture line, however small the resulting probability. This loses the STOP's stated purpose, spec-risk 4 ("a veto that may never fire ... The US risk is zero vetoes"). No ruling settles this translation. G5 rules only on "far more", yet the block says "QUESTIONS FOR ELIAS: None. H1-H3, with G1, G5, E4 and H4, settle every choice", and the readings that would catch the risk (R13) "never stop the rule".

**Evidence.** R-US17 (plan l.721): "STOPS and re-asks ... if a party-bloc Congress under Rule XXII never sends a vetoable bill. That last is the US risk, the mirror of E4's 43 of 43." Spec-risk 4 (l.2138) defines the risk as zero vetoes. The block (l.1240-1246) says readings are "PRINTED ONLY, never part of the outcome ... no reading ever stops the rule". R13 carries the meaningful forms ("S2 at P(NONE_XXII) >= 1/2; S2 at E_XXII[vetoable sent] < 1"). Failure scenario: an exact E_XXII of, say, 0.05 means that over the whole 115th-119th record, a bloc Congress with the draw almost surely sends no Rule XXII vetoable bill. That is R-US17's STOP condition, yet the outcome is INSTALL and the verdict line reads "the draw installs". S1 can miss too: a precision computed on a near-zero E[named] can still clear 27.1 %.

**Fix proposed.** Make S2's default the probabilistic form of "never": fire iff P(NONE_XXII) >= 1/2, i.e. the record replayed most likely sends no Rule XXII vetoable bill. Alternatively use E_XXII[vetoable sent] < 1. Keep "= 0 exactly" as a declared reading in R13. If the panel will not choose, list the translation under QUESTIONS FOR ELIAS rather than writing "None", because no ruling settles it.

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: The reviewer quotes the block correctly, and the mechanics are right: E_XXII = 0 holds only if no LOO index pair sends any measure of V_XXII. But that is a deliberate, labelled default, and the block already handles the contestable reading. It is not a fidelity defect. (1) The default is the literal, as-worded translation, and it says so. USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1226-1227 reads: "S2, NEVER SENDS (R-US17's second STOP, as worded: 'a party-bloc Congress under Rule XXII never sends a vetoable bill' ...)". R-US17's own words (l.721) say "never sends", and so does US-10's done-when (l.289): "a bloc Congress that sends none". Probability zero is the exact meaning of "never" in a probabilistic model. No ruling defines a softer threshold. G5 covers only "far more" (l.287), and H1-H3 (l.726-728) say nothing on it. So "= 0" is the reading most faithful to the ruled text, not a departure from it. (2) The alternatives the reviewer proposes are already in the block as declared readings: "R13: S2 at P(NONE_XXII) >= 1/2; S2 at E_XXII[vetoable sent] < 1; S2 over every kind" (l.1381). Each reading runs the whole rule, and its outcome and headline figures (E_XXII, P(NONE_XXII)) are printed (l.1212-1213, 1355-1356). A reading whose outcome differs is named in the generated verdict line (l.1245-1246, 1257-1258). This is the form the governing instructions prescribe for a contestable choice. G1 (l.2016 area, "run on the defaults as printed ... a choice changed after a result is in sight needs a new digest and a report to Elias"). The brief asks for a default plus a declared alternative, not a question to Elias. So "QUESTIONS FOR ELIAS: None" (l.1410-1412) is consistent with the rulings. Spec-risk 4 (l.2138) also says "The sweep is reported, not tuned." (3) The reviewer's failure scenario does not end in a silent mis-decision. An INSTALL is "held INSTALLED-PENDING-E4: the rates go to Elias before US-21 wires them (E4)" (l.1258-1259). Whatever the outcome, the full document goes to Elias (l.1262). The verdict line prints E_XXII beside "all <|V_XXII|> were sent on the record" and P(none under Rule XXII) (l.1249-1252, sending calibration l.1177-1179). So with E_XXII = 0.05 the line would show 0.05 sent against all sent on the record, about 95 % P(none), and "the outcome differs under R13: S2". All of this reaches Elias before anything goes live. (4) The proposed fix only swaps one unruled threshold (1/2, or < 1) for another. It is no better grounded in any ruling than the literal "never", so it is a design preference, not a correction. The reviewer's structural point has some merit as advice: the literal S2 is weak, because the fit is built from measures that passed. But that bias is itself declared ("SELECTION ON PASSAGE, DECLARED PREMISE", l.900-904; R6). The rule is computable, faithful to the words, and flags the alternative readings in its verdict line. It is not wrong, unfaithful, or mis-deciding in the high-severity sense.
- REFUTED: The quote is accurate (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1231), and the arithmetic point is correct: an exact zero fails as soon as one index pair sends one measure of V_XXII. That is still not a defect of the rule. It is a contested default, and the block already handles it the way the brief requires. (1) The default follows the ruling's own words. R-US17 (l.721) STOPS "if a party-bloc Congress under Rule XXII never sends a vetoable bill". US-10's done-when (l.~300) says "a bloc Congress that sends none". Spec-risk 4 (l.2138) says "a veto that may never fire ... The US risk is zero vetoes". The block labels S2 "as worded" (l.1226-1227). In an exact enumeration, "never" and "none" literally mean probability 0, so the reading is faithful to the words. (2) The defect itself concedes that "No ruling settles this translation". So a threshold of P(NONE_XXII) >= 1/2 or E_XXII < 1 would be just as much an unruled panel choice. It cannot be the "faithful" one while exact zero is "unfaithful". The brief (facts.md l.63-65) tells the panel to give every choice the rulings cannot settle a DEFAULT and, where contestable, a declared reading, and to keep questions to Elias for what is genuinely unsettleable. G1 says "run on the defaults as printed". So writing "None" under QUESTIONS (l.1410-1412) follows the instruction. (3) The proposed fix is already in the block: - R13 (l.1381) declares both forms: "S2 at P(NONE_XXII) >= 1/2; S2 at E_XXII[vetoable sent] < 1". - A reading whose outcome differs is named in the verdict line (l.1245-1246, l.1257-1258: "the outcome differs under <readings>: <outcomes>"). - The INSTALL verdict line prints E_XXII and "P(none under Rule XXII)" with their FITTED labels, beside "all <|V_XXII|> were sent on the record" (l.1249-1252). - INSTALL is held as INSTALLED-PENDING-E4: "the rates go to Elias before US-21 wires them", and the full document goes to Elias whatever the outcome (l.1258-1262). So in the failure scenario (E_XXII = 0.05), the verdict line would itself show about 0.05 sent, P(none) near 95 % and "outcome differs under R13: S2", and nothing reaches play first. That is not a hidden mis-decision. (4) The rule is neither ambiguous nor uncomputable: "= 0 exactly" on rationals (l.843-844) leaves two implementers nothing to resolve differently. The S1 aside does not hold up either. S1 is pooled over every kind (l.1232-1235), expedited measures included, so its E[named] is not tied to the Rule XXII sending figure. The fix only swaps the default for a reading the block already declares and surfaces. That is a preference among defaults, not a real defect.
- REFUTED: The quote is accurate (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md l.1231), but this is a disagreement about a default, not a defect in the rule. (1) The block is faithful to the ruling's word. R-US17 (l.721) says a bloc Congress "never sends a vetoable bill". Spec-risk 4 (l.2138), which the claim cites as the STOP's purpose, is headed "Gridlock by construction, and a veto that may never fire" and calls the risk "zero vetoes". That describes a model that cannot produce a veto at all. Under an exact enumeration, "never" read literally is E_XXII = 0, which is the same as P(NONE_XXII) = 1. That test still catches the structural case the STOP was written for: no observation's share, after the cap floor((S-1)/2) (l.864), carries the opposing party (OP) over the cloture line floor((3W+4)/5) (l.963). The claim's thresholds, P(NONE) >= 1/2 or E < 1, are new cutoffs that no ruling contains. Choosing one would be a further departure from the ruling's wording, not a closer reading of it. G5 had Elias pick a threshold because "far more" is vague; "never" is not vague. (2) The block already handles the near-zero case the claim describes: - Both proposed forms are declared reading R13 (l.1381). - A reading whose outcome differs is named in the verdict line (l.1245-1246, l.1257-1258: "the outcome differs under <readings>: <outcomes>"). - The INSTALL line prints E_XXII and P(none under Rule XXII) next to "(all <|V_XXII|> were sent on the record)" (l.1249-1252). The sending calibration prints them too (l.1177-1179). - INSTALL is held as INSTALLED-PENDING-E4, and the full document goes to Elias before US-21 wires anything (l.1258-1259, l.1262). So in the claim's scenario (E_XXII = 0.05), the verdict line would not just say "the draw installs". Its readings clause would name R13 and its S2 outcome, and Elias sees the rates before play. The STOP's purpose is not lost. (3) "QUESTIONS FOR ELIAS: None" (l.1410) follows the brief's own process (facts.md l.63-65): send Elias only the questions the rulings cannot settle, and give every other choice a default plus a declared reading printed beside it. That is exactly what was done here. The side remark about S1 on a near-zero E[named] is S1 working as G5 ruled. It is not part of this defect.

### form-F1 (high; upheld 0 of 3)

**Quote.** - RULED (Elias's ruling, sNNN), with its option, the outcome it was made on and the
    digest. ... - A CHANGED OUTCOME, in either direction and from any state, fails until it is
    registered ASKED with the old and new outcomes and digests. Only Elias's ruling moves
    ASKED to RULED.

**Defect.** The ruling record has no state for a ruling that amends the block. Most of the buildable options change the rule: every S3 option (pool all four cells, R11, source more roll calls), S2's 'R1, reading (A), as play's premise' and 'source ... and run again', S1's '(c) with R12's drawn veto', and '(c) amended by name'. Each of them moves the block digest and reruns the instrument, so the outcome changes (S3 becomes INSTALL or S1, for example). RULED holds 'the outcome it was made on', so the rerun fails as A CHANGED OUTCOME and is pushed back to ASKED. Only another ruling from Elias can then move it to RULED. A ruled amendment can therefore never pass without a second ruling on the same question. Two careful implementers would also disagree on what to register after an amended run that gives INSTALL: INSTALLED-PENDING-E4, or ASKED.

**Evidence.** In the block's THE STOPS' OPTIONS, every option carries 'amend the rule as ruled', and 'A ruling must name a buildable option: (c) as measured, a stop's option, or (c) amended by name.' The R-US18 precedent handles this outside its block: 'After an amendment the instrument runs again and the ruling record is checked against each run's new outcome' (plan, the paragraph before R-US18's block). The R-US17 block has no such provision, and no paragraph before it carries one.

**Fix proposed.** Add to THE RULING RECORD: "A ruling that amends the block (a stop's option that changes the rule, or (c) amended by name) is installed with the amendment's digest, and the amended block's first run registers its outcome as a first run does: INSTALL is registered INSTALLED-PENDING-E4 (sNNN) and a stop is registered ASKED (sNNN). A ruling that names (c) as measured, or keeping the blocs, is registered RULED with the outcome it was made on." Also give the INSTALL-from-RULED path its mirror form.

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: This is the intended design, not a defect, and the text has only one reading. 1. What the block says (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1457-1469). RULED records "the outcome it was made on". "A CHANGED OUTCOME, in either direction and from any state, fails until it is registered ASKED... Only Elias's ruling moves ASKED to RULED." So when an amended rule's rerun gives a different outcome, the record goes to ASKED and Elias rules again. That is deliberate. The rerun is a new measurement Elias has not seen, so it is not "the same question". The plan's own principle says so at :138: "Nothing measured goes live unruled." 2. The reviewer's evidence misreads the R-US18 precedent. R-US18's sentence "After an amendment the instrument runs again and the ruling record is checked against each run's new outcome" (:1514) does not set up a fresh first-run registration. It points to R-US18's RULED clause, which says: "A changed outcome fails until R-US18 is asked again; it is never re-pinned" (block, ~:1700). R-US19 says the same: "A changed outcome, or a run the ruling was not made on, asks R-US19 again" (~:1975). The precedent's code enforces it in Assets/Editor/UsHouseCountCheck.cs:687: "RULED: the outcome {0} is the one the ruling was made on ({1}) - a changed outcome asks R-US18 again, never re-pinned". R-US17 mirrors the precedent exactly. 3. The path always finishes. If an amended run keeps the same outcome, "A MOVED DIGEST WITH THE SAME OUTCOME" is re-pinned with cause "amendment" (:1464-1465). If the outcome changes, it goes to ASKED. Elias then names "(c) as measured" or a stop's option, and the record becomes RULED. US-21's diagnostic accepts "RULED with a buildable option" (:1347-1348). The full document goes to Elias whatever the outcome (:1262), so the extra ruling simply comes with that E4 reply. Nothing becomes uncomputable or decides wrongly. 4. Two implementers would not disagree. INSTALLED-PENDING-E4 is defined as "a first run that gives INSTALL" (:1453). The first run is the first-run commit (THE BARS, :1492-1497), and NONE holds only before the instrument exists (:1451). An amended rerun starts from an existing state (RULED), and "from any state" sends a changed outcome to ASKED. The proposed fix would replace this precedent-consistent conservatism with a weaker path. That is a policy change, not a correction.
- REFUTED: The claim is refuted. The behaviour it describes is real, but it is deliberate, it has only one reading, and the plan's two other preregistered rules work the same way. It is not a defect. 1. The text has only one reading. USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1453 limits INSTALLED-PENDING-E4 to "a first run that gives INSTALL". Lines 1461-1462 say "A CHANGED OUTCOME, in either direction and from any state, fails until it is registered ASKED with the old and new outcomes and digests." Suppose an amended run gives INSTALL after a RULED stop. It is not a first run, and its outcome differs from "the outcome it was made on" (line 1457). So it is registered ASKED. Two honest implementers would not split between INSTALLED-PENDING-E4 and ASKED, so the "medium ambiguity" part fails. 2. Nothing gets stuck. ASKED with the amended outcome moves to RULED on Elias's next ruling (line 1463). For an INSTALL outcome that ruling can be "(c) as measured", which line 1468 allows. US-21's diagnostic accepts "RULED with a buildable option, at the current block and catalog digests" (lines 1347-1348). An amended rule can therefore always pass. The rates of a rule Elias has not yet seen go back to him, which matches "Whatever the outcome, the full document goes to Elias (E4)" (line 1262). He is not asked "the same question" twice: the amended rule's outcome is new information he did not have when he ruled. 3. The cited precedent says the opposite of what the reviewer claims. R-US18's sentence "After an amendment the instrument runs again and the ruling record is checked against each run's new outcome" (line 1514) only says the outcome is checked. R-US18's own RULED clause then says: "A changed outcome fails until R-US18 is asked again; it is never re-pinned" (lines 1707-1708). R-US19 says the same: "A changed outcome, or a run the ruling was not made on, asks R-US19 again" (lines 1967-1968). Neither has a path from a ruled amendment straight to an installed state. R-US17's lines 1461-1463 copy that design, so the proposed fix would make R-US17 depart from both precedents. 4. Re-running after an amendment is already covered. The block must equal the last RuleDigests entry, which names Elias's ruling (lines 1424-1427). UsVetoDrawCheck runs on the cheap bar (line 1493). The record is then checked against the new outcome and digest (lines 1459-1466).
- REFUTED: The block already says what happens to an amending ruling, and it follows the established precedent. The claimed ambiguity does not exist. (1) There is no gap. A ruling moves ASKED to RULED, recorded "with its option, the outcome it was made on and the digest" (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1457-1458). The amendment adds an entry to RuleDigests ("AMENDED (Hn, sNNN): old -> new", 1424-1427). When the amended block runs again, one of two things happens: - The outcome is the same: "A MOVED DIGEST WITH THE SAME OUTCOME fails until a commit re-pins it, naming ... its cause (source, code or amendment)" (1464-1465). "Amendment" is named there explicitly as a cause. - The outcome changes: "A CHANGED OUTCOME, in either direction and from any state, fails until it is registered ASKED with the old and new outcomes and digests" (1461-1463). Every case resolves deterministically. (2) There is no ambiguity between INSTALLED-PENDING-E4 and ASKED. INSTALLED-PENDING-E4 is defined only as "a first run that gives INSTALL" (1453). The CHANGED OUTCOME clause covers "either direction and from any state", so going from RULED (made on S3) to INSTALL is registered ASKED. Two honest implementers reading this would not differ. (3) The precedent the reviewer cites works the same way, so it does not support the claim. R-US18's sentence "After an amendment the instrument runs again and the ruling record is checked against each run's new outcome" (1514) creates no new state. It says the ruling's recorded outcome is compared with the new run. R-US18's block then says: "A changed outcome fails until R-US18 is asked again; it is never re-pinned" (1707-1708). R-US19's says: "A changed outcome ... asks R-US19 again" (1967). Both blocks carry the same "amend the rule as ruled" options (1671, 1932). The code does the same: UsHouseCountCheck.cs:687 reads "RULED: the outcome {0} is the one the ruling was made on ... a changed outcome asks R-US18 again, never re-pinned". R-US17 copies that structure; it does not depart from it. (4) A second ruling after an amendment changes the outcome is deliberate and conservative, not a mis-decision. It fits G1's principle (plan:2009): "a choice changed after a result is in sight needs a new digest and a report to Elias". It also fits E4's requirement that the full document go to Elias whatever the outcome (1262). Elias rules the amendment before he has seen its new figures, so asking him again on them is the preregistration discipline at work. The proposed fix would let an amended INSTALL bypass a ruling, which would weaken that discipline and break with the R-US18/R-US19 precedent. The claim is not a real defect.

### game-F2 (high; upheld 0 of 3)

**Fix proposed.** Fix the baseline: 'at the first-run commit, against its parent; at US-21, against US-21's parent, which carries US-20's changes'. Write Poland's proof as behaviour, not bytes, from US-20 on: PresidentialVetoDiagnostic's cases (D4, E2) and GovernmentBudgetBillDiagnostic's pins as US-20 re-pins them pass unmoved, and Generated/PolishVetoRates.cs and VETO_B1_BACKTEST.md stay byte-identical. ### inference-S2-knife-edge (high; upheld 0 of 3)

**Quote.**   - case "never sends": E_XXII[vetoable sent] = 0 exactly (that is, P(NONE_XXII) = 1).

**Defect.** As defaulted, S2 can essentially never fire, so R-US17's own US-risk STOP ("the mirror of E4's 43 of 43") is gone in practice. P(sent_j) is a ratio of passing index pairs over the LOO lists. E_XXII is exactly 0 only if, for every measure of V_XXII, no pair of observations in the PP and OP lists (j's own roll calls removed) gets past cloture and the House. One Senate PP observation with a share large enough to reach floor((3W+4)/5), owned by any other measure, makes E_XXII > 0. So E_XXII = 0.05 of the |V_XXII| bills the record sent would INSTALL, even though that model is degenerate in the way the stop exists to catch. The block's own premise makes this worse: every observation comes from a passed measure ("Sending is estimated with a bias toward sending"), so the cells hold, by construction, the high shares that carried bills through.

**Evidence.** S2 is defined at lines 1226-1231. The meaningful thresholds sit only in R13 (line 1381: "S2 at P(NONE_XXII) >= 1/2; S2 at E_XXII[vetoable sent] < 1"), and readings "never stop the rule" (line 1246). Selection premise: lines 900-904. s802 read "never sends" as a deterministic count of 0 of 34 (us_veto_backtest.pl line 231, `if $oneStance == 0`). With a probabilistic sender, the probability-zero version of that test is a knife edge that one observation decides.

**Fix proposed.** Make S2's default case a non-degenerate probabilistic "never", fixed now. For example, "never sends": P(NONE_XXII) >= 1/2, meaning a bloc Congress with the draw more likely than not sends none of the record's Rule XXII vetoable measures. Or E_XXII[vetoable sent] < 1. Compare it exactly on rationals as now. Move "E_XXII = 0 exactly" into R13 as the literal reading, so it is printed and named in the verdict line where its outcome differs.

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: The quote is accurate (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1231), and the arithmetic is correct: P(sent_j) is a ratio of index pairs (1119-1121), so one high-share observation that also clears the House makes E_XXII > 0. That is still not a defect of the rule, for four reasons. (1) The default is the ruling's own wording. R-US17's STOP fires "if a party-bloc Congress under Rule XXII never sends a vetoable bill" (plan:721). The block's S2 adopts that wording "as worded" (1226-1228) and makes "never" exact: E_XXII = 0, which is the same as P(NONE_XXII) = 1. The ruling's own analogy points to that same reading. "The mirror of E4's 43 of 43" (721) refers to E4's veto on "all 43 of 43 statutes" (plan:55), an all-or-nothing extreme, so its mirror is "none sent", not "few sent". s802 used the same literal test (us_veto_backtest.pl:231, `if $oneStance == 0`). The fix reads "never" as "more likely than not sends none" (P(NONE_XXII) >= 1/2) or "fewer than one expected" (E_XXII < 1). Those are new stop conditions the ruling does not contain. Making either one the default would be the less faithful choice and would need Elias's ruling. (2) The block already covers this. Both thresholds the fix proposes are preregistered word for word as declared reading R13 (1381): "S2 at P(NONE_XXII) >= 1/2; S2 at E_XXII[vetoable sent] < 1". Every reading's outcome is printed, and "a reading whose outcome differs is named in the verdict line" (1245-1246, 1256-1258). This is exactly what the brief asks for: a default plus a declared alternative printed beside it where the choice is contestable (facts.md:63-65). The proposal only swaps the default with the reading. That is a preference, not an error. (3) A low E_XXII cannot install unseen. The SENDING CALIBRATION prints E_XXII beside "|V_XXII| - all sent on the record" (1177-1179). The verdict line states E_XXII and P(NONE_XXII) with their intervals (1249-1252). INSTALL is only INSTALLED-PENDING-E4, with "the rates go to Elias before US-21 wires them" (1258-1259). The full document goes to Elias whatever the outcome (1262). The selection bias the reviewer raises is declared (900-904), and reading R6 prints the fit without it (1368-1370). (4) The context of H1 matters. Elias ruled H1, adding the draw, as his answer to this exact STOP at s802 (723-726). A default that re-asks whenever sending is merely unlikely would put the question he already settled back to him. That cuts against G1 and against his instruction to keep questions to what the rulings cannot settle (facts.md:60-64). S2 also keeps its teeth where the ruling meant it to: when the draw cannot make sending possible at all. The planted S2 runs in the mutation suite cover that case (1487). None of the high-severity tests holds: the rule is not wrong, not unfaithful to a ruling, and does not mis-decide.
- REFUTED: This is a contestable default that the block already handles. It is not a defect of the rule. (1) The default follows the ruling's own words. R-US17's STOP is "if a party-bloc Congress under Rule XXII never sends a vetoable bill" (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:721). With a probabilistic sender, the literal "never" is probability zero, which is what line 1231 says. A 1/2 or "< 1" threshold would be a number Elias never ruled. Contrast "far more", where S1 rests on Elias's own reading G5, and even that is marked PROVISIONAL (plan:1232, brief facts.md:61-62). (2) Both of the reviewer's proposed thresholds are already in the block. R13 (plan:1381) reads "S2 at P(NONE_XXII) >= 1/2; S2 at E_XXII[vetoable sent] < 1". Every reading runs the whole rule and prints its outcome (1355-1356, 1212-1213). "A reading whose outcome differs is named in the verdict line" (1245-1246). The verdict line itself prints "<the outcome differs under <readings>: <outcomes>>" (1257-1258). So in the reviewer's E_XXII = 0.05 case, the INSTALL line would read "the outcome differs under R13: S2". The block's stated policy (1410-1412) is to keep a default and print a declared reading beside it where the choice is contestable. That matches what Elias told the panel to do (facts.md:63-65). (3) The worry that one observation decides the outcome is covered by INFLUENCE (1208-1211). It lists every roll call whose removal changes the outcome, and the verdict line carries "FRAGILE: <k> roll calls each flip the outcome" (1256-1257). (4) An INSTALL is never silent or final. Its verdict line prints E_XXII beside "(all <|V_XXII|> were sent on the record)" and P(none under Rule XXII) (1249-1252). The SENDING CALIBRATION prints the same comparison (1177-1179). The install is "held INSTALLED-PENDING-E4: the rates go to Elias before US-21 wires them" (1258-1259), and "Whatever the outcome, the full document goes to Elias (E4)" (1262). So the US-risk check is still in place: a near-degenerate sender reaches Elias, named and labelled, before anything is wired. The quoted text matches the block (plan:1231), and the s802 citation is accurate (us_veto_backtest.pl:231, `if $oneStance == 0`). The issue is only which reading should be the default, and the block already settles that by printing the alternatives.
- REFUTED: Refuted. The block already handles this, and the default is the faithful reading of the ruling, not a defect. (1) Faithful to the ruling. R-US17's STOP says "a party-bloc Congress under Rule XXII never sends a vetoable bill" (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:721). It is called "the mirror of E4's 43 of 43", and that is a literal extreme too: a veto on "all 43 of 43 statutes" (COMPLETED.md:37040). s802 implemented the STOP as an exact zero: `... if $oneStance == 0` (Tools/us_veto_backtest.pl:231, which the reviewer cites). With a probabilistic sender, the exact equivalent of "count = 0" is "E_XXII = 0 exactly, i.e. P(NONE_XXII) = 1" (block line 1231). So the block carries s802's own test over unchanged. The proposed fix (P(NONE_XXII) >= 1/2, or E_XXII < 1) would put a threshold Elias never ruled in place of his word "never". G1 ("run on the defaults as printed") and the brief (facts.md:63-65) send such contestable thresholds to a declared reading, not to the default. H1 also expects the draw to matter rarely: "Parties stay blocs by default ... the draw matters only where the chamber's margin is within the drawn count" (plan:726). A default that stops (c) because it sends less than one expected measure would stop the rule for behaving as ruled. (2) Already handled, in four ways: - **The proposed thresholds are already readings.** R13 already runs exactly them: "R13: S2 at P(NONE_XXII) >= 1/2; S2 at E_XXII[vetoable sent] < 1" (line 1381). "A reading whose outcome differs is named in the verdict line" (lines 1245-1246, 1257-1258). - **The knife-edge is printed.** The worry that one observation decides is exactly what INFLUENCE measures (lines 1208-1211). If one roll call's observation is all that lifts E_XXII above 0, removing it flips the outcome to S2, and the verdict line prints "FRAGILE: <k> roll calls each flip the outcome" (lines 1256-1257). - **A near-zero E_XXII is shown next to the record.** The INSTALL verdict line prints E_XXII with "(all <|V_XXII|> were sent on the record)" and P(none under Rule XXII) (lines 1250-1252), and the SENDING CALIBRATION sits beside the totals (lines 1177-1179). - **INSTALL does not go live.** It is "held INSTALLED-PENDING-E4: the rates go to Elias before US-21 wires them" (lines 1258-1259, 1262). So a near-degenerate draw cannot reach play without Elias seeing it, and it is not a mis-decision. (3) The claim that S2 "can essentially never fire" is not shown by the rule's structure. The PP held the Senate in every Congress (facts.md:128). Each Senate send therefore needs Kp - Ko to cover the gap from the OP's seats up to floor((3W+4)/5) = 60, against a cap of floor((S-1)/2) (lines 864, 963). Whether any leave-one-out pair covers that gap depends on the data. Asserting that it always will would need the record's figures, which the blindness rule forbids. The S2 path is also computable and planted in the mutation suite: "S2 (both cases)" (line 1487).

### computability-C2 (medium; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.** 10*d >= m; the mean share.

**Defect.** "The mean share" is not defined. One honest implementer takes the unweighted mean of d/m over the cell's observations. Another takes the pooled ratio sum d / sum m, which is the rate R8 uses ("sum d / sum m"). The two differ for every cell with unequal m, and so do its BOOT interval and its label.

**Evidence.** THE CELL FIGURES lists q_any, q10 and "the mean share" with no formula. R8 separately uses "the pooled rate" sum d / sum m. THE PRINT gives the mean share a BOOT label.

**Fix proposed.** State the formula: "the mean share = (1/n_drawn) * sum over the drawn list of d/m (each roll call weighted equally). In a bootstrap replicate it is the same mean over the replicate's list with multiplicity, and an empty replicate list is UNDEFINED."

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: The quote is accurate (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:938-939), but the block already pins down what it means, and the second reading conflicts with its own words. (1) "Share" is defined per observation: line 912 says "every share d/m <= 1/2", line 919 sorts by "ascending share (compare d1*m2 with d2*m1)", and lines 865-866 call d/m "the recorded crossing share". So "the mean share" of a cell is the mean of its observations' shares d/m over L. (2) The roll call is the unit and is weighted equally. Lines 916-918 say "n of a cell = |L| ... That one n is used everywhere: N_MIN, the union, the draw index, CP and the label. Members are never the unit." Lines 924-925 say "each figure is a share of n roll calls". Sum d / sum m weights each roll call by m (members), which "Members are never the unit" excludes, and the mean share's label prints n=<n> observations (line 1081). (3) The block gives the pooled ratio its own name. It calls sum d / sum m "the pooled rate" (line 925: "R8 prints the binomial at the pooled rate") and uses it only in declared reading R8 (line 1373). So "mean share" and "pooled rate" are two separately named figures, not one undefined term. (4) The fix's bootstrap clauses are also already covered. Replicate cells hold observations "with the picks' multiplicity" (lines 1068-1069). An empty replicate list gives a zero denominator, which is "UNDEFINED" with the k count (lines 1075-1078). The n := 1 empty-list convention is limited to "Crossers, the enumeration and the realizations" (lines 868-870), not to cell figures. At most, writing the formula out would be a low-severity clarity edit. It is not a medium ambiguity on which two honest implementers would differ.

### computability-C3 (medium; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.** S1 and S2 are not evaluated, and their figures print UNFITTED

**Defect.** The decisive digest is uncomputable on the S3 path, and its line grammar is underspecified. Under S3 the per-measure and total figures are UNFITTED, yet the digest grammar requires "p_sent.<congress>|<measure>=<p/q>", "p_named...", "e_sent=..." through "p_none_xxii=..." as rationals. An ASKED record still needs that digest, and the mutation suite plants S3. "cell.<XX>.obs.<i>=... (i 0-based, drawn list, fit order)" has no defined drawn list for an UNFITTED cell. The text also does not say whether the cell lines are grouped per cell (HP's status, pooled, n and obs, then HO's) or per key (all four statuses first). It does not say whose <congress>|<measure> an obs line carries, though presumably the OWNER's. Two implementers would produce different digests.

**Evidence.** THE DECISION, S3: "When S3 fires, S1 and S2 are not evaluated, and their figures print UNFITTED." THE DECISIVE DIGEST lists the cell keys with "(XX in HP, HO, SP, SO, in that order)" on the status line only, followed by p_sent/p_named/e_* lines with no S3 exception. Mutation suite: "planted runs proving the UNFITTED, S3, S2 (both cases) and S1 paths".

**Fix proposed.** Add three things. (1) "Under S3 the p_sent, p_named and e_*/p_none lines are omitted. The digest is source, the cell lines, unpredictable, counted, v_count, v, v_xxii and outcome." (2) "An UNFITTED cell's obs lines list its union, or its own list when it is not pooled, in fit order." (3) "The cell lines are grouped per cell, in the order HP, HO, SP, SO: status, pooled, n, then obs.<i>. <congress>|<measure> is the roll call's OWNER."

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: The main claim, that the digest cannot be computed on the S3 path, is false. The rule says what the figures print. It does not say they lack values. 1. An UNFITTED cell still draws, and its drawn list is defined (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:929-933). A cell with n < 12 is POOLED and "draws from the union, sorted in FIT ORDER". "A union with n < 12 leaves the cell UNFITTED". A cell can only be UNFITTED when both partners are below 12, and then "both draw from the union" (931-932). The no-draw list in THE MODEL item 2 (857-860) does not include UNFITTED cells. The catalog keeps "Fitted, PooledWith and the EXACT LIST THE CELL DRAWS FROM: already merged for a pooled cell, in fit order" for each of the four cells, whatever its status (1331-1334). So claim (2), that an UNFITTED cell has no drawn list, is false. Its list is the union. 2. Under S3 the decisive rationals can always be computed. P(pass) counts the index pairs over the LOO lists that pass, divided by n_PP * n_OP. "A party with no draw, or an empty list, is the single index 'K = 0' (n := 1)" (1119-1121). This holds even if a union were empty. The thin-cell status is "held fixed in every LOO list ..." (927-928), so p_sent, p_named and the e_*/p_none totals are defined rationals in every state. 3. "Prints UNFITTED" governs THE PRINT, not the digest (1089-1091: "every figure that reads it prints 'UNFITTED', with no value"). The block already separates the printed label from the value in the same way elsewhere. When V_XXII is empty, the figures "print 'UNFITTED m=0'" (1230), yet e_sent_xxii (an empty sum, 0/1) and p_none_xxii (an empty product, 1/1) are still well-defined "p/q" values for the digest (1445, 1448). The digest grammar (1430-1449) takes every listed line with no S3 exception, and it can be filled on that path. The ASKED record and the S3 mutation case are therefore not blocked. The proposed fix (1) would change the rule rather than repair it. 4. The leftover points do not amount to a medium defect:    - The obs line's <congress>|<measure> is the OWNER's. Observations are per distinct roll call keyed to its OWNER (905). THE PRINT gives "(identity, owner, d, m)" (1170), and the catalog stores OwnerIndex (1333).    - Grouping per cell versus per key is a possible formatting ambiguity, but it cannot mis-decide or fail anything. The decisive digest is not preregistered. The instrument's own first run registers it (1453-1457), and a later change of line order is handled as a code-caused re-pin (1464-1466). Since only one implementation ever computes it, no two implementers' digests are ever compared. At most that is a low-severity clarity note, not the claimed medium defect.

### computability-C4 (medium; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.** instrument's exact P(sent) and P(named) as the same rationals

**Defect.** US-21's diagnostic is told to match the runtime's full-cell enumeration against "the instrument's exact P(sent) and P(named) ... (on the full cells)". The instrument's per-measure figures are all LOO ("Per measure j of V, under (B), exact and LOO"). Full-cell figures exist only as reading R5, which prints headline figures, not per-measure rationals. LOO "stays instrument-side", so the runtime cannot reproduce the LOO values. The comparison therefore points at numbers the instrument never produces.

**Evidence.** THE FIRST RUN: "Per measure j of V, under (B), exact and LOO". THE FIT: "Play and the catalog use the full cells. In-sample is R5." DECLARED READINGS print "outcome and headline figures" only. HOW THE FIGURES REACH THE GAME: "LOO stays instrument-side."

**Fix proposed.** Require the instrument to compute and print (in US_VETO_DRAW.md, or in the catalog) the full-cell, in-sample P(sent_j) and P(named_j) as reduced rationals for every counted veto in V. Then point US-21's diagnostic at that list by name.

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: The claim's central premise, that the comparison "points at numbers the instrument never produces", is false. The quote is accurate (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1351-1352), and so are the four evidence quotes (lines 1151, 936-937, 1335-1336 and 1356). They do not add up to the defect, for four reasons. (1) The instrument does compute these numbers. Every declared reading "runs the whole rule with one change" (line 1356), and R5 is "in-sample, no LOO" (line 1367; THE FIT says "In-sample is R5", line 937). So the R5 run computes P(sent_j) and P(named_j) on the full cells, as exact reduced rationals, for every measure of V. That covers every counted veto in V. The instrument computes them through the runtime's own UsVeto.PassProbability (lines 1140-1142, 1119-1123). Only the PRINT is limited to headline figures (line 1212). (2) The parenthetical "(on the full cells)" at line 1352 already picks those figures over the LOO ones. Those are the same full cells the catalog ships (line 936: "Play and the catalog use the full cells"; lines 1331-1334: the catalog holds the exact merged lists). No honest reader would compare against the printed LOO values. (3) US-21's diagnostic is an in-Editor assertion, not a reading of the document. It can get the target by calling the instrument's R5 path, or UsVeto.PassProbability, on the catalog's lists. The cheap bar already regenerates the catalog in memory the same way (line 1337). Both routes run the same arithmetic and give the same rationals, so two implementers cannot reach different verdicts. Nothing needs printing in US_VETO_DRAW.md for the assertion to be computable. (4) The LOO point is beside the target. "LOO stays instrument-side" (lines 1335-1336) only stops the runtime from building LOO lists. The diagnostic is told to compare on full cells, so the runtime never has to reproduce LOO values. At most, "the instrument's" could name the R5 run explicitly, for example "(the instrument's R5 enumeration, on the full cells)". That is low-severity wording, not a medium false reference, and nothing would be mis-decided or uncomputable.

### consistency-IC-8 (medium; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.** Crossers count only where they entered under item 6, and no fresh draw is made.

**Defect.** THE MODEL item 6 is scoped "IN PLAY" ("the drawn crossers enter a chamber's recorded division only where they change its outcome ... Everywhere else the division is the blocs', byte for byte"). THE OVERRIDE's SIDES reuses that rule for (B) and (A) on the record, which item 6 does not cover, so the backtest's omega_j is ambiguous: does the override division under (B) include Kp/Ko on every pair, or only on pairs where the draw mattered? THE VETO also contradicts item 6. It sets "The nays are S_PP,H - Kp where the PP stands NAY", while item 6 leaves the division at the blocs' counts (nays = S_PP,H) wherever the draw did not matter. (a)'s outcome is the same either way, but the 'chamber's division' that (a) reads is defined two ways.

**Evidence.** THE MODEL item 6; THE OVERRIDE 'SIDES' and '(B): Y = the YEA side of that division over the SEATS'; THE VETO '(a) fires iff ... 2 * (the PP's House nays in the chamber's division)' and 'The nays are S_PP,H - Kp'.

**Fix proposed.** Restate item 6 for every premise: "In play, under (B) and under (A), the drawn crossers enter the chamber's division only where THE DRAW MATTERS; otherwise the division is the blocs'". Change THE VETO to "The nays are S_PP,H - Kp where the PP stands NAY and its crossers entered (item 6), S_PP,H where it stands NAY and they did not, and 0 otherwise".

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: Refuted. The block already settles the first half, and the second half does not change any computed figure. (1) omega_j under (B) is not ambiguous. THE OVERRIDE's SIDES (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1018-1027) says "Crossers count only where they entered under item 6". The criterion it imports is item 6's "only where they change its outcome (WHEN THE DRAW MATTERS)" (:876-878). SIDES then applies it explicitly "Per premise", listing (B) and (A). THE DRAW MATTERS is defined for every premise and chamber, not for play alone: "THE DRAW MATTERS iff the outcome at the drawn (Kp, Ko) differs from the outcome at (0, 0)" (:975-976). The default's alternative is also named twice. At :1039-1040, R15 prints "(a) the passage's full drawn crossers applied to the override", and at :1384-1385, R15 reads "(a) the passage's full drawn crossers, whether or not they entered the passage". So the default counts crossers on a (B) pair only where the draw mattered, and the blocs' division otherwise. "Crossers on every pair" is R15(a), a declared reading, not a second honest reading of the default. A third reading, "item 6 is play-only so no crosser ever enters on the record", would make SIDES' (B) and (A) bullets vacuous and R15(a)'s contrast pointless. Also, omega_j is "PRINTED ONLY, never deciding" (:1032-1034) and is excluded from the decisive digest ("no ... override", :1449). So even a divergence could not mis-decide the rule. (2) THE VETO's "The nays are S_PP,H - Kp where the PP stands NAY" (:999) has no consequence. The cap K <= floor((S-1)/2) (:864) gives 2*(S - Kp) >= S + 1 > S. So (a) fires whether the nays are read as S - Kp or as S. The block asserts exactly this in every run, reading and replicate: "under the cap, (a) fires exactly where the PP's House stance is NAY and S_PP,H >= 1. The draw decides whether a bill REACHES the president, never whether (a) fires" (:1001-1005; again at :1204). The reviewer concedes "(a)'s outcome is the same either way". So the two implementers the medium bar needs, ones who would get different results, do not exist. At most this is a cosmetic wording point (low), not the medium defect claimed. The proposed rewrite of THE VETO would change no figure.

### facts-F5 (medium; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.** - BUDGET VOTE (play): every act the US budget procedure tables (WorldClock.BudgetProcedureOf,
  US-22: the appropriation, a continuing resolution, the pension-age act)

**Defect.** This points to a procedure that will not exist when the rule reaches play. Today WorldClock.BudgetProcedureOf returns BudgetProcedure.Unsourced for the USA, and the government's bill is voted alone. US-22 adds the US procedure, but US-22 needs US-21, which is the item that wires this rule. At US-21 there is no appropriation, continuing resolution or pension-age act, only the Unsourced government budget bill. The definition does not say whether that bill is a BUDGET VOTE. Two implementers could disagree, and the answer decides whether a draw can touch a US budget at US-21 (the block's own planted budget-division proof is 'installed at US-21').

**Evidence.** Assets/Scripts/Elections/WorldClock.cs:353-359 ("until it is sourced the government's bill is voted alone"; the USA maps to Unsourced); docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:452 (US-22 *Needs:* US-14, US-18, US-20, US-21).

**Fix proposed.** Add: "Before US-22, the US government budget bill tabled under BudgetProcedure.Unsourced is a BUDGET VOTE; from US-22, every act the US procedure tables (the appropriation, a continuing resolution, the pension-age act)."

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: The facts the reviewer cites are right. WorldClock.cs:356-359 maps the USA to BudgetProcedure.Unsourced, and the docstring at :353-354 says "until it is sourced the government's bill is voted alone". USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:452 shows US-22 needs US-21, so at US-21 the only US budget act is the Unsourced government bill. But the block already decides that this bill is a BUDGET VOTE, so it is not an ambiguity two honest implementers would settle differently. (1) Read literally, the definition at plan:831 ("every act the US budget procedure tables (WorldClock.BudgetProcedureOf, ...)") covers it. The code names that bill's procedure through exactly that function: SimulationManager.TableGovernmentBudget logs "the chamber decides ... by {WorldClock.BudgetProcedureOf(country.Id)}" (SimulationManager.cs:1185), and ResolveGovernmentBudget branches on it (:1217). Under Unsourced, the US budget procedure tables the government's bill and votes it alone. The text after "US-22:" lists what US-22 adds; it does not limit the definition. (2) Elsewhere the block settles the US-21 case outright. Lines 1285-1287 read: "US-21's diagnostic plants j = n - 1 (the largest share): every US budget division is identical to the unplanted run, and the counter reads 0. This is installed at US-21 and re-run at US-22." At US-21 the only US budget division is this Unsourced bill's, so an implementer who let it draw would fail the diagnostic the block installs at US-21. Line 1282 ("UsVeto refuses every budget vote") and line 1284 ("a contested budget act at margin 1 draws zero") point the same way. (3) H1, quoted just above the block at plan:726, says "Budget votes ... stay as they are", and THE VETO at plan:1008-1009 withholds the draw from budget acts. Read together, these leave one possible reading: the Unsourced US government budget bill draws nothing at US-21. The proposed sentence would only restate what the block already enforces.

### fidelity-F2 (medium; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.** S2, NEVER SENDS (R-US17's second STOP, as worded: "a party-bloc Congress under Rule XXII

**Defect.** S2 is evaluated on V_XXII (Rule XXII measures only). That is not how the same STOP was run at s802, which is the form that fired and that Elias ruled H1 on. s802 tested every measure the PP opposed in a chamber (the 34 F3 measures), treating expedited kinds by majority and the rest by cloture. The block narrows the base to V_XXII, calls it "as worded", and moves s802's form to R13 ("S2 over every kind") without saying it departs from the evaluated STOP.

**Evidence.** Tools/us_veto_backtest.pl l.194-202 and l.231: the loop is over all rows with opposed_f3 in either chamber, the Senate test is `$EXPEDITED{$x->{kind}} ? 2 * $s->{$other} > $s->{sworn} : ... three fifths`, and the stop text reads "a party-bloc Congress under Rule XXII never sends a vetoable measure ... (0 of %d opposed". The plan's STOPPED paragraph (l.723) says "Of the 34 measures the president's party opposed in a chamber, none reaches him".

**Fix proposed.** Add to S2: "DECLARED: s802 evaluated this STOP over every measure the PP opposed (F3's 34, expedited kinds by majority); this block narrows it to V_XXII on the words 'under Rule XXII'. s802's form is R13's 'S2 over every kind'." Alternatively, make s802's base the default and keep V_XXII as the reading. Either way, name the departure in the verdict line whenever the two outcomes differ.

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: The reviewer's facts check out: s802 did run the STOP over all 34 F3 measures, expedited kinds by majority (Tools/us_veto_backtest.pl:194-202, 231; COMPLETED.md §802, "The Senate paths... read for every kind a veto fell on"). But this is not a defect of the rule at medium severity. (1) The quote is accurate and the reference is true. USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1226-1228 cites R-US17's own words, "a party-bloc Congress under Rule XXII never sends a vetoable bill", and plan l.721 has exactly that text. "As worded" is literally correct, so this is no false reference. H1-H3 (l.725-728) do not define the STOP's base, so the block contradicts no ruling. The brief only requires that the two STOPs "survive in some form" (facts.md:95-96). (2) The fix's working part is already in the block. s802's form is a declared reading: R13, l.1381-1382, "S2 over every kind (V in place of V_XXII)". And l.1245-1246 already says "every declared reading and its outcome. A reading whose outcome differs is named in the verdict line". So whenever the two bases give different outcomes, the verdict line already names it. The all-kinds P(NONE) and E[vetoable sent] over V are also always printed (l.1156-1162, l.1243). (3) The narrowing cannot mis-decide in the direction that matters. V_XXII is a subset of V (l.828). If E over V is 0, every P(sent_j) on V is 0, so E_XXII is 0 too. If V is empty, V_XXII is empty. So the block's S2 fires whenever the s802-form S2 would fire: it never installs where s802's base would stop. It can only stop more often, which re-asks Elias with R13's differing outcome named. (4) The definition is unambiguous: two honest implementers would compute the same S2 (l.1229-1231). All that is left is an optional note that R13's "over every kind" was s802's base. That is at most low clarity, not medium. The block already prints that base, decides on the stricter one, and names any disagreement.

### fidelity-F4 (medium; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.** COUNTED ONLY: every count, sum, denominator, cell, rate and digest of this rule reads

**Defect.** This clause allows "The ONE exception" (the US-10 identities). The block itself requires other all-row or unpredictable-row counts: printed item 1's "THE RECORD per president: measures and vetoes, counted and in all.", the per-president row "0 counted vetoes; 2 declared unpredictable", the override's "their sustained votes print on their own line only", and the decisive digest's `unpredictable.<i>` lines (the clause names digests). An implementer who follows the clause literally cannot print "in all". One who prints it breaks the clause. On a rule whose H2 obligation is that H.R. 131 and H.R. 504 are "never counted for or against", two honest implementers would differ on where these two rows may appear.

**Evidence.** Block l.769-772 (the COUNTED ONLY clause) against l.1167 (item 1), l.1316-1317 (the per-president row), l.1315 (the sustained votes) and l.1438 (the digest's unpredictable lines).

**Fix proposed.** Rewrite as: "COUNTED ONLY: every rate, expected count, cell and denominator reads counted measures only. Exceptions, each printed as the record's count and entering no rate: THE US-10 IDENTITIES; THE RECORD's 'in all' column; the UNPREDICTABLE line and its per-president note; the two sustained override votes; the digest's unpredictable lines (by name)."

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: The quote is accurate, but the medium-severity claim does not hold up. Each place the reviewer cites is set out explicitly elsewhere in the block, so honest implementers would not differ on where H.R. 131 and H.R. 504 may appear. (1) The decisive digest is fully listed, and USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1438 names `unpredictable.<i>=<congress>|<measure>  (CSV order)`. :1319-1320 says "they enter the decisive digest by name only", and :1448-1449 says "Nothing else enters". An implementer builds the digest from that key list, so the general clause at :769 cannot remove those lines. (2) The sustained override votes are covered at :1315 ("their sustained votes print on their own line only"). The UNPREDICTABLE line is covered at :1321-1322, with its exact text. Item 1 (:1167) points to that same line ("The UNPREDICTABLE line"). (3) The "in all" column in item 1 (:1167) does not conflict with H2 or with :1316-1317.
