The word "strict" elsewhere in the block (line 996) is attached to "More than half" and is clearly about an inequality; the R13 pair "strict"/"lax" qualifies the stop, not an operator. At most this is a cosmetic preference, not an ambiguity that two honest implementers would resolve differently.

### consistency-IC-16 (low; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.** (each space-separated "C:n" entry with C in {R, D}; any other entry stays outside and

**Defect.** The extract writes '-' when a row has no Senate independent (us_veto_backtest.pl tests `senate_I_caucus ne '-'`). Read literally, '-' is an 'other entry' that 'stays outside and is printed', so item 9 would list nearly every row as an ignored caucus entry.

**Evidence.** us_veto_backtest.pl lines 110 and 187 (`$x->{senate_I_caucus} ne '-'`); THE FIRST RUN item 9 'every caucus entry ignored as neither R nor D'.

**Fix proposed.** Add: "A senate_I_caucus of '-' means no entry and is not printed."

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: The quote is accurate, but the harm it claims ("item 9 would list nearly every row") cannot happen, and the block already covers the '-' value by reference. (1) Where '-' comes from. Tools/us_veto_prep.pl writes '-' in senate_I_caucus in two cases. The main one is a row with no Senate roll call (line 411). The other is a Senate roll call that lists no independent at all (line 406, `|| '-'`). (2) The rule never reads senate_I_caucus on a row with no Senate roll call. USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:801-803 (SEATS step 2) adds the caucus entries only "for each chamber whose final is a roll call". :1024-1025 adds them only on the roll call under (A). :969-970 says a chamber with no roll call passes undrawn under (A), or is modelled on the SEATS under (B). :864-866 takes S from the SEATS under (B). So the "nearly every row" reading, which comes from the rows with no roll call, has no footing in the block. (3) On a Senate roll call, '-' cannot occur in the window. Prep's members list holds every senator on the roll call, Not Voting included (us_veto_prep.pl:287-291 checks this against the count). Sanders and King sat as independents through all of the 115th-119th Senates, so every Senate roll call lists at least one independent and gets a C:n entry. That is a fact about the Senate's membership, not a figure of the rule. (4) The block ties itself to the Perl that skips '-'. :745-747 reads the record "as Tools/us_veto_backtest.pl's read_csv reads it". :798 makes SEATS "literally us_veto_backtest.pl's %seatsOf", and that is exactly the code at line 187 that tests `ne '-'`. Line 110 does the same check, after line 105 has already returned on a chamber with no roll call. (5) Item 9 (:1189) only prints, so even an over-literal implementer could not change a decision. The proposed sentence is harmless, but it fixes no real defect.

### facts-F6 (low; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.** The debt-limit act is the panel's default
  (US-22 has the debt limit ride reconciliation; 641(a)(3)), with R18 printed.

**Defect.** Mis-attributed citation. US-22 cites 2 U.S.C. 641(a), (e) for the debt limit riding reconciliation. The 641(a)(3) citation, and the standalone 'raise or suspension, as a bill through both chambers ... and the veto', are US-24's text. As written, the parenthesis seems to justify a standalone debt-limit act with US-22's words.

**Evidence.** docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:448 (US-22: "ride reconciliation at simple majorities (2 U.S.C. 641(a), (e))"); :466 (US-24: "as a bill through both chambers (reconciliation may carry it, 641(a)(3)) and the veto").

**Fix proposed.** Write: "(US-22 has the debt limit ride reconciliation, 641(a), (e); US-24 also lets it pass as a bill of its own, 641(a)(3))".

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: The parenthesis is accurate and attributes nothing wrongly. It makes two claims separated by a semicolon. The first is that "US-22 has the debt limit ride reconciliation", which is true: USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:448 says "the rates — and direct spending and the debt limit where they move — ride reconciliation at simple majorities (2 U.S.C. 641(a), (e))", and R-US9 (a) at :629 says the same. The second is a bare statutory cite, "641(a)(3)", and the text never says US-22 cites it. 641(a)(3) is paragraph (3) of the 641(a) that US-22 cites, and it is exactly the clause that lets the debt limit ride reconciliation. The on-disk statute ElectionsData/usa/raw/records/govinfo_uscode_2_641_reconciliation.html:27 reads "(3) specify the amounts by which the statutory limit on the public debt is to be changed and direct the committee having jurisdiction to recommend such change". The sources table at :2120 lists 641(a) as on disk. So the cite is the narrowest correct support for the first claim. It is not "US-24's text": US-24 at :466 cites the same statute for the same point ("reconciliation may carry it, 641(a)(3)"). The parenthesis also does not pretend to justify a standalone act with US-22's words. The block names the act as "US-24's debt-limit act" (:833) and gives the parenthesis only as the reason the panel's default classes it as a BUDGET VOTE, since the debt limit belongs to the budget/reconciliation path. R18 (:1391) prints the alternative reading. Both implementers would compute the same thing, so there is no ambiguity, no false reference and no unfaithfulness. The proposed fix is only a rewording.

### facts-F7 (low; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.** "**Ruling record:** INSTALLED-PENDING-E4 (sNNN): (c)." /

**Defect.** The mirror forms write the section as 'sNNN'. The precedent instrument (UsHouseCountCheck) and the plan's actual mirror lines write '§NNN'. The plan's current line after this block is also a fifth form, 'NONE (§832) - the instrument is not built; ...', which the block's four forms leave out. The instrument compares the mirror as an exact string, so an implementer may print 's840' or '§840' and treat the NONE line as either a failure or out of scope.

**Evidence.** Assets/Editor/UsHouseCountCheck.cs:690 (mirror built from RulingSection, e.g. "§800"); docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1503 ("**Ruling record:** NONE (§832) - ..."), :1995 ("ASKED (§801)").

**Fix proposed.** State that in the mirror line sNNN is printed as the section sign plus the number ("§NNN"), as R-US18's mirror is. Add the NONE form ("**Ruling record:** NONE (§832) - the instrument is not built; its first run registers the outcome.") as the one form the instrument fails on by design.

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: The NONE half of the claim is wrong, and the sNNN half is not a defect in the rule. (1) The NONE state is already handled. USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1451-1452 says: "NONE: the state before the instrument exists. The instrument fails while it reads NONE, so the first-run commit registers the outcome." Line 1497 requires the first-run commit to register "INSTALLED-PENDING-E4 or ASKED". So the plan line at :1503, "NONE (§832) - the instrument is not built...", is a pre-instrument state that fails by design whatever its mirror text says. Its mirror form never needs to match anything. The four quoted forms (:1471-1474) cover exactly the states in which the instrument can pass. The proposed fix ("the one form the instrument fails on by design") repeats what :1451-1452 already says. "Failure" and "out of scope" also lead to the same action, since the first-run commit must replace the line either way. (2) "sNNN" is the block's declared ASCII placeholder for the section, not a competing spelling. The block must be plain ASCII (:1418, "in plain ASCII inside a ```text fence"), so it cannot write a section sign. Line 737 defines the token: '"sNNN" and "sMMM" stand for the COMPLETED.md section a later commit takes'. The block uses the same notation for the real section throughout: "COMPLETED.md s832" at :1422 and :1490, where it can only mean §832. The rendering outside the block is fixed three ways: - the line right after the block, "(§832)" at :1503; - the R-US18 and R-US19 mirrors, "(§800)" at :1730 and "(§801)" at :1995; - both precedent instruments, UsHouseCountCheck.cs:40 (RulingSection = "§800") with the mirror built at :690, and UsSenateRaceCheck.cs:39 ("§801") with the mirror built at :670. MetaTextCheck's ban on the section sign covers only Assets/Scripts/UI, LawCatalog.cs and Assets/Scripts/Data (MetaTextCheck.cs:60-64), not Editor instruments. So nothing pushes an implementer toward "s840". (3) The spelling cannot change any outcome. The mirror is "never a digest" (:1470) and is not part of the decisive digest (:1430-1449). The implementer writes both the instrument's section string and the plan line in the same first-run commit. The exact-string check (:1475) passes only when they agree, so a mismatch shows up as a FAIL at once and the outcome, digest and decision are untouched. At most this is a cosmetic point, not a defect in the rule.

### fidelity-F3 (low; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.** E[named] = 0, it prints "not evaluated (denominator 0)".

**Defect.** G5's "far more" was implemented at s802 so that a rule naming nothing counts as "far more" (`!$nm ||`). The block instead leaves S1 unevaluated at E[named] = 0 and does not declare the change. The outcome label then differs from s802's convention (S2 instead of S1 + S2), and the label enters the decisive digest and the ruling record.

**Evidence.** Tools/us_veto_backtest.pl l.227: `my @far = grep { my $nm = $named{$_}{pooled} // 0; !$nm || ... < $b1Precision } @RULES;`. The block's outcome set is "INSTALL (no stop fires); S3; S1; S2; S1 + S2", and its digest line is `outcome=INSTALL|S3|S1|S2|S1+S2`.

**Fix proposed.** Either adopt s802's convention (S1 fires where E[named] = 0, "names none") or keep the block's choice with the declaration "departs from s802's G5 implementation, which counted a rule naming none as far more".

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: The quote and the tool line are both accurate. Tools/us_veto_backtest.pl l.227 does count a rule naming nothing as "far more" (`!$nm ||`), and plan l.1234-1235 says "Where E[named] = 0, it prints \"not evaluated (denominator 0)\"". It is still not a defect of the rule, for four reasons. (1) The block follows G5 as G5 is recorded. Plan l.287 reads "'far more' read, before the first run, as a pooled precision below B1's on Poland (PROVISIONAL, G5)", and docs/generated/US_VETO_BACKTEST.md l.103 reads "a rule whose pooled precision is below it." A rule that names nothing has no precision, so its precision is not below B1's. The `!$nm` branch is an implementation detail of s802 that no record states. The block's S1 says "as G5 reads it" (l.1232), and that is accurate. The block also handles the zero case explicitly: "fires iff 166 * E[hits] < 45 * E[named]" is false at 0, and l.1235 says so outright. Two implementers cannot read it differently. (2) s802 needed `!$nm` for a different test. s802's stop was "every rule names far more" across several rules (`@far == @RULES`, l.229), and the branch kept a rule that names nothing from blocking that "every rule" conjunction. The block tests one rule, (c), so the convention has no job there. (3) The decision cannot change. The block asserts E[named] = E[vetoable sent] (l.1157), and V_XXII is a subset of V (l.828). With every P(sent_j) >= 0, E[named] = 0 forces either E_XXII[vetoable sent] = 0 or an empty V_XXII, so S2 fires in every such run (l.1229-1231). Either way the run is STOPPED and re-asked. The STOPPED verdict line prints "each stop with its figures" (l.1260), so S1's "not evaluated (denominator 0)" reaches Elias anyway. S1's options (l.1271) are already among S2's (l.1267-1270). (4) The block does not inherit the s802 tool's verdict code. It keeps us_veto_backtest.pl byte-unchanged "as the record of s802" (l.1146-1147) and states its own S1 test in full. A difference from an undocumented edge branch in a superseded tool is not a false reference and not an unfaithful reading of any ruling. At most it would be an optional note, not a defect needing an amendment.

### fidelity-F6 (low; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.** re-asks. When S3 fires, S1 and S2 are not evaluated, and their figures print UNFITTED.

**Defect.** S3 is the panel's added STOP, and when it fires it suppresses R-US17's own STOPs, including S2's case "V_XXII is empty". That case reads no cell and is fully computable on an unfitted fit. If S3 fires, a STOP of R-US17 that holds goes unreported, and the outcome sent to Elias names only the panel's addition.

**Evidence.** Block l.1223-1231. The S2 empty case is defined on V_XXII, which is a count of the record and not fitted (THE PRINT, NOT FITTED: "|V|, |V_XXII|").

**Fix proposed.** Always evaluate S2's "no Rule XXII measure on the veto path" case, since it reads no cell. Add the outcomes "S3 + S2" to THE OUTCOME set and to the digest's `outcome=` line.

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: The mechanism is described correctly, but what the claim says follows from it does not hold up, and the ordering is a deliberate rule, not a defect. 1. The rule still stops and re-asks. R-US17 asks that code "STOPS and re-asks" (plan l.721). When S3 fires, the block says "the rule re-asks" (l.1223-1225), and the outcome S3 never installs (l.1237). No run ends in INSTALL when S2's empty case holds, so nothing is decided wrongly. 2. The fact is not "unreported". |V_XXII| is a NOT FITTED count ("counts of the record (... |V|, |V_XXII| ...)", l.1050-1051). It is printed in THE RECORD whatever the outcome (item 1, "|V_XXII|", l.1167-1168). It also enters the decisive digest as "v_xxii=<|V_XXII|>" (l.1442), and "Whatever the outcome, the full document goes to Elias (E4)" (l.1262). An empty V_XXII therefore reaches Elias as "0" even under S3. Only S1's and S2's computed figures print UNFITTED (l.1225, l.1230). 3. Nothing is ambiguous. "When S3 fires, S1 and S2 are not evaluated" (l.1225) is explicit, so two implementers cannot build it differently. 4. The proposed "S3 + S2" outcome would cause trouble of its own. S2's options begin "install (c) as measured" and "R1, reading (A)" (l.1267-1268). R1 still uses the draw (l.1358-1359), so neither option can be built while a cell is UNFITTED: H4 says "an unfitted figure ... never counts in a decision" (brief l.62-63), and "A ruling must name a buildable option" (l.1468-1469). Putting S3 first makes sure the fit exists before R-US17's own stops are put to Elias. Those stops are then evaluated on the next run, as the brief requires ("must survive in some form", brief l.95-96). The worst case is one extra re-ask round, and only if a thin cell and an empty V_XXII ever happen together. That is a design preference, not a rule that is wrong, cannot be computed or is unfaithful to a ruling.

### fidelity-F7 (low; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.** US-21's sentence "under it no Congress of record overrides", measured again with the

**Defect.** US-21's plan text says the same-sides premise was "measured and said: under it no Congress of record overrides". The block re-measures this with the draw but gives no consequence when the measurement contradicts it. The plan's US-21 sentence could stay false after the first run with nothing obliging a correction or a report to Elias. US-21's text is outside the block, and the block says US-21's text owns its own design.

**Evidence.** US-21 (plan l.442); block l.1037-1038 ("PRINTED ONLY, never deciding"); block l.738-740.

**Fix proposed.** Add: "If the re-measure finds a Congress of record that overrides under the draw, the first-run commit amends US-21's sentence to the measured figure and the reply to Elias names it (E4)."

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: The claim is refuted. The quote is accurate (block lines 1037-1038), but neither half of the defect holds up. (1) The report to Elias already exists. The re-measure is one of "THE OVERRIDE's prints" (l.1032-1038), printed as item 6 of the document (l.1183). Then l.1262 reads: "Whatever the outcome, the full document goes to Elias (E4)." So every run puts the measured-again sentence in front of Elias. The claim that nothing obliges "a report to Elias" is false. (2) Amending US-21's prose falls outside the rule's scope, by the block's own terms. l.738-740 hands US-21's design text to US-21, "outside this block". US-21 (plan l.441-444) lands after the first run and has its own done-when. The sentence "measured and said: under it no Congress of record overrides" records US-10's measurement at s802. That measurement was made without the draw, under the one-stance premise (the "same sides" premise, Poland's), and the draw does not make it false. Under the draw, an override is only a probability. omega_j (l.1033) and "P(override | named)" (l.1035) are "PRINTED ONLY, never deciding" (l.1032), and the override is kept out of the decisive digest (l.1449). So the proposed trigger, "if the re-measure finds a Congress of record that overrides under the draw", is undefined. It could mean omega > 0, a positive expected count, or a seeded realization, and two honest implementers would read it differently. The fix would bring in the very ambiguity it claims to remove. An obligation to edit plan text that the block deliberately leaves out is not a defect in what the rule decides, how it computes, or how faithful it is to H1-H3.

### fidelity-F8 (low; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.** 7. The cells pool over presidents, Congresses, kinds and outcomes. They are split by

**Defect.** H1 says "a seeded draw of defectors per party per chamber". The block fits by role (PP/OP) and not by party label, and THIN CELLS pools a cell across chambers. The draw is still made once per party per chamber, so this is a defensible reading, but neither departure from H1's literal "per party ... per chamber" is declared as a reading of those words. R4 and R11 print alternatives without saying they are the literal readings.

**Evidence.** H1 (l.726). Block l.879-880 ("split by chamber, role and stance only") and l.929-930 ("POOLED with the same role-and-stance cell of the other chamber").

**Fix proposed.** Add to item 7: "DECLARED: H1's 'per party per chamber' names where draws are made (once per party per chamber per bill). The fit's cells are by role, and a thin cell pools across chambers. R4 (by party label) and R11 (within the chamber) are the literal readings."

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: Refuted. The quoted text is accurate (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md l.879-880 and l.929-930), but neither departure exists, and the proposed fix would add a false statement to the block. (1) The block already takes H1's "per party per chamber" literally, as the unit a draw is made in. Item 2 (l.851-852) says "once per party per chamber per bill", and item 5 (l.873) says "ONE DRAW per party per chamber per bill serves every vote of that chamber on it". Every party draws its own count in every chamber. Fitting by role leaves this intact: on any bill, PP and OP map one to one onto R and D, so each party still gets its own draw (item 2, l.855-856). (2) H1 (l.726) does not set how the fit is split into cells. It says only that the draw is "fitted on the recorded chamber-by-chamber votes since 2017". That phrase names the data, in contrast to s802's one-stance premise (l.723: "Read chamber by chamber ... as the members voted there"). A pooled cell still reads only individual chambers' recorded roll calls (l.896, l.905-912). The panel's brief also treats the split as open, not ruled: facts.md l.69-72 lists "per party, per chamber ... pooled or split - and what decides the split" as something the panel must decide. Elias's standing instruction (facts.md l.63-65) asks for a DEFAULT plus a declared alternative reading printed beside it, which the block already provides: - the default and its reasons are in item 7 (l.879-880, "no split is chosen from the data") and THIN CELLS (l.927-934); - the alternatives are R4 (l.1366) and R11 (l.1378); - every reading is run and printed, and one whose outcome differs is named in the verdict line (l.1245-1246, l.1257-1258, l.1410-1412); - R11 is also offered as an S3 option (l.1265). (3) The proposed fix is false on its own terms. R11 pools a thin cell "with the other role of its chamber" (l.1378, l.934): HP with HO, SP with SO. That pools across the president's party and the other party, so it is no more literal to "per party" than the default. R4 is not "the literal reading" of "per party" either, because the default already gives each party its own draw. Adding "R4 and R11 are the literal readings" would write a false claim into the frozen block.

### form-F8 (low; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.** THE OUTCOME is exactly one of: INSTALL (no stop fires); S3; S1; S2; S1 + S2. Its figures

**Defect.** The outcome is supposed to be defined once, but the block spells it two ways. THE OUTCOME and the ASKED mirror write 'S1 + S2'. The decisive digest writes 'outcome=INSTALL|S3|S1|S2|S1+S2'. The ruling record compares 'the generated outcome' with the registered one without saying which spelling it means.

**Evidence.** In the block: 'outcome=INSTALL|S3|S1|S2|S1+S2' under THE DECISIVE DIGEST, and '"**Ruling record:** ASKED (sNNN): S1 + S2."' under THE MIRROR.

**Fix proposed.** Add to THE OUTCOME: "Spelled exactly INSTALL, S3, S1, S2 or 'S1 + S2' everywhere it prints or is registered; in the decisive digest the spaces are dropped ('S1+S2')."

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: The quote is accurate: USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1237 says "INSTALL (no stop fires); S3; S1; S2; S1 + S2", :1447 says "outcome=INSTALL|S3|S1|S2|S1+S2", and :1473 says "ASKED (sNNN): S1 + S2." But the difference in spelling changes no decision, and the rule already says exactly what each output looks like. (1) The digest line has its own byte format. Line 1430 says it is "SHA-256 over these lines ... each 'key=value'", and line 1447 gives the value literally as S1+S2. An implementer who follows that template writes S1+S2. Nothing in the block tells anyone to copy THE OUTCOME's prose spelling into the digest. (2) Nothing outside the instrument has to reproduce the digest. It is not fixed in advance. The first run computes it and registers it (1451-1453, 1497), and later runs only compare it with that registered value (1134, 1459-1465). The mutation-suite control is the same code reproducing its own output (1476-1477). So even an implementer who wrote "S1 + S2" in the digest would agree with itself every time and decide nothing differently. (3) "The generated outcome ... equal the registered ones" (1459) compares two values held by the same instrument. Spelling cannot affect that comparison. (4) The mirror line has its own explicit template, "S1 + S2" (1473). That matches THE OUTCOME (1237) and the existing R-US18 mirror "S1 + S4 [(i)]" (1995). So nothing is wrong, uncomputable or mis-decided, and two honest implementers would not reach different verdicts. At most it is a cosmetic difference between a prose line and a serialization template, so I count it as not a defect.

### form-F9 (low; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.**   typed (23 on the preregistered extract).

**Defect.** The text says V_COUNT is 'computed, never typed', and then types it. As written, the 23 is neither asserted nor used, so a wrong figure here would never be caught. §791's corrections removed typed counts for exactly this reason ('no typed counts').

**Evidence.** COMPLETED.md §791 lists 'no typed counts' among the corrections. The block asserts other record counts explicitly, such as the UNPREDICTABLE set and 'B1 = 45 of 166'.

**Fix proposed.** Either delete '(23 on the preregistered extract)', or turn it into an assertion: "Asserted: V_COUNT = (the record's vetoed rows) - |UNPREDICTABLE|; a difference is a DEFECT." That form names no typed figure.

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: The quote is accurate (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:767-768), but it is not a real defect. (1) The figure is correct and already on the record. H2 (plan:727) and the s802 STOP text (plan:723) give 25 vetoes, and say Trump's two 2025 vetoes are unpredictable. docs/generated/US_VETO_BACKTEST.md:13 and :98 give 25 vetoed, and its veto table (lines 56-80) shows exactly two vetoed rows with no roll call in either chamber: 119 H.R. 131 and 119 H.R. 504 (lines 79-80). 25 - 2 = 23. (2) The claim that "a wrong figure would never be caught" is false, because the count is pinned by things the block already asserts. The SOURCE PIN fixes the CSV bytes (plan:752-755). UNPREDICTABLE is asserted to be exactly those two vetoed rows (plan:763-765, re-asserted at item 10, plan:1206). Identity (iii) (plan:1200) asserts that the document's pooled (a) row is reproduced: 22 vetoed among the named plus 3 vetoes outside it, which is 25 vetoed over all rows (US_VETO_BACKTEST.md:22). Identity (ii) (plan:1198-1199) reproduces "vetoes sendable" against the same 25. So any V_COUNT other than 23 would fail an asserted identity. (3) "Computed, never typed" tells the instrument not to hardcode a constant. The parenthetical is a reader's note, explicitly scoped "on the preregistered extract", and it does not break that instruction. The block also prints other typed reference figures next to computed ones, for example "F3's 34" at plan:790 and plan:826. (4) The proposed assertion, "V_COUNT = vetoed rows - |UNPREDICTABLE|", follows directly from the definitions at plan:763-768, since every UNPREDICTABLE row is vetoed by definition. It would check nothing new. (5) The §791 "no typed counts" precedent (COMPLETED.md:37613) was about a typed count that was wrong (213 vs 212). This one is correct and already backed by the identities.

### game-F9 (low; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.**     "**Ruling record:** INSTALLED-PENDING-E4 (sNNN): (c)." /
    "**Ruling record:** INSTALLED (sNNN; E4 sMMM): (c)." /

**Defect.** The block does not settle whether the mirror line writes 's' or '§'. The block's 'sNNN' is its ASCII stand-in for a section, but the plan's real mirror lines use '§'. The line after this block reads '**Ruling record:** NONE (§832) - ...', and UsHouseCountCheck's RulingSection = "§800". One implementer emits '(s845)', another '(§845)'. The instrument then fails against a plan line written the other way.

**Evidence.** Plan line 1503: '**Ruling record:** NONE (§832) - the instrument is not built; its first run registers the outcome.' UsHouseCountCheck.cs:40: 'private const string RulingSection = "§800";'. Block lines 737-738: '"sNNN" and "sMMM" stand for the COMPLETED.md section a later commit takes.'

**Fix proposed.** Add to THE MIRROR: 'In the plan's line, a section prints as "§" followed by its number (U+00A7), as R-US18's and R-US19's mirrors do; "s" is this block's ASCII spelling only.'

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: This is not a real defect of the rule. The quote is accurate (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1471-1472), but the context already settles the spelling. (1) The block says it is "in plain ASCII inside a ```text fence" (line 1418). A grep finds no "§" and no non-ASCII byte anywhere in lines 730-1501. So "s" is the block's one ASCII spelling of the section sign, used the same way throughout: "COMPLETED.md section 832" (734), "s832" (750, 1422, 1425), "P.L. 94-329 s601(b)" and "D.C. Code s1-206.04" (811). Line 737 calls sNNN a stand-in for "the COMPLETED.md section", not a literal string. (2) The mirror line is plan prose outside the block. It is already written with "§": "**Ruling record:** NONE (§832)" (line 1503). The block's own NONE state is mirrored there with the section sign. Both precedent mirrors do the same: "ASKED (§800)" (1730) and "ASKED (§801)" (1995). So do both precedent instruments, which build the mirror from a constant, RulingSection = "§800" / "§801" (UsHouseCountCheck.cs:40 and :690, UsSenateRaceCheck.cs:39 and :670). The convention an implementer edits is in plain view. (3) The claimed failure cannot mis-decide anything. The instrument's record and the plan line are written by the same first-run commit, and "The instrument fails while the mirror and the record differ" (1475). A mismatch fails loudly at once; that check exists for exactly this. The mirror is "never a digest" (1470) and is not in the decisive digest (1430-1449), so neither spelling changes an outcome or a digest. (4) The line sits outside the frozen block. Fixing it later needs no amendment, so the "cheap now, costly later" premise does not apply. At most this is a cosmetic note. It does not meet the bar: no two honest implementers would end up with a passing instrument and different decisions.

### inference-r13-bounds (low; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.** S1 on the BOOT upper bound (strict) and on the lower bound (lax).

**Defect.** The labels read backwards and the comparator is missing. S1 fires when precision is below 27.1 %. Testing the upper bound (hi < 45/166) stops less often, so it is lax about stopping; testing the lower bound stops more often. The bounds are IEEE doubles, while EXACTNESS covers only THE DECISION. Implementers could pick opposite conventions and opposite boundary handling, and the verdict line names a reading only where its outcome differs.

**Evidence.** R13 at line 1382. EXACTNESS at lines 843-844. BOOT is computed in double (line 1074).

**Fix proposed.** Write: "S1 fires iff the BOOT upper bound < 45/166 (requires the whole interval below; stops least)" and "S1 fires iff the BOOT lower bound < 45/166 (stops most)". Compare the double's exact binary value with the rational 45/166, equal never below, and print UNDEFINED where the interval is undefined.

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: The quote is accurate (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1381-1382), but none of the claimed defects holds up. (1) The labels are not backwards. S1 is a stop that fires iff precision < 45/166 (line 1234: "fires iff 166 * E[hits] < 45 * E[named]"). Testing the upper bound fires only when the whole 95% interval lies below B1, so S1 needs significant evidence before it stops. That is the strict criterion in the ordinary hypothesis-test sense. Testing the lower bound fires whenever precision is not significantly above B1, which is the lax criterion. The reviewer's 'lax about stopping' is a different framing. It does not show an error, and the computation is set by the bound each reading names, not by the parenthetical. (2) The comparator is not missing. R13 swaps one quantity in S1's own test, which is a strict '<' with 'Equal is never below' (EXACTNESS, lines 843-844). Read that way, the tests are 'hi < 45/166' and 'lo < 45/166', and no other honest reading exists. (3) Boundary handling cannot differ. Each declared reading 'runs the whole rule with one change' (line 1355), so EXACTNESS's rational comparison applies to R13's S1 test. Every finite IEEE double is a dyadic rational, while 45/166 = 45/(2*83) is not, so no BOOT bound (line 1074) can ever equal 45/166. 'Opposite boundary handling' therefore has no case where it changes the outcome. (4) Readings never decide or stop anything (lines 1240, 1245-1246), so the only effect would be on whether R13 is named in the verdict line, and (1)-(3) leave nothing to change that. Residual, outside the stated defect and only in the proposed fix: the block does not say what R13's S1 variant prints when the precision's BOOT interval is 'UNDEFINED IN <k> OF 2000' (k >= 50, lines 1075-1077). That is a separate low clarity gap, not the claim made here.

### inference-readings-not-digested (low; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.**   Rationals are "p/q" in lowest terms, q > 0, zero written "0/1", with no sign. Nothing
  else enters: no interval, reading, (A), override, realization, INFLUENCE or companion.

**Defect.** The verdict line names every reading whose outcome differs, and the FRAGILE count k. Both are what Elias is asked to weigh. Neither enters the decisive digest, so a later code change that flips a reading's outcome or changes k passes silently. It needs no re-pin, no "MOVED DIGEST" report and no listing in the next reply. The verdict line Elias saw at registration can change without trace.

**Evidence.** Verdict line clauses (lines 1256-1258). MOVED DIGEST procedure (lines 1464-1467).

**Fix proposed.** Add lines to the decisive digest, after outcome=, that carry no figures: "reading.<Rn>.outcome=..." for each declared reading in order, and "influence.k=<k>" plus "influence.<i>=<roll-call identity>". Or register a second "verdict digest" over the generated verdict line under the same re-pin and report rules.

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: The quote is accurate (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1448-1449), and readings and INFLUENCE really are left out of the decisive digest. That is a stated design choice, not a gap. (1) The quoted sentence itself says so: "Nothing else enters: no interval, reading, (A), override, realization, INFLUENCE or companion". The section on the decision gives the reason. "THE OUTCOME is exactly one of: INSTALL ... Its figures are not part of it" (1237-1238). The list headed "PRINTED ONLY, never part of the outcome" names "INFLUENCE" and "every declared reading and its outcome ... no reading ever stops the rule" (1240-1246). The decisive digest fingerprints what decides, and the ruling record guards that decision (1459-1467). (2) It follows the plan's own precedent. R-US18's digest covers figures "under every reading not made printed only ... Nothing else is in it" (1693-1698). R-US19's covers "under every reading not made printed only ... Nothing else" (1956-1960). Every R-US17 reading is printed only (1355-1356: "never a stop"), so leaving them out is that same form applied. The proposed fix would break with it. It would also bring double-arithmetic results into a digest of exact rationals: R8 is "doubles, exact pmf" (1373-1374), and R13's BOOT bounds are IEEE double (1074). (3) The verdict line also carries BOOT intervals ("95% [..] BOOT", 1251-1255), which are just as unpinned. By the reviewer's logic those would need pinning too. The block's line between pinned and unpinned is consistent: what decides versus what is printed. (4) "Without trace" overstates it. With the block's digest and the SourceDigest pinned, a reading's outcome can change in only three ways. (a) A block amendment takes a new block digest and is reported to Elias ("A choice changed after a result is in sight takes a new digest and is reported to Elias (G1)", 1148-1149; RuleDigests 1424-1427). (b) A source change moves source= and so the decisive digest, which forces the MOVED DIGEST / CHANGED OUTCOME report (1432, 1461-1467). (c) A code change that departs from the pinned specification is an implementation defect, not a rule defect. Even then, the instrument rewrites docs/generated/US_VETO_DRAW.md with the verdict line in it (1143-1144), and "the full document goes to Elias" whatever the outcome (1262). The behaviour described is real, but it is intended and consistent with the precedents. It does not make the rule wrong, ambiguous or unfaithful to H1-H3.

## The second pass - the session's edits (`rus17c-edits-pass2`)

Two lenses (consistency of the edits, facts of the edits) read the session's edits as a diff against the panel's text, and the block in place; the same verification.

### Confirmed (11)

### consistency-C2-1 (medium; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.**   - an UNFITTED cell prints "UNFITTED n=<n> - fewer than 12 observations (pooled n=<n'>)",
    with no figure and no interval;

**Defect.** The n/n_own split was not carried into TOO THIN. After the edit, n means |drawn list|, and for a pooled cell that is the union (THE FIT: "n = |L|"; digest: "an UNFITTED cell writes cell.<XX>.n as its union's size"). An UNFITTED cell is always pooled, so under the new definitions n=<n> and pooled n=<n'> both mean the union size, and n' is defined nowhere. The panel's form meant own count, then union count. The print and the digest now disagree about what the first n is.

**Evidence.** THE FIT: "A CELL'S DRAWN LIST L: L_own, or for a pooled cell the union it draws from (THIN CELLS); n = |L|. L and n serve ... the label." THE DECISIVE DIGEST: "an UNFITTED cell writes cell.<XX>.n as its union's size".

**Fix proposed.** Write: "an UNFITTED cell prints \"UNFITTED n_own=<n_own> - fewer than 12 observations (pooled n=<n>)\", with n its union's size (THE FIT), with no figure and no interval".

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: I confirm the defect. It is half-fixed: the session redefined n, but this print line still uses the panel's old form. 1. The TOO THIN line in the plan (G:\UNITY\Projects\PoliSim\docs\specs\USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1119) still reads "UNFITTED n=<n> - fewer than 12 observations (pooled n=<n'>)". The session left it unchanged: it appears identically in final_spec.txt:357 and block.txt:388, and it is in no hunk of session_edits.diff. 2. The edits changed what <n> means.    - THE FIT now says "A CELL'S DRAWN LIST L: L_own, or for a pooled cell the union it draws from (THIN CELLS); n = |L|" (plan:934-935). This replaced the panel's single "n of a cell = |L|", where L was the cell's own observations (diff lines 120-127).    - THE PRINT's own n bullet was rewritten to "n: for a cell figure, the cell's n (its drawn list, full cells)" (plan:1079; diff 206-210).    - The digest says "an UNFITTED cell writes cell.<XX>.n as its union's size" (plan:1500). 3. An UNFITTED cell is always a pooled cell. A cell with n_own >= 12 is never pooled. If one cell is below 12 but its partner is not, their union is at least 12. So UNFITTED requires both n_own < 12 and a union under 12 (plan:947-952). For such a cell, under the current definitions <n> is the union's size. 4. <n'> is defined nowhere: the only hit for n' in the block is this line, and the panel's text never defined it either. In the panel's text the form still made sense: n was the own count and n' the pooled union, which is how the first review read it (confirmed.txt:60). Under the new definitions the first n is the union, so the line either prints the union twice or makes an implementer read n as n_own, against plan:1079. That is an ambiguity in a generated print. 5. Related gap: THE FIRST RUN item 2 prints only "n" for the cells (plan:1205). This line is the natural place for n_own to reach the document; today it appears only in the digest (plan:1483). The reviewer slightly overstates one point. Read strictly, the print's first n and the digest's cell.<XX>.n both mean the union, so they do not disagree. The real problems are the undefined n' and the panel's own-then-union intent, which the new definitions break. The print does not enter the decisive digest and cannot change the outcome, so medium (ambiguity) is the right ceiling. The proposed fix is correct: "UNFITTED n_own=<n_own> - fewer than 12 observations (pooled n=<n>)", with n the union's size (THE FIT), no figure and no interval.

**What was done.** Done with facts-E2: an UNFITTED cell prints "UNFITTED n_own=<n_own>, pooled with <YY> n=<n> - fewer than 12 observations", n its union's size (THE FIT).

### consistency-C2-4 (medium; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** - R9: the role taken from THE PRESIDENT ON A DATE of each roll call in place of the
  decision president. It changes only which cell, P or O, an observation enters; V, (B)
  and (a) keep the decision president.

**Defect.** The new scope sentence is false under THE FIT's own definitions. THE FITTED ROLL CALLS are selected by the PP's bloc stance ("every distinct roll call on which the PP of its OWNER has bloc stance NAY"). The PP observation is the PP's yeas, and the OP observation exists only where the OP stands YEA. Swapping the PP on a roll call therefore changes which roll calls are fitted, which party's members give each observation, and whether an OP observation exists. It does not merely move observations between P and O cells. Two implementers will build R9 differently, and R9's outcome can be named in the verdict line.

**Evidence.** THE FIT: "THE FITTED ROLL CALLS, per chamber: every distinct roll call on which the PP of its OWNER has bloc stance NAY. Each gives: a PP observation: d = PP yea ... an OP observation only if the OP's bloc stance is YEA".

**Fix proposed.** Write: "R9: THE FITTED ROLL CALLS and their observations are read with the PP of THE PRESIDENT ON A DATE of each roll call in place of the owner's decision president (selection, PP and OP observations alike); V, (B) and (a) keep the decision president."

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: Confirmed. The scope sentence is a session edit: session_edits.diff lines 320-323 replace the panel's one-line R9 with the current text. It contradicts how the block itself defines the fit and the cells. 1. Selection depends on the PP. USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:921-922 says the fitted roll calls are "every distinct roll call on which the PP of its OWNER has bloc stance NAY". 2. Each observation is defined by the PP's role and stance. At :923-926 the PP observation is "d = PP yea", and an OP observation exists "only if the OP's bloc stance is YEA" (d = OP nay + present). 3. The cells are split by role and stance together. :849-850 says "HP and SP, the PP standing NAY ... HO and SO, the OP standing YEA". THE MODEL item 7 (:892-893) says the cells are "split by chamber, role and stance only (four cells)". Take a roll call where the president on its date (:791-794) is not the decision president. Suppose the decision PP stands NAY and the other party stands YEA. That can happen for any roll call dated 2017-01-03 to 2017-01-19, which :780-781 explicitly admits, or 2021/2025-01-03 to 01-19. R9 can then be built two ways, with different results: - **Literal reading** ("changes only which cell, P or O"): keep the default's selection and observations and swap their cells. The decision PP's NAY-stance observation (d = yea) goes into an O cell, which is defined as "OP standing YEA". The other party's YEA-stance observation goes into a P cell, defined as "PP standing NAY". That breaks :849-850 and :892-893. Where the OP also stood NAY (:925-926), nothing in the block says where the lone observation goes. - **Substitution reading** ("role taken ... in place of the decision president", applied to THE FIT's "PP of its OWNER"): this roll call is no longer selected, because the date-PP stands YEA. Conversely, roll calls where the decision PP stood YEA and the date-PP stands NAY are newly selected, and d is redefined for each party. That is not "only which cell an observation enters". Nothing else in the block settles which reading is meant. :791 only defines the date. Readings never enter the decisive digest (:1501-1502), but :1281-1282 names a reading whose outcome differs in the verdict line, so the choice can show up in generated text. Medium (ambiguity, plus a false scope claim) is the right severity. The reviewer's fix resolves it: apply THE PRESIDENT ON A DATE's PP to THE FITTED ROLL CALLS' selection and to both the PP and OP observations, and keep V, (B) and (a) on the decision president.

**What was done.** Done: R9 reads THE FITTED ROLL CALLS and their observations with the PP of THE PRESIDENT ON A DATE in place of the owner's decision president - the selection and both observations alike; V, (B) and (a) keep the decision president.

### consistency-C2-5 (medium; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** One, asked beside the block (the plan, after the digests line), never blocking the first
run: S2's reading of "never".

**Defect.** False reference. The plan has no question after the digests line: line 1560 ("The block's digests ... PENDING_DIGEST (§832)") is followed directly by the R-US18 heading. The block preregisters a question that does not exist where it says it is. The preregistering commit's DocumentClaimCheck and the review would hold it to that.

**Evidence.** USA_STAGE_PLAN.md lines 1558-1562: the Ruling record line, the digests line, a blank line, then "**R-US18 - The House's seat method**". There is no S2 question.

**Fix proposed.** Add the question line after the digests line before the digest is taken (for example "**Asked of Elias (R-US17 (c), S2):** ..."), or reword the reference to where it is actually asked (for example COMPLETED.md s832).

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: Confirmed. The block now says the S2 question is asked in a place where it does not exist. USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1454-1455, inside the block, reads: "One, asked beside the block (the plan, after the digests line), never blocking the first run: S2's reading of "never"." The session's edit introduced this sentence. session_edits.diff lines 342-345 show it replacing the panel's "None. H1-H3, with G1, G5, E4 and H4, settle every choice." Outside the block the plan runs 1556 END marker, 1558 "**Ruling record:** NONE (§832)...", 1560 "**The block's digests** ... `PENDING_DIGEST` (§832).", then 1562 "**R-US18 — The House's seat method**". Nothing about S2 or "never" comes after the digests line. A search of the plan and COMPLETED.md for the question found it only at plan:1455 (the block itself). COMPLETED.md has no s832/§832 section yet, so the question is not recorded there either. confirmed.txt lists no confirmed defect asking for this question, so it is the session's own addition, and the edit stopped halfway: the block points at a place, and the place has no text. The preregistering commit's DocumentClaimCheck and the review would hold the plan to this pointer. One correction to the reviewer's fix: the question line goes outside the block, so adding it changes no digest and does not need to happen "before the digest is taken". The fix is to add, after plan line 1560 and before the R-US18 heading, a line such as "**Asked of Elias (R-US17 (c), S2, never blocking the first run):** is "never sends a vetoable bill" read as E_XXII[vetoable sent] = 0 exactly (the default), or as P(NONE_XXII) >= 1/2 / E_XXII < 1?" The other option is to reword plan:1454 to name where it is actually asked. Medium (false reference) is the right severity.

**What was done.** Done: the asked paragraph ("Asked blind, before the first run (s832)") stands after the digests line, outside the block.

### consistency-C2-6 (medium; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** An answer
that comes before the first run is written in by amendment with a new digest; otherwise
the first run is on this default

**Defect.** The new QUESTIONS text lets the first run be on an amended block. THE FIRST RUN still says that run is on the block "as preregistered". An implementer or reviewer holding the first-run commit to THE FIRST RUN's sentence would reject an amended first run that QUESTIONS allows.

**Evidence.** THE FIRST RUN: "The first run is on the block as preregistered (H1, H3). A choice changed after a result is in sight takes a new digest and is reported to Elias (G1)."

**Fix proposed.** Change THE FIRST RUN's sentence to: "The first run is on the block as preregistered, or as amended by Elias's answer to QUESTIONS before it (the RuleDigests list's last entry) (H1, H3)."

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: Confirmed. The edit created a real contradiction between two parts of the block. 1. THE FIRST RUN, USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1183, is unchanged context in session_edits.diff (diff line 242): "The first run is on the block as preregistered (H1, H3). A choice changed after a result is in sight takes a new digest and is reported to Elias (G1)." 2. The edit replaced QUESTIONS' "None. ..." with new text at :1454-1458: "An answer that comes before the first run is written in by amendment with a new digest; otherwise the first run is on this default". The word "otherwise" means that when Elias answers before the run, the first run is not on the default. It is on an amended block with a new digest. 3. Inside the block, "preregistered" always names the s832 text, never just "any digest taken before a run": - :733 "Preregistered in COMPLETED.md section 832" - :1470 "the preregistered digest is recorded in COMPLETED.md s832" - :1473-1474 RuleDigests' first entry is "preregistered (s832)", and later entries are "AMENDED (Hn, sNNN): old -> new" - :1546 "RuleDigests first entry = the preregistered digest" - :768 and :1086 "the preregistered extract" So "the block as preregistered" is the s832 block. A first run on a block amended under QUESTIONS is, in the block's own terms, a run on an AMENDED block. That breaks :1183's sentence. The harmonizing reading fails. It says the preregistered text itself provides the amendment path, or that H3 only needs some digest before the run. But the amendment replaces the S2 default text, so the run is no longer on the text as preregistered. And :1183's second sentence only covers changes made after a result is in sight. Nothing at :1183 allows a change made before the run. Nothing mechanical decides the case either way. The checks (block equals the list's last entry, :1475; the first entry proven by the review row, :1476-1477) would accept an amended first run. :1183's prose would reject it. A reviewer holding the first-run commit to :1183 would get a different verdict than one holding it to :1457. That is ambiguity, not a mis-decision, so medium is right. The proposed fix works: RuleDigests exists at the first-run commit (:1472), so "the RuleDigests list's last entry" is a valid reference. One more change is needed for the amendment to be recordable: the entry label "AMENDED (Hn, sNNN)" at :1474 assumes an H-ruling. An answer to the S2 question would need a label such as "AMENDED (<ruling>, sNNN)". Side observation, outside this claim: :1454 says the question is "asked beside the block (the plan, after the digests line)". The plan has no such question. :1560 is the digests line and :1562 starts R-US18. That is fine only if the question is added at the preregistering commit.

**What was done.** Done: THE FIRST RUN is on the block as preregistered, or as amended by Elias's answer to QUESTIONS FOR ELIAS before it (the RuleDigests list's last entry).

### consistency-C2-7 (medium; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** "Asserted" means the instrument fails while the statement is false. A DEFECT is a code or
data failure: it fails the instrument and is never put to Elias.

**Defect.** The new header's DEFECT definition contradicts the block's own DEFECTs, which only Elias can clear. A pin mismatch "is a DEFECT until an amendment naming Elias's ruling re-pins it". A changed UNPREDICTABLE set "is a DEFECT until the block is amended by a ruling". THE RULING RECORD says "A changed SourceDigest after the first run is always reported this way", meaning to Elias. The play DEFECT ("No president found on a US vote day is a DEFECT") fails no instrument at all. The same header defines "Asserted" as failing the instrument, yet several assertions belong to US-21's diagnostic or to a commit's bars ("asserted by blob id", "asserted in the first-run commit's bars").

**Evidence.** THE PINS: "A run whose SourceDigest or prep blob differs from a pin is a DEFECT until an amendment naming Elias's ruling re-pins it." UNPREDICTABLE: "Any other set is a DEFECT until the block is amended by a ruling."

**Fix proposed.** Write: "A DEFECT is a code or data failure. It fails the check that finds it (the instrument; in play, US-21's diagnostic) and is never one of the rule's outcomes or registered ASKED. A DEFECT that only a ruling clears (a re-pin, a changed UNPREDICTABLE set) is put to Elias as that ruling. 'Asserted' means the named check (by default the instrument) fails while the statement is false."

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: Confirmed, though the real harm is closer to low than medium. I read the whole block (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:730-1556). Where it came from: the definition was in the panel's drafting note, outside the fence (scratchpad s832/final_spec.txt:6-7). The session's new header put it inside the digested block at plan:736-737. It now says flatly that a DEFECT "fails the instrument and is never put to Elias". 1) "Never put to Elias" contradicts two DEFECTs in the same block that only Elias can clear. - plan:754-755: "A run whose SourceDigest or prep blob differs from a pin is a DEFECT until an amendment naming Elias's ruling re-pins it." - plan:764-765: "Any other set is a DEFECT until the block is amended by a ruling." - plan:1473-1475 says every amendment must name Elias's ruling ("A change without a ruling fails"). - Read literally, a DEFECT never reaches Elias, so the amendment route in those two clauses can never open, and the only cure left is reverting the bytes. That undoes what the panel wanted: its attack and ruling (panel.md:1081-1085) pinned the source precisely so that a source change would go to Elias as a choice instead of being quietly handled as a DEFECT fix. - One can reconcile the two (the DEFECT is not asked; the amendment is), but the header's "never" is false as written. 2) A half-fix the edits introduced: plan:788-789, "No president found on a US vote day is a DEFECT." By the header that means it "fails the instrument". But THE INSTRUMENT is UsVetoDrawCheck run on the CSV (plan:1169) and never sees play. US-21's diagnostic list (plan:1386-1393) has no president check either. So nothing named fails, and what play does in that case is left open. Weaker parts of the claim: - plan:1520 ("always reported this way") only reports to Elias. Reporting is not putting a question to him, so that point does not show a contradiction. - plan:1177 ("asserted in the first-run commit's bars") and plan:1386 ("US-21'S VETO DIAGNOSTIC asserts") name their own checker. They only bend the header's one-checker definition of "Asserted" (low). Nothing here mis-decides an outcome. The proposed fix is sound: say a DEFECT fails the check that finds it, is never one of the rule's outcomes or registered ASKED, and that one only a ruling clears is put to Elias as that ruling. Also add the president-found check to US-21's diagnostic list.

**What was done.** Done: the header defines "Asserted" by the named check (by default the instrument) and a DEFECT as failing the check that finds it, never an outcome or ASKED; a DEFECT only a ruling clears is put to Elias as that ruling.

### consistency-C2-8 (medium; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** Beside them, for US-21's diagnostic only and
never deciding: P(sent_j) and P(named_j) on the full cells (in-sample), as rationals.

**Defect.** The new full-cell figures conflict with THE PRINT. They are per-measure probabilities that read cells, so THE PRINT makes them FITTED. A FITTED figure must print in the label with a BOOT interval "on EVERY surface" and as a percent to one decimal (NUMBER FORMAT). The BOOT rule defines per-measure replicate figures as LOO only, so there is no specified interval for an in-sample figure. The text also does not say where US-21's diagnostic reads the rationals: the catalog does not carry them, and the document prints them.

**Evidence.** THE PRINT: "A FITTED FIGURE is any number computed from the cells' observations ... every per-measure probability that reads a cell ... It prints in the label below on EVERY surface". BOOT: "PER-MEASURE figures: every measure j of the full V is scored from the replicate's cells, LOO". The catalog list holds no probabilities.

**Fix proposed.** Add to THE PRINT's NOT FITTED/DRAWN classes: "DIAGNOSTIC (not a printed figure): the full-cell P(sent_j)/P(named_j) of the counted vetoes of V, written as p/q in their own section of US_VETO_DRAW.md with no label or interval, never on a screen or in a reply; US-21's diagnostic reads them there." Or have the catalog carry them.

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: I could not refute this. The core defect stands at medium severity; the second half is weaker. 1. The edit added a new sentence at USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1187-1188: "Beside them, for US-21's diagnostic only and never deciding: P(sent_j) and P(named_j) on the full cells (in-sample), as rationals." It is the plus side of session_edits.diff at lines 246-247, and it fixes confirmed defect IC-6 (confirmed.txt:99-101). 2. The same edit pass narrowed THE PRINT at 1067-1071 to "every per-measure probability that reads a cell". A full-cell P(sent_j) reads the cells, so it is a FITTED FIGURE. Under that rule it "prints in the label below on EVERY surface". The label for a derived figure is "<value> FITTED n=<n> m=<m> 95% [<lo>, <hi>] BOOT" (1112). 3. NUMBER FORMAT (1114) says "shares, probabilities and rates print as percent to one decimal". That directly conflicts with "as rationals". 4. No exemption exists. The NOT FITTED class (1072-1076) and the DRAWN class (1077) do not cover these figures, and no clause anywhere in the block exempts a diagnostic-only figure. 5. The BOOT interval is not defined for an in-sample figure. BOOT's per-measure rule (1100-1101) is "scored from the replicate's cells, LOO (every copy of j's roll calls removed)". An implementer must either print the LOO figure's interval beside an in-sample value, where the value can fall outside its own interval, or invent a no-LOO bootstrap the text never specifies. R5 ("in-sample, no LOO", 1407) prints only headline figures (1247-1248), so it does not settle this either. 6. The new figures are also missing from "PRINTED, in this order" item 3 (1208-1211). That list names only the LOO P_H, P_S, P(sent), P(named) and omega (FITTED). Weaker half: "where US-21's diagnostic reads them" is not a strong defect. The diagnostics live in Assets/Editor (Assets/Editor/PresidentialVetoDiagnostic.cs:57), and UsVeto.cs and the instrument also sit in Assets/Editor until US-21 (1173-1175). The diagnostic can therefore call the instrument's computation directly. The label and number-format conflict, and the missing interval, are real ambiguities that the edit introduced. The reviewer's fix works: add a DIAGNOSTIC class to THE PRINT, printed as p/q with no label or interval and never on a screen or in a reply. Alternatively, list the figures under item 3 with an explicit exemption.

**What was done.** Done with facts-E4: THE PRINT gains a DIAGNOSTIC class - the full-cell, in-sample P(sent_j) and P(named_j) as reduced rationals in their own section of US_VETO_DRAW.md after item 3, no label or interval, on no other surface; US-21's diagnostic reads them there.

### facts-E2 (medium; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.**   - an UNFITTED cell prints "UNFITTED n=<n> - fewer than 12 observations (pooled n=<n'>)",

**Defect.** The n_own/n split was made in THE FIT, THE PRINT's n and the digest, but this line was missed. Before the edit, n here was the cell's own count and n' the union. Now n is defined as the drawn list's size, and for a pooled (so for every UNFITTED) cell that is the union. The decisive digest says so explicitly ('an UNFITTED cell writes cell.<XX>.n as its union's size'). So n and n' name the same number, and the own count, which decided the pooling, is no longer printed. An implementer either prints the union twice or keeps the old meaning of n, against the new definition.

**Evidence.** Plan lines 932-935 (L_own/n_own; drawn list L, n = |L| 'serve ... the label'); 1079 (n for a cell figure = its drawn list); 1499-1501 (UNFITTED cell's n = its union's size); 1119 (TOO THIN form unchanged).

**Fix proposed.** Write: "an UNFITTED cell prints \"UNFITTED n_own=<n_own>, pooled with <YY> n=<n> - fewer than 12 observations\"" (n = the union's size, as in the digest). Also make PRINTED item 2 ('THE CELLS: n; ...') print n_own and n.

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: I could not refute it. The edits split the own count from the drawn count, but this TOO THIN line was left in its old form. 1. **n is now the drawn list's size, and the label uses it.** USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:932-935 defines L_own and n_own (n_own alone decides pooling). It then defines the drawn list L as "L_own, or for a pooled cell the union it draws from", with n = |L|, and says "L and n serve the draw index, CP, q_any, q10, the mean share and the label". 2. **An UNFITTED cell is always a pooled cell, so its L is the union.** THIN CELLS at :947-952 pools a cell with n_own < 12 into "the union of the two own lists". Only "a union of fewer than 12 observations leaves the cell UNFITTED". 3. **The digest says so outright.** :1500-1501 reads "an UNFITTED cell writes cell.<XX>.n as its union's size and its obs lines over that union". THE PRINT's own rule at :1079 agrees: a cell figure's n is "the cell's n (its drawn list, full cells)". 4. **So the line now names one number twice.** In "UNFITTED n=<n> - fewer than 12 observations (pooled n=<n'>)" (:1119), n is the union's size, and "pooled n", the natural meaning of n', is the union's size too. n' appears nowhere else in the plan (a grep finds only :1119). The own count, which decided the pooling and which the digest now records as cell.<XX>.n_own (:1483), is never printed. 5. **The edits never touched this line.** The diff changed :932-952, :1079-1081 and the digest lines. It has no hunk for the TOO THIN line, and none for PRINTED item 2, "THE CELLS: n; ..." (:1205), which still prints only n. The result is that an implementer must either print the union twice or give n its old meaning (the own count), which contradicts :934-935. Severity medium is right: this is an ambiguity in a printed label. It cannot mis-decide, because the decisive digest (:1483-1484, :1500) fixes both n_own and n. Fix: write "UNFITTED n_own=<n_own>, pooled with <YY> n=<n> - fewer than 12 observations", with n as the union's size, matching the digest. Also make PRINTED item 2 read "THE CELLS: n_own and n; ...".

**What was done.** Done: as consistency-C2-1; THE FIRST RUN item 2 prints n_own and n.

### facts-E4 (medium; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** omega_j; VETO_j = 1 iff j is a counted veto. Beside them, for US-21's diagnostic only and
never deciding: P(sent_j) and P(named_j) on the full cells (in-sample), as rationals.

**Defect.** The new in-sample figures are per-measure probabilities that read a cell. THE PRINT makes every such figure FITTED, to be printed 'in the label below on EVERY surface' with a BOOT interval, and in percent to one decimal (NUMBER FORMAT). The edit prints them 'as rationals' and gives no label. BOOT cannot give them an interval either: its PER-MEASURE rule scores every replicate LOO ('every copy of j's roll calls removed'), so an in-sample interval is undefined. PRINTED's item 3 also lists only the LOO figures, so where these print is left open as well.

**Evidence.** Plan lines 1067-1071 (FITTED = every per-measure probability that reads a cell; label on every surface); 1100-1101 (BOOT per-measure figures are LOO); 1114-1117 (NUMBER FORMAT); 1208-1211 (item 3 lists P_H, P_S, P(sent), P(named), omega only).

**Fix proposed.** Add to THE PRINT's NOT FITTED/DRAWN classes a third class, e.g.: "DIAGNOSTIC (not a fitted figure's print; for US-21's assertion only): the full-cell in-sample P(sent_j) and P(named_j), printed as reduced rationals in their own list after item 3, with no label or interval and never on any other surface." Or have them carry the label with a BOOT interval scored in-sample (state the replicate rule).

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: I confirm the defect at medium severity. The edit at USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1187-1188 adds new figures: "Beside them, for US-21's diagnostic only and never deciding: P(sent_j) and P(named_j) on the full cells (in-sample), as rationals." These are per-measure probabilities that read a cell. The same edit pass rewrote THE PRINT's definition at :1067-1071: "A FITTED FIGURE is any number computed from the cells' observations ... every per-measure probability that reads a cell ... It prints in the label below on EVERY surface: the document, the catalog's comments, any screen and any reply." It added exactly one per-measure exception to NOT FITTED (:1072-1076): RECORD BUDGET CLASS measures, printed "0/1" or "1/1 at K = 0, no draw". It made no exception for the in-sample diagnostic figures. So by the block's own definition they are FITTED, and Elias's H2 ("every fitted figure prints its n and an interval beside it, labelled FITTED n=...") binds them. "As rationals" also conflicts with NUMBER FORMAT (:1114-1116), which says probabilities print as percent to one decimal and that an exact rational is rounded for print. The interval is not specified either. BOOT's PER-MEASURE rule (:1100-1101) scores every replicate "LOO (every copy of j's roll calls removed)". An in-sample replicate rule could be guessed from R5 ("in-sample, no LOO", :1407), but the block never states one. Placement is also open. PRINTED item 3 (:1208-1211) lists only "P_H, P_S, P(sent), P(named) and omega (FITTED)". The catalog's contents (:1366-1371) do not include these figures. So it is unclear whether they print at all. If they print beside item 3 as the word "Beside" suggests, they break THE PRINT and H2. If they do not print, "as rationals" has no surface. I checked for a refutation. The reading "computed, not printed" would avoid the clash, but no text says that. The section is headed "what the backtest on the record measures and prints", and US-21's diagnostic (:1391-1392) compares against "the instrument's exact P(sent) and P(named) as the same rationals", so the instrument has to emit them somewhere. Two implementers could build this two different ways, which makes it a real ambiguity the edit introduced. The reviewer's proposed fix is sound: add a DIAGNOSTIC class to THE PRINT beside NOT FITTED and DRAWN, printed as reduced rationals in their own list after item 3, with no label or interval, never on any other surface, and stated as not subject to H2's label because they never decide. The other route is to give them the label with a BOOT interval and state the in-sample replicate rule.

**What was done.** Done: as consistency-C2-8; THE FIRST RUN's per-measure paragraph and item 3 point to the DIAGNOSTIC section.

  draw").

Lines 1072-1076 put "a RECORD BUDGET CLASS measure's per-measure figures ("0/1" or "1/1 at K = 0, no draw")" under NOT FITTED. That matches the quote exactly. In the diff (session_edits.diff:188-203) these are the only places the C9 fix landed. 2. The edits left THE FIRST RUN's print list alone, and it still says FITTED:    - Item 3 (line 1208): "PER MEASURE OF V, in CSV order: ... budget class ..." and lines 1210-1211: "P_H, P_S, P(sent), P(named) and omega (FITTED)".    - Item 5 (line 1216): "Every counted veto with P(named), FITTED (or "not in V")". ### facts-E5 (low; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** - DATA: the final-agreement roll calls of counted measures, the only roll calls the

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: I confirm the defect at low severity. USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:909-910 now says "DATA: the final-agreement roll calls of counted measures, the only roll calls the extract carries." That is literally false, and the block contradicts it in three places. (1) The next sentence, at :910-912, says "The override roll calls are saved out of tree with their member votes, but the extract keeps only their totals". So the extract does hold the override roll calls. (2) THE OVERRIDE, at :1041, uses the same verb: "the extract carries the override votes' totals only (their party split is out of tree; THE FIT, DATA)". The one possible refutation fails. The block's term A ROLL CALL (:776) is limited to house_final and senate_final, which would leave override votes out. ### Refuted (12)

### consistency-C2-2 (medium; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.** - UNCONTESTED (the PP not AGAINST, or both blocs AGAINST): no draw, and the test at K = 0.
  This applies off V, under (A) and in play.

**Defect.** IC-1 is only half fixed. Item 2's exclusion list gained the both-AGAINST case, but not UNCONTESTED's other branch, "the PP not AGAINST". A play bill is on the veto path when the PP is AGAINST in one chamber. In the other chamber the PP may stand FOR with the OP also FOR. Item 2 then says "The OP standing FOR (YEA) draws from HO or SO", while UNCONTESTED says there is no draw. The same holds under R1 for a measure of V where the PP's recorded stance in one chamber is YEA. The two sections still give opposite answers on whether the OP draws, and that decides draw counts, keys ("one play key per chamber per bill") and P(the draw matters).

**Evidence.** THE MODEL item 2 lists "No draw is made for: an OP standing AGAINST; under (A) or in play, a PP standing AGAINST in a chamber where the OP also stands AGAINST (UNCONTESTED); ..." and has no entry for a chamber where the PP is not AGAINST.

**Fix proposed.** Add this to item 2's no-draw list: "under (A) or in play, either party in a chamber where the PP does not stand AGAINST (UNCONTESTED)". Or open item 2 with "A draw is made only in a chamber where the PP stands AGAINST".

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: The two sections are worded differently, but the gap the reviewer names changes nothing that is computed. One of the two cases it cites cannot happen at all. 1) Play. The block makes one stance in both chambers the premise of play. USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:895 reads "(B), ONE STANCE (play's premise, US-18; spec-risk 4)" and :897 reads "the PP's one stance is NAY in both chambers". At :1303, adopting reading (A) for play "amends US-18's one stance". US-18 itself (:420, "a party votes as one") and :723 ("each party voting as one, so one stance in both chambers") say the same. So a play bill on THE VETO PATH (play) (:840) has the PP AGAINST in both chambers. The reviewer's play case, with the PP AGAINST in one chamber and FOR in the other, does not arise. P(the draw matters) and MARGIN are printed only per measure of V under (B) (:1208-1211), where the PP is NAY in both chambers (:897). The realizations and play keys (:1140-1158) are also play/(B) only. 2) R1 / (A). Here the case does exist: a measure of V with the PP NAY in one chamber and the PP and OP both YEA in the other. UNCONTESTED (:990-991) says plainly "the PP not AGAINST ... no draw ... This applies off V, under (A) and in play". Item 2's no-draw list (:869-873) is a list of exclusions, not a promise that a draw is made in every other case. Even read the reviewer's way, nothing changes: - The only extra draw would be Ko, the OP's draw in a chamber where both blocs stand FOR. - Ko is capped at floor((S_OP-1)/2) (:877). - With N0 = 0, Y = S_PP + S_OP - Ko is always greater than N = Ko. So a House (A) vote (:971) and a Senate majority vote (:977) always pass. - For cloture, Y >= S_PP + (S_OP+1)/2, which is far above floor((3W+4)/5) in every Senate of record. The OP holds at most 53 seats (facts.md:128). So every index pair in the enumeration passes, and P(pass) = 1/1 with or without the draw (:1149-1151). A reading prints only its outcome and headline figures (:1247-1248), so every R1 figure and its outcome come out identical either way. No digest line is affected either: the decisive digest excludes readings (:1501-1502). No figure, outcome, key or digest can differ, and the play case is excluded by the block's own premise. This is not a medium defect, and by the "if uncertain" rule it is refuted.

### facts-E1 (medium; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.** - R9: the role taken from THE PRESIDENT ON A DATE of each roll call in place of the
  decision president. It changes only which cell, P or O, an observation enters; V, (B)
  and (a) keep the decision president.

**Defect.** The scope sentence the edit added makes R9 incoherent. The cells are defined by role AND stance: HP/SP = the PP standing NAY (d = PP yea), HO/SO = the OP standing YEA (d = OP nay + present). THE FITTED ROLL CALLS select a roll call by 'the PP of its OWNER has bloc stance NAY'. If the vote-day president's party differs from the decision president's (roll calls 2017-01-03..19, 2021-01-03..19, 2025-01-03..19, which the block admits explicitly), a PP-NAY observation relabelled becomes an OP-NAY observation, and an OP-YEA observation becomes a PP-YEA one. No cell holds either, and their d is defined against the wrong side. Read as written, R9 cannot be computed. Read naturally (re-select the fitted roll calls by the vote-day PP's NAY and re-derive d and m), it changes far more than 'which cell'. Two implementers get different R9 outcomes, and the verdict line names R9 when its outcome differs.

**Evidence.** Plan lines 921-926 (THE FITTED ROLL CALLS: selection by the OWNER's PP stance NAY; PP obs d = PP yea; OP obs only if OP YEA, d = nay + present); lines 849-850 (THE CELLS by role and stance); lines 1415-1417 (R9 as edited); line 780 (roll calls from 2017-01-03 admitted); THE PRESIDENT ON A DATE lines 791-794 (D before 2017-01-20).

**Fix proposed.** Replace the second sentence of R9 with: "Under R9, THE FITTED ROLL CALLS are re-selected with PP and OP taken from THE PRESIDENT ON A DATE of each roll call (a roll call is fitted iff that PP's bloc stance is NAY; its observations are derived as in THE FIT with that PP and OP), and THIN CELLS are re-decided on the result. V, (B), (a) and the per-measure scoring keep the decision president."

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: Refuted. R9 as edited can be computed, and only one reading fits its words. The scope sentence (plan lines 1415-1417) says R9 "changes ONLY which cell, P or O, an observation enters". So THE FITTED ROLL CALLS (lines 921-922, chosen by the OWNER's decision PP standing NAY) and the way each observation's (d, m) is derived (lines 923-926) stay as they are. Under R9, each observation already derived goes into the P or O cell of its chamber according to whether its party is the PP of THE PRESIDENT ON A DATE. That date is defined at lines 791-794 and taken from the roll call's IDENTITY date (line 777). That is a finished algorithm. The reviewer's "natural reading" (pick the fitted roll calls again by the vote-day PP, then derive d and m again) is exactly what "only" rules out, so the claim that two implementers would compute it differently does not hold. The claim that "their d is defined against the wrong side" is also false. DEFECTOR (line 929) defines d against the party's OWN recorded bloc stance: PP yea when the PP stands NAY, OP nay + present when the OP stands YEA. So a relabelled observation still holds a crossing share with d/m <= 1/2 (line 928), and the draw (lines 874-879) reads only that share. A cell is just a list of (d, m) pairs. The stance named in THE CELLS (lines 849-850) is how the default's selection happens to fill them, not a filter that R9 breaks. Declared readings are defined as "the whole rule with one change" (line 1395), and other readings redefine the cells the same way: R2 pools over stances (lines 1400-1403) and R4 splits by party label instead of role (line 1406). So R9 overriding which cell an observation enters is the normal form, not a new contradiction. The scope sentence is also the very fix the first review prescribed (confirmed.txt line 66, C5: "R9 changes only which cell, P or O, an observation enters; V, (B) and (a) keep the decision president"). The edit applied it in full. Whether choosing the roll calls again would be a better sensitivity test is a design preference, not a defect the edit introduced.
