Practical note for the session: write that paragraph after line 1560 before the preregistering commit. Its absence would not change the block's digest.

### consistency-C2-11 (low; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.** over an empty V_XXII, e_sent_xxii=0/1 and p_none_xxii=1/1 (the empty sum and product);

**Defect.** With E_XXII on an empty V_XXII now given the exact value 0/1, both of S2's cases hold at once: "V_XXII is empty" and "E_XXII[vetoable sent] = 0 exactly". The verdict line names "for S2, its case" and does not say which. Under S3, the digest writes UNFITTED only for a figure that "reads an UNFITTED cell", but THE DECISION prints all of S1's and S2's figures UNFITTED. A total that happens to read no unfitted cell (for example e_sent_xxii when SO is unfitted and no V_XXII measure has an OP YEA) gets a rational in the digest and UNFITTED in the print.

**Evidence.** THE DECISION: "When S3 fires, S1 and S2 are not evaluated, and their figures print UNFITTED." S2's cases: "V_XXII is empty ... ; or ... E_XXII[vetoable sent] = 0 exactly".

**Fix proposed.** In S2, write: "case 'never sends' is evaluated only where V_XXII is non-empty". In the digest, write: "where outcome=S3, every e_* and p_none_xxii line is written =UNFITTED".

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: Both halves of the claim fail on checking. Neither changes the outcome or the digest, and neither was introduced or left half-fixed by the edits. (1) The two S2 cases overlapping on an empty V_XXII is not the edits' doing. S2's case lines (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1264-1266) are unchanged context in session_edits.diff (diff lines 264-266). Pre-edit, E_XXII was already "the same [sum] over V_XXII" and case 2 already said "(that is, P(NONE_XXII) = 1)" (plan:1238, 1266). So the empty sum 0 and the empty product 1 overlapped with case 1 in the panel's own text. The new digest line (plan:1499) only writes those values into the hash. Whichever case is named, the outcome is "S2" (plan:1273), and the digest records only outcome=S2 (plan:1496). The only text affected is the case label inside the printed STOPPED verdict line (plan:1296-1297). The block also points to case 1. Case 1's own parenthetical says that on an empty V_XXII "its figures print 'UNFITTED m=0'" (plan:1264-1265). TOO THIN says an unfitted figure prints with no value (plan:1119-1122), and S3 says no decision is computed on an unfitted figure (H4, plan:1259-1260). So case 2's "= 0 exactly" has nothing fitted to read there. (2) The print and the digest under S3 do not contradict each other. They are two outputs with two explicit, deterministic rules. The print: "When S3 fires ... their figures print UNFITTED" (plan:1260). The digest: "a figure that reads an UNFITTED cell is written key=UNFITTED" (plan:1498). Nothing in the block requires the digest to reproduce the printed values. "Not evaluated" refers to the stops, not to computing the figures, and every key is still defined in every run. The first review's defect was that "the digest has no defined content for those lines on an S3 run", with implementers diverging (confirmed.txt:90 and :128). That is now fixed: any implementer following plan:1497-1501 writes the same digest. The first review's own alternative fix was to "still compute them ... for the digest only" (confirmed.txt:91), and the session's rule follows that alternative for figures that read only fitted cells. So it is a deliberate design choice, not half a fix. The rational written in the reviewer's scenario is exact and changes no decision or registration.

### consistency-C2-12 (low; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.** yardstick, the identities at K = 0, INFLUENCE's k ("k of <distinct fitted roll calls>")

**Defect.** THE PRINT now requires INFLUENCE's k in the form "x of y", but the verdict line, which is generated, still prints it bare.

**Evidence.** Verdict line: "<FRAGILE: <k> roll calls each flip the outcome | no single roll call flips the outcome>".

**Fix proposed.** Verdict line: "<FRAGILE: <k> of <distinct fitted roll calls> roll calls each flip the outcome | ...>".

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: This is not a defect. The "x of y" form in THE PRINT (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1072-1076) describes how a NOT FITTED figure prints: plainly, with no FITTED label or interval. It is not a rule that must hold on every surface. Only the FITTED bullet carries the clause "It prints in the label below on EVERY surface" (lines 1067-1071), and the NOT FITTED bullet has nothing like it. The verdict line is its own exact template, "generated and never typed" (line 1284). It already prints other members of the same NOT FITTED list without a denominator: "(all <|V_XXII|> were sent on the record)" at line 1287, and "of the <V_COUNT> counted vetoes" at line 1290. Those counts were in the NOT FITTED list before the edits, and their bare form was never a contradiction. So the bare "<k> roll calls each flip the outcome" at line 1292 follows the template's existing pattern. It is also not a new conflict: the diff never touches the verdict line (no hunk contains "roll calls each flip"). The edit only moved k from FITTED to NOT FITTED so that it takes no BOOT label (confirmed.txt, computability-C9), and the verdict line already gives k none. Adding the "of <distinct fitted roll calls>" denominator there would at most be a style choice, not a fix for a contradiction.

### consistency-C2-13 (low; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.** its record row prints "0 counted vetoes; 2 declared unpredictable"

**Defect.** The per-president record row counts non-counted rows ("2 declared unpredictable"), but COUNTED ONLY's new exception list does not cover it. The list names only the UNPREDICTABLE set's derivation, its line, its override line, its digest entries and item 1's "in all" counts. THE OVERRIDE's "Every recorded override vote beside the model's" also reads the two sustained votes without any limit, even though TRUMP'S section confines them to "their own line only".

**Evidence.** COUNTED ONLY: "The exceptions ...: THE US-10 IDENTITIES ...; the UNPREDICTABLE set's own derivation over all rows, its line, its sustained override votes' line and its digest entries (by name); and THE FIRST RUN item 1's counts \"in all\"."

**Fix proposed.** Add "the per-president rows' 'declared unpredictable' counts" to the exceptions. In THE OVERRIDE, write: "Every recorded override vote of a counted measure beside the model's (the UNPREDICTABLE pair's on their own line)".

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: Refuted. The edited exception list already covers both cases, and the block reads as one consistent rule. (1) The record row. The row that prints "0 counted vetoes; 2 declared unpredictable" (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1086-1087, repeated at 1352-1353) is the term's row in THE FIRST RUN item 1, "THE RECORD per president: measures and vetoes, counted and in all. The UNPREDICTABLE line." (1202-1203). The edited COUNTED ONLY already exempts "THE FIRST RUN item 1's counts \"in all\"" (774-775). It also exempts "the UNPREDICTABLE set's own derivation over all rows, its line" (773-774). For that term, the "2 declared unpredictable" is the in-all vetoes minus the counted vetoes, and those two vetoes are exactly the UNPREDICTABLE set (763-764). So the count sits inside two exceptions that are already listed. It also enters no figure, denominator or decision, which is the condition the exceptions state (770). (2) The override line. "Every recorded override vote beside the model's" (1058) is panel text that the session did not edit; the diff's override hunk changes only the SIDES "Reason" sentence. It cannot pull in the two sustained votes, for two reasons: - The model computes an override (omega_j) only for measures of V (1055-1056, 1186-1187). TRUMP'S section puts H.R. 131 and H.R. 504 outside V (1348), so they have no model override for a recorded vote to sit beside. - TRUMP'S section says it outright: "not in the override's counts: their sustained votes print on their own line only" (1351). The edited exception names that same line, "its sustained override votes' line" (774). The exception list, the "only" and THE OVERRIDE's list therefore agree. Neither proposed fix adds anything the block does not already say. At most this is a low-severity wording preference, not a contradiction or a half-made fix.

### consistency-C2-14 (low; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.** "sNNN" and "sMMM" stand
for the COMPLETED.md section a later commit takes.

**Defect.** The new placeholder sentence leaves the mirror's spelling open. THE MIRROR's forms write "(sNNN)", but the plan's live mirror line outside the block writes "(§832)" and uses a NONE form that THE MIRROR does not define. "The instrument fails while the mirror and the record differ", so whether it should match "s833" or "§833" is undecided.

**Evidence.** Plan line 1558: "**Ruling record:** NONE (§832) - the instrument is not built; its first run registers the outcome." THE MIRROR lists only the INSTALLED-PENDING-E4, INSTALLED, ASKED and RULED forms, with "(sNNN)".

**Fix proposed.** Add: "In the mirror line, sNNN is written as the plan writes sections (§NNN), and the NONE state's mirror is the line the preregistering commit writes."

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: Refuted: this is not a defect, and the edits did not introduce it. (1) Nothing is left open about how the section is spelled. The block has to be plain ASCII (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1466, "in plain ASCII inside a ```text fence"), so "§" cannot appear inside it. "sNNN" is just the ASCII way of writing a section number. The edits apply the same convention in the same commit: inside the block they write "COMPLETED.md s832" (lines 1470, 1543) and "preregistered (s832)" (1473), and the lines outside the block write "(§832)" (1558, 1560). The plan's own header settles how a section is written outside a block (line 3: "A bare § number is a `COMPLETED.md` record"). The precedent is the same: R-US18's and R-US19's mirror lines read "ASKED (§800)" and "ASKED (§801)" (lines 1785, 2050), and the instrument for that precedent builds its mirror line with § (UsHouseCountCheck.cs:40 `RulingSection = "§800"`, :690). Its digest labels use § too: "preregistered (§791)" (:33). (2) The NONE form is not a gap. The block says "NONE: the state before the instrument exists. The instrument fails while it reads NONE, so the first-run commit registers the outcome" (1504-1505). The instrument therefore never compares a mirror in the NONE state. THE MIRROR's forms (1523-1527) cover exactly the states it can register: INSTALLED-PENDING-E4, INSTALLED, ASKED and RULED. Line 1558 is the preregistering commit's text, outside the block, and the block does not have to define it. (3) The edits did not cause this. The "(sNNN)" mirror forms are the panel's text and the diff leaves them unchanged. The added sentence ("sNNN and sMMM stand for the COMPLETED.md section a later commit takes", line 737-738) only says what the placeholders mean, and it agrees with every place they are used (1474, 1506-1510, 1524-1527). The first review raised no placeholder or mirror defect, so nothing here was left half-fixed. (4) It cannot change a decision. The first-run commit writes both the instrument and the line the instrument checks, and the mutation suite has a "mirror line changed" case (1538). Whichever spelling that commit uses, it is held consistent; the outcome cannot be decided wrongly and nothing becomes uncomputable.

### consistency-C2-3 (low; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.**    - In play the bill is on THE VETO PATH (play). On the record the measure is in V, under
     premise (B) (item 9), and outside the RECORD BUDGET CLASS.

**Defect.** The edit added "under (A) or in play" to item 2's no-draw list, but item 2's first bullet still limits every draw on the record to premise (B). Read literally, nothing draws under (A), so the new (A) exclusion is dead text, and so is R1's "with the draw". Item 2 now contradicts itself and R1.

**Evidence.** R1: "(A), chamber by chamber as recorded (s802's declared reading, with the draw and the Vice President), in place of (B)." Item 2's exclusion now names "(A)".

**Fix proposed.** Change to: "On the record the measure is in V, under premise (B) (item 9) or, in R1, under (A), and outside the RECORD BUDGET CLASS."

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: Refuted. The edit did not create dead text, and no new contradiction exists. (1) The DECLARED READINGS mechanism already covers this. USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1395 says each reading "runs the whole rule with one change", and R1 at :1398-1399 is "(A) ... with the draw and the Vice President), in place of (B)". Under R1, item 2's "under premise (B) (item 9)" (:865-866) therefore reads as (A). Draws then happen under (A), and the new no-draw clause "under (A) or in play, a PP standing AGAINST in a chamber where the OP also stands AGAINST (UNCONTESTED)" (:869-870) applies. It is live text, and it agrees with R1's "with the draw". (2) The block treats (A) as a drawing premise in several places the edit did not touch. Item 9 (:903-904) says "R1, reading (A) chamber by chamber, is printed beside it". The Vice President "votes under (B), under R1 and in play" (:979). Draws enter (A)'s margin tests through Y = Y0 + D (:964, :971). (3) The (A) wording is not new. The panel's own UNCONTESTED paragraph already said "This applies off V, under (A) and in play" (diff line 154, unchanged in substance at :990-991). The edit only copied that existing exclusion into item 2's list, which is the fix the first review asked for (confirmed.txt, consistency-IC-1). (4) Item 2's first bullet, "On the record the measure is in V, under premise (B) (item 9)", is a context line in the diff (diff line 87). R1's "with the draw" is also panel text. So any tension between those two existed before the edits, and the edits neither introduced it nor half-fixed it. Being in V is the same test under (A): a measure with a PP-NAY roll call is in V (:836-837), and chambers where the PP is not NAY fall under UNCONTESTED. The proposed fix ("or, in R1, under (A)") repeats what R1's "in place of (B)" already says. It is optional polish, not a defect.

### facts-E6 (low; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.** - S1, FAR MORE (R-US17's first STOP as US-10 read it before its first run, s802,
  PROVISIONAL under G5;

**Defect.** The reading is right, but the citation for 'before its first run' only half holds. COMPLETED.md §802 lists '"Far more" is a pooled precision below B1's (PROVISIONAL, G5)' under 'Departures, declared', and does not say it was read before the first run. That statement is in Tools/us_veto_backtest.pl (lines 28-29, 'DECLARED BEFORE THE FIRST RUN') and in docs/generated/US_VETO_BACKTEST.md line 103. Also, US-10's stop applies the threshold to EVERY rule (@far == @RULES); S1 applies it to (c) alone.

**Evidence.** COMPLETED.md line 37953 (§802 Departures list); Tools/us_veto_backtest.pl lines 28-29 and 230; docs/generated/US_VETO_BACKTEST.md line 103.

**Fix proposed.** "(R-US17's first STOP with US-10's threshold, declared before its first run in us_veto_backtest.pl and US_VETO_BACKTEST.md and recorded in s802, PROVISIONAL under G5; applied here to (c) alone; ...)"

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: The citation holds. In this block, "s802" names US-10's session and record. It is not a pointer to the wording of COMPLETED.md §802. The block uses the same form throughout: "US-10's record (s802)" at USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:772 and :1229, "the record of s802" at :1182 (it names us_veto_backtest.pl and US_VETO_BACKTEST.md as that record), and "s802's declared reading" at :1398. Read that way, "before its first run" is backed by three sources: - s802's own tool: Tools/us_veto_backtest.pl:28-29, "THE READING OF "FAR MORE" ... DECLARED BEFORE THE FIRST RUN (G5, PROVISIONAL)". - Its generated document: docs/generated/US_VETO_BACKTEST.md:103, "declared before the first run, PROVISIONAL". - The plan's own US-10 entry at USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:287: ""far more" read, before the first run, as a pooled precision below B1's on Poland (PROVISIONAL, G5)". COMPLETED.md §802 (line ~37953) files "Far more" under "Departures, declared". That does not contradict the timing. The edited text matches the first review's confirmed fix form-F5 word for word, and that review gave §802 and US-10's Built line as its sources. The second complaint does not hold either. US-10 applied the threshold across every rule (us_veto_backtest.pl:227,230, @far == @RULES), while S1 applies it to (c) alone. But under H1-H3, R-US17 is ruled down to the single rule (c), so "every rule" in the block can only mean that one rule. S1 also names R-US17's per-rule precision test ("(a) only if its precision ... is at least B1's") as a second source. In any case "R-US17's first STOP" was already in the panel's text and the edit did not introduce it. Nothing is wrong or half-fixed here.

### facts-E7 (low; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.** otherwise the president of record from WorldClock.TryGovernmentAt (ExecutiveKind
  Presidency; PP its party, REP or DEM).

**Defect.** The sources check out (WorldClock's US rows are Presidency kind, Biden 2021-01-20 to 2025-01-20 and Trump from 2025-01-20 with no end date; PresidencyOfRecord.Of is Poland-only). Two gaps remain. GovernmentOfRecord has no party field: the party is Cabinet[0] ('Cabinet holds that one party'). For the game-seated branch, the block does not name where PP comes from (PresidencyOfRecord.President.BackingParty, set from the contest's ElectedParty). A game president backed by a third or created party gets a PP that is not R or D, and 'OP is whichever of R and D is not the PP' is then undefined.

**Evidence.** WorldClock.cs lines 52-73 (fields), 64 (Cabinet holds the one party), 404-408 (US rows); PresidentialElection.cs lines 151-163 (game president built with sitting.ElectedParty as BackingParty); PresidencyOfRecord.cs line 49.

**Fix proposed.** "... from WorldClock.TryGovernmentAt (Kind Presidency; PP = its Cabinet[0], REP or DEM); for a game-seated president PP = President.BackingParty. A PP other than REP or DEM is treated as no PP (no draw, (a) never fires), DECLARED."

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: Refuted. Two of the three parts are not defects, and the third was already in the panel's text, so it falls outside this pass (defects the edits introduced or left half-fixed). (1) "PP its party" for the record branch is unambiguous. WorldClock.cs:64 documents the field: "a PRESIDENCY (the USA: the president and their party ARE the government of record, and Cabinet holds that one party)". The only US rows hold exactly one party each: WorldClock.cs:406 new[] { "DEM" } for Biden and :407 new[] { "REP" } for Trump, both kind Presidency. "Its party" can only mean that one Cabinet entry. A spec does not have to name the struct field, and there is no second reading to mis-decide on. (2) The game-seated branch does say where PP comes from. The PRESIDENT entry defines PP as the president's party (plan:782, "the row's president and president_party"). Plan:789 reads that same party for the game branch: "A president found with no party has no PP". PresidentialElection.cs:161-162 builds the game president with sitting.ElectedParty as BackingParty, the President struct's only party field (PresidencyOfRecord.cs:27). Only one field can be meant. (3) A third-party or created-party PP leaving "OP is whichever of R and D is not the PP" non-unique is a real gap in principle, but the edits did not make it. The diff shows the OP definition as unchanged context (session_edits.diff:52). The panel's game branch, "In play the president is the one of the vote's day (PresidentialElection.PresidentAt)" (diff:54-55), already allowed any party. The edit only added the record fallback, and its "REP or DEM" is a true statement about the record rows (WorldClock.cs:406-407). The confirmed defect being fixed (confirmed.txt:2-4) was the missing record president and "no president" vs "no party". The edit fixes both, so nothing it touched is half-fixed. The block also covers the case in part. Plan:871 says a third or created party makes no draw ("it votes its bloc"). Plan:1022 says "A president whose party holds no House seat vetoes nothing". Plan:1031 says a player-president is not read by (a). A US game president at all needs US-8, which US-21 does not depend on (plan:443). The reviewer's fix ("a PP other than REP or DEM is treated as no PP") would also clash with plan:1031-1032, "Congress's draw is the same whoever is president". If the third-party OP question is worth settling, it should go to the panel-text review as its own item, not count as a defect from these edits.

### facts-E8 (low; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.** - UsVeto.cs lands in Assets/Editor beside the instrument, and the catalog in
  Assets/Editor/Generated, until US-21's gate first calls them; US-21 then moves both into
  Assets/Scripts/Elections (and its Generated folder) byte for byte, asserted by blob id.

**Defect.** The order of events is reversed. Runtime code (the gate in SimulationManager) cannot reference a class compiled in the Editor assembly, so the gate cannot 'first call them' while they sit in Assets/Editor and 'then' move them. The UnwiredSubsystemCheck fact itself is right: it enumerates Assets/Scripts only and treats every Assets/Editor file as a harness (IsHarness), so both files stay out of its counts.

**Evidence.** UnwiredSubsystemCheck.cs lines 105, 141-142, 263-264, 377-379 (only Assets/Scripts declarations are counted; Editor files are harnesses); no asmdef in the project, so Assets/Editor compiles into the Editor assembly.

**Fix proposed.** "... in Assets/Editor/Generated until US-21; US-21 moves both into Assets/Scripts/Elections (and its Generated folder) byte for byte, asserted by blob id, in the commit whose gate first calls them."

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: The facts the reviewer cites are correct, but the order of events is not reversed, so this is not a defect. Facts I checked: - The project has no .asmdef files, so Assets/Editor compiles into the Editor assembly. - UnwiredSubsystemCheck.cs only counts declarations under Assets/Scripts. The checks at lines 141-142 and 263-264 skip any file outside it, and IsHarness at lines 377-379 returns true for any path outside Assets/Scripts. - So runtime code cannot call UsVeto while it sits in Assets/Editor. Why the sentence is still right: USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1173-1175 says the files sit in Assets/Editor "until US-21's gate first calls them; US-21 then moves both". In "stays in A until E; X then does Y", the word "then" means "at that point". So the sentence names the commit where the stay ends: US-21, the one whose gate first calls them. It does not say the gate calls them while they are still in Editor. Under that reading, the reviewer's suggested rewording ("moves both ... in the commit whose gate first calls them") means the same thing. The rest of the block places the move the same way, so nothing contradicts it: - 1364-1365: catalog "written ... into Assets/Editor/Generated and moved into Assets/Scripts/Elections/Generated at US-21" - 1378-1379: "in Assets/Editor until US-21's move ... stays unwired until US-21" - 1548-1549: "UsVeto.cs and the catalog in Assets/Editor until US-21" Nothing can be built in the wrong order, because a gate calling an Editor-assembly class will not compile. So no result is decided wrongly and no real ambiguity remains. The edit correctly carries out option (ii) of confirmed defect game-F1 (confirmed.txt:9). At most this is a matter of style, not a clarity defect.

### facts-E9 (low; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.** by the
  20th Amendment's noon of 20 January (the rule records_by_date.md applies), D before
  2017-01-20, R from 2017-01-20 to 2021-01-19, D from 2021-01-20 to 2025-01-19, R from
  2025-01-20.

**Defect.** The dates are right and match WorldClock's rows. But the line cites the noon rule and then assigns all of 20 January to the incoming president: a roll call that morning belongs to the outgoing one under the cited rule. records_by_date.md also covers 2021 and 2025 only (its window), not 2017.

**Evidence.** records_by_date.md lines 152-153, 257-258, 276 (noon; window from 2022-07-01, Biden and Trump rows only); WorldClock.cs 406-407 (date-granular From).

**Fix proposed.** Add: "(date-granular, as the CSV's dates and WorldClock's rows are: the whole of 20 January goes to the incoming president, DECLARED; 2017's date is the Amendment applied, not a records_by_date.md row)."

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: This is not a defect. The line gives its operative boundaries as explicit dates (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:792-794: "D before 2017-01-20, R from 2017-01-20 to 2021-01-19, D from 2021-01-20 to 2025-01-19, R from 2025-01-20"). Nothing is left for a reader to decide or compute differently. The noon clause only says where the boundary day comes from. A morning-of-20-January split cannot be computed anyway. Roll-call identities are date-only (plan:777, "<that final date yyyy-MM-dd>"), and the block reads nothing else of the record, so no time of day is available. The whole-day convention is also the project's standing reading, stated in the same words for decisions. veto_record.md:58 says "The terms change at noon on 20 January (the 20th Amendment, as `records_by_date.md` derives Biden's last day); its §2 table begins in 2021". veto_record.md:60 says "A decision dated 20 January is the incoming president's". WorldClock.cs:406-407 is date-granular in the same way (Trump from D(2025,1,20), next to the comment "noon 2025-01-20"). So the line matches both the CSV's decision-president reading and the in-play path (plan:786, WorldClock.TryGovernmentAt). Nothing contradicts it. The second half of the claim is not a false reference either. "(the rule records_by_date.md applies)" points to the 20th Amendment rule, which records_by_date.md quotes and applies (records_by_date.md:153, 263; G11 at :246). It does not claim that records_by_date.md has a 2017 row. veto_record.md:58 uses the same citation and itself notes that the table begins in 2021. Trump's first inauguration on 2017-01-20 is the same Amendment applied, and the reviewer agrees the dates are correct. The proposed fix would restate a convention the explicit dates already fix.

## The third pass - the second pass's fixes (`rus17c-edits-pass3`)

Two lenses (did each fix land whole; the whole block once more) read the block and the asked paragraph after the digests line; the same verification.

### Confirmed (4)

### landed-R3-2 (medium; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** **The first run does not wait on the answer** (G1, "run on the defaults as printed")

**Defect.** The asked paragraph after the digests line cites G1 as the authority for the first run not waiting. G1 was Elias's word on R-US18's seven and R-US19's eight choices (2026-10-06, installed §797), given before R-US17's rule (c) existed. The plan's own precedent for blind questions goes the other way: "The instrument is built once Elias has answered them or given his word to run on the defaults ... the figures, once on the card, would be in sight of any later answer" (§791). Not blocking is the block's own default (QUESTIONS FOR ELIAS: "never blocking the first run"). Elias has not ruled it, yet the paragraph presents it to him as something he already said.

**Evidence.** Plan line 1812: "Answered (Elias's ruling G1, 2026-10-06; installed §797): run on the defaults as printed - ... (R-US18's choice 4 and R-US19's B7 ...). Both instruments run as preregistered". Plan line 1803 (the §791 precedent: the instrument waits for answers or the word). Block QUESTIONS gives no ruling for not blocking.

**Fix proposed.** Reword: "**The first run does not wait on the answer** - a default of this block (QUESTIONS FOR ELIAS), not a ruling: unlike R-US18's and R-US19's choices, which waited for his word (G1), H3 asks only that the digest precede the first run. An answer that comes before it is written into the block by amendment with a new digest; one that comes after it is a choice changed after a result is seen (G1's last sentence, by analogy), and reported." The other way is to have the first run wait for his answer or his word, as §791 and §794 did, and change QUESTIONS' "never blocking the first run" to match.

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: Confirmed. The paragraph cites G1 as its authority for not waiting, and G1 does not cover R-US17. 1. The quote is at G:\UNITY\Projects\PoliSim\docs\specs\USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1574: "**The first run does not wait on the answer** (G1, "run on the defaults as printed")". 2. G1 was scoped to two other instruments. COMPLETED.md:37728 (§797) gives Elias's words as "G1 run R-US18 (§791) and R-US19 (§794) on the defaults as printed - a result never changes a choice". The plan's G1 lines say the same: line 1812 "Both instruments run as preregistered", and its twin at about line 2078. G1 was given on 2026-10-06. R-US17 was ruled to (c) on 2026-10-13 (H1-H3, §803, COMPLETED.md:37961-37967). Nothing in H1-H3 or §803 covers whether the first run waits; H3 only puts the digest before the first run (plan line 728). 3. The plan's own precedent goes the other way. At §791 and §794 the instrument waits for Elias's answers or his word to run on the defaults: plan line 1803 "The instrument is built once Elias has answered them or given his word to run on the defaults ... the figures, once on the card, would be in sight of any later answer", and COMPLETED.md:37598 and 37679 "The instrument waits on them". When the session did stretch G1 in §797, it said so: "the session's reading of G1 and G6 together, said here" (COMPLETED.md:37743). Line 1574 gives no such notice. 4. Inside the block, not blocking is only a default. QUESTIONS FOR ELIAS (line 1466) says "never blocking the first run" with no ruling behind it. The block cites G1 only for what happens to a choice changed after a result: THE FIRST RUN at lines 1193-1194 ("is reported to Elias (G1)") and QUESTIONS at line 1470 ("G1's case"). That narrower use is fair by analogy. Line 1574 is the only place that makes G1 the authority for the first run not waiting. The result: the text addressed to Elias tells him he already ruled something he never ruled, in a preregistration whose whole point is provenance. Medium severity fits. Fix: replace "(G1, "run on the defaults as printed")" with something like "- a default of this block (QUESTIONS FOR ELIAS), not a ruling: G1 ruled only R-US18's and R-US19's defaults, which waited for his word (§791, §794)". The rest of the sentence can stay ("one that comes after it is G1's case ... reported"), marked as by analogy. The other way to fix it is to have the first run wait for his answer or his word, as at §791 and §794, and change QUESTIONS' "never blocking the first run" to match.

**What was done.** Done (outside the block): the asked paragraph says the first run's not waiting is a default of this block, not a ruling of Elias's - R-US18's and R-US19's choices waited for his word (G1; s791, s794), H3 asks only that the digest precede the first run; an answer after it is a choice changed after a result is seen, G1's principle by analogy. The same is put right in COMPLETED.md s832, which had repeated the citation.

### landed-R3-3 (low; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** A single recorded crossing large enough to carry one measure past cloture then keeps S2 silent.

**Defect.** The asked paragraph's account of the default is incomplete, and it is the sentence Elias's answer will rest on. Under (B), P(sent_j) = P_H * P_S. Getting past cloture with positive probability keeps S2 silent only if the House also passes that measure with positive probability. Where the PP holds the House, that needs a PP crossing large enough in the House cell too. As written, the sentence makes the default sound more fragile than the rule defines it.

**Evidence.** Block, THE BACKTEST'S DECISION FIGURES: "P(sent_j) = P_H * P_S". S2: "E_XXII[vetoable sent] = 0 exactly (that is, P(NONE_XXII) = 1)". WHEN THE DRAW MATTERS, HOUSE (B): "passes iff 2*Y > W_H".

**Fix proposed.** Write: "A single measure of the record that the draw can carry through both chambers, its Senate past cloture, with any probability at all then keeps S2 silent."

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: CONFIRMED, severity low. The sentence is at USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1574, in the asked paragraph after the digests line: "A single recorded crossing large enough to carry one measure past cloture then keeps S2 silent." The block's own definitions do not support that as a sufficient condition. 1. Line 1160 defines P(sent_j) = P_H * P_S. Line 1278 says S2 fires iff E_XXII[vetoable sent] = 0 exactly. So S2 stays silent only if some j in V_XXII has P_H(j) > 0 AND P_S(j) > 0. Getting past cloture with positive probability gives only P_S(j) > 0 (line 985-992: cloture, then passage on the same draw). 2. Line 972-974, House (B): the House passes iff 2*Y > W_H, where Y = S_OP - Ko + Kp with the OP standing YEA. Where the PP holds the House, this needs Kp - Ko > W_H/2 - S_OP. That Kp comes from the HP cell, not SP (line 856-857, item 7 at line 893-894; the two are pooled only when n_own < 12). A large Senate observation therefore does not imply a large enough House one. 3. The block contains a clean counterexample without touching any data. Line 899-901 (premise (B)) takes the OP's stance from the House roll call. Where that stance is NAY, line 974 gives Y = Kp. The cap at line 879-880 gives Kp <= floor((S_PP-1)/2), so 2Y <= S_PP - 1 < W_H, and P_H(j) = 0 however far past cloture the Senate draw carries j. So "past cloture" is necessary but not sufficient. The sentence makes the default sound easier to keep silent, and so more fragile, than S2 as defined. Elias reads this sentence to choose among the readings. Why only low: the exact definition comes just before it in the same paragraph ("the expected number ... exactly 0 (equivalently, the chance that none is sent exactly 1)"). The block itself (S2 at 1273-1278, QUESTIONS FOR ELIAS at 1464-1471) is exact and does not repeat the loose gloss, so the preregistered digest is unaffected. Nothing in the block or plan already handles this; QUESTIONS FOR ELIAS contains no corrected version. FIX (plan text only, outside the fence, so no new digest is needed): replace the sentence with "A single measure of the record that the draw can carry through both chambers with any probability at all (in the Senate, past cloture; in a House the president's party holds, past a House majority too) then keeps S2 silent."

**What was done.** Done with whole-P3-3 (outside the block): the asked paragraph says a single measure the draw can carry through both chambers with any probability - past cloture in the Senate and, where the president's party holds the House, through the House too, on crossings recorded on other measures' roll calls - keeps S2 silent.

### landed-R3-5 (low; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** - The first run is on the block as preregistered, or as amended by Elias's answer to

**Defect.** Fix C2-6 widened THE FIRST RUN only to the QUESTIONS answer. The block allows other amendments that can come before the first run: a pin mismatch "is a DEFECT until an amendment naming Elias's ruling re-pins it", and carrying the override split "would be a prep change (re-pinned, by amendment)". A first run on a block re-pinned by a ruling before any result exists would break THE FIRST RUN's sentence as now written.

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: Confirmed, low severity. The fix for C2-6 landed only for the QUESTIONS answer. That names exactly two allowed states. Elsewhere the block allows other amendments that Elias makes by ruling, and none of them is tied to a time after the first run: - THE PINS, :757-758: "A run whose SourceDigest or prep blob differs from a pin is a DEFECT until an amendment naming Elias's ruling re-pins it." The first run is itself such a run. A failing instrument cannot land the first-run commit, so the first run that counts would be on the re-pinned block. - THE FIT, DATA, :916-917: carrying the override split is "a prep change (re-pinned, by amendment)". - UNPREDICTABLE, :767-768: "a DEFECT until the block is amended by a ruling". - WHAT STAYS AS IT IS, :1327-1328: the block expects the prep tool may change ("re-run whenever the prep tool changes"). - THE DIGEST LIST, :1485-1487 ("one entry per amendment naming Elias's ruling") and THE BARS, :1557 (RuleDigests first entry = preregistered) both allow such an entry before the first-run commit. - G1's sentence at :1193-1194 covers only a choice changed after a result is in sight. So a first run on a block re-pinned by Elias before any result exists is neither "as preregistered" nor "as amended by Elias's answer to QUESTIONS". A reviewer holding the first-run commit to THE FIRST RUN's sentence would reject it. That is the same failure the second pass confirmed as C2-6, now for the sibling case. Nothing in facts.md (H3 at :56, G1 at :60) rules out a re-pin before the run: H3 only asks for the digest before the first run, and an amended block still meets that. The reviewer's fix is sound: "The first run is on the block as preregistered, or as amended before it by Elias's rulings (his answer to QUESTIONS FOR ELIAS, a re-pin), the RuleDigests list's last entry (H1, H3)." The plan paragraph at :1574 and QUESTIONS at :1466-1470 agree with each other and are not affected.

**What was done.** Done (in the block, the last edit before the digest): the first run is on the block as preregistered, or as amended before it by Elias's rulings (his answer to QUESTIONS FOR ELIAS, a re-pin), the RuleDigests list's last entry.

### whole-P3-3 (low; upheld 1 of 1)

**Quote.** A single recorded crossing large enough to carry one measure past cloture then keeps S2 silent.

**Defect.** The sentence explains the asked choice to Elias, and under the block's own arithmetic it is not true in general. S2 ('never sends') stays silent iff E_XXII > 0, that is iff for some j in V_XXII both P_H(j) > 0 and P_S(j) > 0. In a Congress where the PP holds the House (115th R 235-197 under Trump, 117th D 218-213 under Biden), the House test under (B), 2*Y > W_H with Y = S_OP - Ko + Kp, also needs a PP crossing, so one Senate crossing is not enough. Under LOO the crossing must also come from a roll call of another measure, not j's own. Elias is asked to rule on this framing, so it should match the rule.

**Evidence.** WHEN THE DRAW MATTERS, HOUSE (B): "passes iff 2*Y > W_H ... With the OP standing YEA, Y = S_OP - Ko + Kp". THE FIT, LOO: "measure j's draws in the backtest read every cell minus every observation of every roll call final for j". S2: "E_XXII[vetoable sent] = 0 exactly". The seats come from the brief's facts. No figure was computed.

**Fix proposed.** Write: "A recorded crossing on another measure's roll call large enough to carry one such measure past cloture (and, where the president's party holds the House, one carrying it through the House too) then keeps S2 silent."

**The skeptics:**

- UPHELD: Confirmed, low severity. The sentence at USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1574, "A single recorded crossing large enough to carry one measure past cloture then keeps S2 silent", says a Senate crossing is enough on its own. The block's own arithmetic says it is not. 1. What silences S2. S2 fires on E_XXII[vetoable sent] = 0 exactly (block line 1278). P(sent_j) = P_H * P_S (line 1160). So S2 stays silent only if some measure j of V_XXII has a nonzero chance in BOTH chambers. 2. The House test under (B). It passes iff 2*Y > W_H, with Y = S_OP - Ko + Kp when the OP stands YEA (lines 972-974). With every K = 0, Y = S_OP. Where the president's party holds the House, S_OP < S_PP, so 2*S_OP < W_H and the House fails without a PP crossing. The brief's seats (facts.md:127-128) show the PP holding the House in three of the five Congresses: 115th R 235-197, 117th D 218-213 and 119th R 218-214, all under a same-party president. 3. A concrete case. This is arithmetic on seats only, not a figure of the rule. Cloture needs floor((3W+4)/5) = 60 of 100 (line 986). Per Congress that means Kp - Ko >= 11 (115th), 12 (116th), 10 (117th), 11 (118th) and 13 (119th), carried by K = round(share*S) (line 880). A Senate crossing share between 20% and about 21.6% therefore clears cloture only for a 117th measure. A 117th measure is in a House the PP holds, where it also needs Kp - Ko >= 3 (2Y > 431). So a crossing "large enough to carry one measure past cloture" can exist while S2 still fires. 4. The leave-one-out rule (lines 958-959) adds a smaller gap. Measure j never draws from its own roll calls, so the crossing has to come from another measure's roll call. 5. Not already handled. The sentence before it gives the exact reading ("expected number ... exactly 0"), but this sentence then glosses it wrongly. The block's QUESTIONS FOR ELIAS section (lines 1466-1472) does not repeat or correct the gloss. The paragraph is outside the hashed block, so the rule itself is sound, but this is the text Elias rules from (it is the C2-5 fix). Fix: replace the sentence with "A recorded crossing on another measure's roll call large enough to carry one such measure past cloture (and, where the president's party holds the House, a House crossing large enough to carry it through the House too) then keeps S2 silent." A simpler accurate alternative: "S2 then stays silent as soon as any one vetoable Rule XXII measure has a nonzero chance of passing both chambers under the draw."

**What was done.** Done: as landed-R3-3.

**Quote.** - DIAGNOSTIC (not a fitted figure's print; for US-21's assertion only): the full-cell,

"Not a fitted figure's print" can be read two ways: that they are not fitted figures, or that they are fitted figures printed some other way. On the second reading they break the EVERY-surface clause and also H2 as ruled ("every fitted figure prints its n and an interval beside it, labelled FITTED n=..."), in the document that goes to Elias whole (E4).

**Evidence.** THE PRINT, first bullet ("any number computed from the cells' observations ... on EVERY surface: the document") against the DIAGNOSTIC bullet ("written as reduced rationals in their own section of US_VETO_DRAW.md ... with no label or interval"). H2 at plan line 727: "every fitted figure prints its n and an interval beside it".

**Fix proposed.** Make the carve-out explicit and keep H2. In THE PRINT's first bullet, after "every in-game chance", add "(the DIAGNOSTIC class below excepted from the form, not from H2)". Then make DIAGNOSTIC: "... written as reduced rationals, each followed by THE LABEL's derived form '<value> FITTED n=<n> m=1 95% [<lo>, <hi>] BOOT in-sample', its replicate figures scored from the replicate's cells with nothing removed, in their own section ... on no other surface." If an interval for these is unwanted, the alternative is to take them out of the document: have the instrument hand them to US-21's diagnostic through the catalog or a test fixture, and drop "US-21's diagnostic reads them there".

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: Refuted: the fix landed, and the carve-out is explicit. THE PRINT (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1071-1086) is a list of classes. Each later class takes certain numbers out of the general FITTED FIGURE rule by its own header and states how they print instead. DRAWN (:1081-1082) shows the pattern. Its seeded realizations run "on the LOO lists" (:1163), so they too are literally "computed from the cells' observations". Yet the block calls them "(not fitted; a check)" and prints them in DRAWN FORM (:1133-1134), not THE LABEL. DIAGNOSTIC (:1083-1086) is a fourth class built the same way. It names exactly these numbers ("the full-cell, in-sample P(sent_j) and P(named_j) of every measure of V") and gives their whole form: "reduced rationals in their own section of US_VETO_DRAW.md after item 3, with no label or interval, and on no other surface". The specific clause governs the general one. Both readings of "not a fitted figure's print" give the same document, so two implementers cannot build it two ways. THE FIRST RUN points to the class consistently: :1197-1198 says "Beside them, never deciding: the DIAGNOSTIC full-cell figures (THE PRINT)", and item 3 at :1222 says "Then the DIAGNOSTIC section (THE PRINT)". US-21's assertion at :1403-1404 reads them as "the same rationals (on the full cells)". The bullet is close to verbatim the fix that pass 2 confirmed for facts-E4 (pass2.json:151), and that pass's verifier called it "sound" (pass2.json:145); C2-8's verifier said a p/q class "with no label or interval" "works". The H2 objection (plan :727) is a policy reading, not an inconsistency. These figures are a reproduction fixture for US-21. They never decide (:1197) and are confined to one section. The same measures' LOO P(sent) and P(named) print in THE LABEL in item 3 (:1219-1222), so every figure presented as a fit result still carries its n and interval. The proposed fix would also add new spec: an in-sample BOOT replicate rule that the block nowhere defines. I am not certain this is a defect, so per the brief I refute.

### whole-P3-1 (medium; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.** - A FITTED FIGURE is any number computed from the cells' observations, under the default
  or any reading: the cell figures, every per-measure probability that reads a cell, every

  any reply.

**Defect.** The C2-8/E4 fix added the DIAGNOSTIC class but did not carve it out of the FITTED FIGURE definition. The full-cell P(sent_j) and P(named_j) are still 'per-measure probabilit[ies] that read a cell', so by this sentence they are FITTED and must print in the label on 'EVERY surface: the document'. The new DIAGNOSTIC entry prints them in that same document 'with no label or interval'. It calls them 'not a fitted figure's print', which reads as 'a fitted figure printed without its label'. DRAWN escapes the definition by saying '(not fitted; a check)'. DIAGNOSTIC says nothing that clear. Without an explicit exception the block contradicts itself inside THE PRINT. It also sits uneasily with H2 ('every fitted figure prints its n and an interval beside it').

**Evidence.** THE PRINT, lines 1071-1075 against 1083-1086: "DIAGNOSTIC (not a fitted figure's print; for US-21's assertion only): the full-cell, in-sample P(sent_j) and P(named_j) of every measure of V, written as reduced rationals in their own section of US_VETO_DRAW.md after item 3, with no label or interval, and on no other surface." THE FIRST RUN, line 1197, and item 3, line 1222, point to that section.

**Fix proposed.** In A FITTED FIGURE's sentence write "... and every in-game chance, except the DIAGNOSTIC rationals below (a check, as DRAWN is). It prints in the label below on EVERY surface ...", and change the DIAGNOSTIC entry to "DIAGNOSTIC (not fitted; a check, for US-21's assertion only): ...". If Elias's H2 is read to cover them anyway, print each one with "n=<n> m=1, in-sample check, no interval" instead.

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: Refuted: the exception is already stated inside THE PRINT, and the block makes other exceptions the same way. At G:\UNITY\Projects\PoliSim\docs\specs\USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1083-1086 the DIAGNOSTIC entry says how its figures print: "(not a fitted figure's print; for US-21's assertion only) ... written as reduced rationals in their own section of US_VETO_DRAW.md after item 3, with no label or interval, and on no other surface." That is a specific exception to the general sentence at 1071-1075, and it leaves no second way to build it. The reviewer says DRAWN escapes the definition by its own wording. It does not. DRAWN's figures are also "any number computed from the cells' observations": the realizations run on the LOO lists (1162-1163), and their means estimate the expected counts that 1071-1073 name as FITTED. DRAWN escapes only through its own entry at 1081 ("(not fitted; a check)"). The definition sentence carves it out no more than it carves out DIAGNOSTIC. So "general definition, exception stated in the class's own entry" is how the block already works, and DIAGNOSTIC follows it. "Not a fitted figure's print" plainly switches off the label rule for this output, and the entry then gives the form. Every other place the term is used agrees: THE FIRST RUN 1196-1198 ("Beside them, never deciding: the DIAGNOSTIC full-cell figures (THE PRINT)"), item 3 at 1222 ("Then the DIAGNOSTIC section (THE PRINT)"), and US-21's assertion at 1403-1404 ("the same rationals (on the full cells)"). The text that landed is almost word for word the fix the second pass confirmed (confirmed2.txt:61, facts-E4). So the C2-8/E4 fix landed as confirmed. The H2 point (727: "every fitted figure prints its n and an interval beside it") is not cured by the reviewer's main fix either: calling them "not fitted; a check" changes nothing about how the figures are made. That question re-opens a design the second pass accepted. It is not a contradiction this fix introduced. What remains is at most a low-severity polish: optionally add "except the DIAGNOSTIC rationals" to the sentence at 1071-1075. It is not a medium defect.

**Quote.** its first entry ("preregistered (s832)"), then one entry per amendment naming Elias's
    ruling ("AMENDED (Hn, sNNN): old -> new").

**Defect.** Since the C2-6 fix, the expected first amendment is Elias's answer to QUESTIONS FOR ELIAS (S2's reading), written in before the first run. That answer is a new ruling, not one of H1-H3, and its letter is unknown. R-US18's list, for example, records 'AMENDED (G1, §797)'. The fixed form 'Hn' either forces a mislabel or makes the instrument's list format reject the amendment the block itself foresees.

**Evidence.** QUESTIONS FOR ELIAS: "An answer that comes before the first run is written in by amendment with a new digest". THE FIRST RUN: "or as amended by Elias's answer to QUESTIONS FOR ELIAS before it (the RuleDigests list's last entry)". The plan's R-US18 digests line: "AMENDED (G1, §797)".

**Fix proposed.** Write: "then one entry per amendment naming Elias's ruling (\"AMENDED (<ruling>, sNNN): old -> new\", e.g. H4 or the answer to QUESTIONS by its recorded name)".

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: "Hn" is an example placeholder, not a fixed label format, and the claimed consequence (a forced mislabel or a rejected amendment) cannot happen. (1) The rule itself is "one entry per amendment naming Elias's ruling" (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1485-1486), and the block points to "R-US18's form" for the list (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1483). (2) R-US18 sets the precedent. Its prose template reads "AMENDED (Fn): old -> new" (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1583), but the amendment it actually recorded is "AMENDED (G1, §797)" (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1801), and R-US19 recorded "AMENDED (G1-G3, §797)" (line 2066). So a letter in these templates has never limited which ruling gets named; the real ruling's name is written in. (3) The built instruments keep each entry's label as a free string that nothing parses. UsHouseCountCheck.cs:31-35 declares `(string Digest, string Why)[] RuleDigests` with Why = "AMENDED (G1, §797): choice 4 answered ...", and the only check on the list (UsHouseCountCheck.cs:582) compares the block's digest with the last entry's Digest. Why is only printed. UsSenateRaceCheck.cs:35 is the same. So no "list format" exists that could reject an answer recorded under a new ruling name such as I1. (4) H4 and H5 are already taken (lines 1814, 2115-2116), so an answer to QUESTIONS FOR ELIAS would come in a new series, and the template's literal letter could be slightly confusing. That is the same cosmetic looseness R-US18's "Fn" carried without harm, not a contradiction with QUESTIONS FOR ELIAS (line 1469) or THE FIRST RUN (lines 1191-1192), which both refer to the amendment by what it records, not by a label.

## The amendment of §833 - S2 read in the typical world (`rus17c-amend833-review`)

One finder read the amendment's diff, the block and the plan's lines after it; the same verification.

### Confirmed (0)

### Refuted (5)

### F1 (medium; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.** then one entry per amendment naming Elias's
    ruling ("AMENDED (Hn, sNNN): old -> new").

**Defect.** The block requires every RuleDigests entry for an amendment to name an H-numbered ruling. Elias's answer on S2's reading has no H number, so the entry the first-run commit must write for s833 cannot take the form the block prescribes. The plan's digests line works around this with a different label: "AMENDED (Elias's answer on S2's reading, 2026-10-16, §833)". The R-US18 and R-US19 precedents each name a ruling id ("AMENDED (G1, §797)", "AMENDED (G1-G3, §797)").

**Evidence.** Block line 1489 (THE DIGEST LIST) against plan line 1575 (the digests line) and lines 1806 and 2071 (the precedents). No part of the amendment touches the DIGEST LIST bullet.

**Fix proposed.** Either give the answer an id (for example H5, recorded in COMPLETED.md s833 and used in both the digests line and the Answered line), or widen the block's form to "AMENDED (<Elias's ruling or answer>, sNNN): old -> new". Either way the edit belongs in the same s833 amendment, so the block digest has to be recomputed and the digests line's new digest replaced.

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: This is not a defect. "Hn" in the DIGEST LIST bullet (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1487-1489) is a placeholder for whatever names Elias's ruling. It is not a required H-numbered id, for three reasons. (1) The block itself counts this kind of answer as one of Elias's rulings. Line 1192-1193 reads: "as amended before it by Elias's rulings (his answer to QUESTIONS FOR ELIAS, a re-pin), the RuleDigests list's last entry". So the block expected an answer to QUESTIONS FOR ELIAS to be the amending ruling, and that answer was never going to carry an H number. The digests line's label at line 1575, "AMENDED (Elias's answer on S2's reading, 2026-10-16, §833)", names exactly that ruling. (2) The precedent already uses the template loosely. R-US18's preregistration prescribes "each naming Elias's ruling ("AMENDED (Fn): old -> new")" (line 1588). Its actual entry is "AMENDED (G1, §797)" (line 1806), and in code it is "AMENDED (G1, §797): choice 4 answered - reading (vi) printed only" (Assets/Editor/UsHouseCountCheck.cs:34). The letter differs, a section was added and free text replaces old -> new. R-US19's entry follows the same pattern (UsSenateRaceCheck.cs:36). The R-US18 and R-US19 precedents the claim cites therefore show the form being adapted, not followed literally. (3) Nothing parses the label. RuleDigests is a (Digest, Why) array, and Why only appears in the check's message (UsHouseCountCheck.cs:582, UsSenateRaceCheck.cs:476). The first-run commit can write "AMENDED (Elias's answer on S2's reading, s833): ..." without breaking anything. The proposed fix would also cause harm. H5 is already taken by "H5, the order" (line 2121; the H1-H5 batch of 2026-10-13, with H4 at line 1819). Widening the block's form would edit the block beyond Elias's answer, against the "nothing else changed" requirement, and force a new digest for wording that does not need to change.

### F2 (low; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.**   - case "no Rule XXII measure on the veto path": V_XXII is empty (its figures print
    "UNFITTED m=0"); or
  - case "never sends": 2 * P(NONE_XXII) >= 1, that is, the chance that no measure of
    V_XXII is sent is at least one half.

**Defect.** When V_XXII is empty, P(NONE_XXII) is the empty product, 1/1. The block says so itself: "p_none_xxii=1/1 (the empty sum and product)". So 2 * 1 >= 1 and both S2 cases are true at once. The STOPPED verdict prints "for S2, its case", but nothing says which case to name. The old "= 0 exactly" case overlapped in the same way, so the amendment did not create this. It was simply a chance to fix it, and the review was asked to check the empty case.

**Evidence.** Block lines 1278-1281 (S2), 1311-1312 (the STOPPED verdict, "for S2, its case") and 1514 (empty product = 1/1). The empty case still fires S2 correctly; only the case label is ambiguous.

**Fix proposed.** Make the second case exclusive, for example: "case \"never sends\": V_XXII is non-empty and 2 * P(NONE_XXII) >= 1". Alternatively, state that the first case takes precedence when both hold.

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: This is not a defect in the amendment, and it changes nothing that the rule decides. (1) The overlap was there before the amendment. The diff (amend833.diff, hunk @@ -540) only swaps the second case's test, "E_XXII[vetoable sent] = 0 exactly (that is, P(NONE_XXII) = 1)", for "2 * P(NONE_XXII) >= 1". Over an empty V_XXII both tests are true, because USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1514 gives "e_sent_xxii=0/1 and p_none_xxii=1/1". The claim admits this itself ("the amendment did not create this"). The review's own test is "nothing else changed", so an exclusivity clause Elias never ruled on is outside an amendment meant to write in only his answer. (2) The empty case still reads correctly: S2 fires (plan:1278-1279). Which case gets named reaches nothing decisive. THE OUTCOME is just "S2" (plan:1288). The decisive digest records only "outcome=INSTALL|S3|S1|S2|S1+S2" and has no case key (plan:1511). The ruling record and the mirror name only the outcome (plan:1541). The case shows up only in the generated verdict-line text (plan:1311-1312). (3) Even that label has a natural reading. The cases are an ordered list with the specific one first, "no Rule XXII measure on the veto path" (plan:1278). Its parenthetical says V_XXII's figures print "UNFITTED m=0", and plan:1131 says "a derived figure with m = 0 prints 'UNFITTED m=0'". So in the printed figures the verdict line quotes, P(NONE_XXII) has no value in the empty world, and the line can only cite the first case's "UNFITTED m=0". The mutation suite's "S2 (both cases)" (plan:1555) can still prove each path: a planted non-empty V_XXII hits only the second case. At most this is a cosmetic ambiguity that predates the amendment, and uncertain counts as refuted.

### F3 (low; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.** By default it is read as the words have it: the expected number of the record's vetoable measures sent under Rule XXII exactly 0 (equivalently, the chance that none is sent exactly 1). ... Reading R13 prints both on every run. **The first run does not wait on the answer**

**Defect.** The task says the Asked paragraph was updated, but the working-tree diff leaves it unchanged. Several of its present-tense statements are now false. The default is no longer the words' own reading. R13 no longer prints "both" of the alternatives it lists (>= 1/2 and < 1); it now prints = 0 and < 1. The Answered line below it corrects these, but the paragraph does not say it has been superseded.

**Evidence.** git diff -U0 on docs/specs/USA_STAGE_PLAN.md changes only lines 734, 1275-1280, 1438-1440, 1470-1475 and 1575, and adds 1579-1580. Line 1577 (Asked) is unchanged.

**Fix proposed.** Start the Asked paragraph with "(Superseded by the answer below, §833.)" or put its statements in the past tense ("By default it was read ...; Reading R13 printed both ..."). This is outside the block, so the digest does not move.

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: The diff evidence is right: line 1577 (the Asked paragraph) is unchanged. But leaving it unchanged is not a defect. It matches how this plan records every question it asks. 1. The paragraph is a dated record of the question. Its heading is "**Asked blind, before the first run (§832).**" (G:\UNITY\Projects\PoliSim\docs\specs\USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1577). The statements the claim calls false ("By default it is read as the words have it", "Reading R13 prints both on every run", "The first run does not wait on the answer") all describe the block as it stood at §832. They were true then. 2. The plan never rewrites an Asked paragraph once it is answered. It adds an Answered paragraph straight after it.    - R-US18 (line 1808): "**Asked blind, before the first run (§791).** ... The instrument is built once Elias has answered them or given his word to run on the defaults". This is still present tense, although line 1817 records the answer, "**Answered (Elias's ruling G1, 2026-10-06; installed §797):**".    - R-US19 has the same pair at lines 2073 and 2083.    The §832/§833 pair at lines 1577 and 1579 follows the same pattern. Changing 1577 to past tense, or tagging it "superseded", would break that convention. 3. The supersession is already stated on the next line. Line 1579 says the amendment was "Written into the block by amendment, the digest moved as listed above. Reading R13 now prints the words' own reading (the expected count exactly 0, the preregistered default) and the expected count below one beside it. The first run is on the amended block." Each of the three statements the claim objects to is corrected there, and the word "now" marks it as replacing what came before. The digests line (1575) also gives the AMENDED §833 digest. 4. The block itself is consistent with the answer: S2 at block line 1280, R13 at 1438-1440, and QUESTIONS FOR ELIAS at 1470-1475 ("None open ..."). The task text did say the Asked paragraph "was updated", and that is inaccurate. But that is a slip in how the orchestrator described the change, not a defect in the plan or the amendment. This is a dated historical record, already superseded by the paragraph right after it, kept the way the plan keeps the others.

### F4 (low; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.** - INSTALL: "By R-US17's rule (c) as ruled (H1-H3), the draw installs:

**Defect.** Since s833, S2's threshold, and therefore whether the outcome is INSTALL, depends on Elias's answer as well as H1-H3. The generated verdict line still credits the rule to H1-H3 alone, so a reader of US_VETO_DRAW.md cannot see that S2 was read in the typical world. The header line was amended to mention s833, but the verdict template was not.

**Evidence.** Block line 1300 (the INSTALL verdict) against line 735 (the amended header) and lines 1275-1281 (S2 now "read as Elias answered on 2026-10-16").

**Fix proposed.** Change the template to "By R-US17's rule (c) as ruled (H1-H3; S2's reading, s833), the draw installs:" or similar, and make the STOPPED template match. This is a block edit, so do it in the same amendment and recompute the digest.

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: This is not a defect. The template "By R-US17's rule (c) as ruled (H1-H3)" (USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1300) names the rulings that chose rule (c). It does not claim the block is unamended, and it is still true after s833. The block's own header does the same thing. Line 732 keeps "Elias's rulings H1-H3" as the rule's provenance, and the amendment adds its record separately at line 735 ("amended at section 833 (Elias's answer of 2026-10-16 on S2's reading, before any run)"). The phrase "amend the rule as ruled" in THE STOPS' OPTIONS (line 1315) uses "as ruled" in the same sense. The amendment is also not hidden from a reader of US_VETO_DRAW.md: - (1) The document is stamped with the block's digest and the ruling-record state (lines 1187-1188). That digest is the amended 24de40a7..., and the plan's digests line and the RuleDigests list record it as AMENDED for §833 (lines 1487-1490 and 1575). - (2) The verdict line's readings clause, "<the same under every declared reading | the outcome differs under <readings>: <outcomes>>" (lines 1309-1310, with 1296-1297), names R13 wherever the result differs. R13 now carries the words' own reading, E_XXII[vetoable sent] = 0 exactly. So whenever the typical-world threshold is what decides INSTALL against S2, the verdict line already says so. - (3) DECLARED READINGS (line 1261) prints R13's outcome and P(NONE_XXII) on every run. The suggested change would only make the verdict line more explicit, and it would widen a minimal amendment beyond its one answered question. Since I am uncertain at most, this counts as refuted.

### F5 (low; upheld 0 of 1)

**Quote.**   - planted runs proving the UNFITTED, S3, S2 (both cases) and S1 paths (s830's form).

**Defect.** A planted "never sends" case with P(NONE_XXII) = 1 passes this bar under both the old reading and the new one, so it does not prove the amended threshold. Nothing requires a planted run inside the band the amendment changed, or at the >= boundary.

**Evidence.** Block line 1555 (THE MUTATION SUITE) against the amended case at lines 1280-1281 (2 * P(NONE_XXII) >= 1).

**Fix proposed.** Name the planted S2 "never sends" runs: one at P(NONE_XXII) = 1/2 exactly, which fires, and one just below 1/2 with V_XXII non-empty, which stays silent. For example: "S2 (both cases; the never-sends case at P(NONE_XXII) = 1/2 and just below it)".

**The skeptics:**

- REFUTED: This is not a defect of the amendment, and the suite line does not contradict it. G:\UNITY\Projects\PoliSim\docs\specs\USA_STAGE_PLAN.md:1555 reads "planted runs proving the UNFITTED, S3, S2 (both cases) and S1 paths (s830's form)". Both of S2's cases are still at 1278-1281: the empty V_XXII case, and "2 * P(NONE_XXII) >= 1". So "both cases" still names exactly what the stop has. "s830's form" means a planted run that drives a path and shows the result. COMPLETED.md:38420, the h4plant run, shows that form: it proves a path is reached and handled. It does not prove a threshold. The suite holds every stop to that same level. S1's comparison, "166 * E[hits] < 45 * E[named]" (plan 1284-1285), has no planted run at its boundary either. Before the amendment, S2 "= 0 exactly / P(NONE_XXII) = 1" had no "just below 1" run required. A planted run at P = 1 would also have passed an implementation that wrongly used >= 1/2. So the gap the finding describes, a test that cannot tell one threshold from another, was there before s833. The change to "P(NONE_XXII) = 1/2" did not create it. The diff (amend833.diff) touches only the header, the S2 wording, R13 and QUESTIONS FOR ELIAS. The review's own criterion, "nothing else changed", argues against widening the suite inside this amendment. What the finding asks for is extra test strength: boundary runs that no part of the block requires for any stop. It is not an inconsistency with what Elias answered. With that uncertainty, the finding counts as refuted.
